Aerial perspective was built, reviewed and removed as a device for oblique views, a deliberate deviation recorded in `docs/TODO.md`. The single lamp is kept bit-for-bit as a cartography mark, default off. The GPU raster over 15 views by 11 styles has a 99.9th percentile of 0 and a worst channel of 1 of 255 with 0.0007% of pixels differing, bars unchanged; the world fingerprint is identical, the save format untouched, and pen and ink pixel-identical. Raster timings at 2048 and 4096 on seed 42 on an RTX 3070 Ti: on the processor 166/669 ms before, 509-580 ms and 2.5-3.4 s under the sky, which takes 24 samples a land pixel, and 240-271 ms and 0.8-1.7 s under the lamp; on the device 102/323 ms before and 153-180 ms and 660-810 ms after. The browser pays the processor cost in full, having no WGSL raster, which is recorded with a separable-sweep cut as the remedy. The render review of the second pass on seed 718106 at 2048: the basin is smooth with three or four faint lines, the steppe straw and olive with green valleys, and the range has its bite back; the first pass had been returned for contour nests on flat basins, steppe drawn as sand, and a flat pale sheet from the aerial veil plus a sky 10.9% flatter than the lamp. |
| F14 Generalisation, graticule and scale | 2026-09-13, for 2.0.3 | 15c8364 (merged into release/2.0) | One parameter, the map sheet: how many pixels of what the reader sees one cell covers, 1.0 for exports and the live view quantised to half-octave bands, so a change of zoom band redraws the ink and not the ground. Rivers are kept by Topfer and Pillewizer's radical law with the ratio equal to pixels per cell: 198 of 279 at fit on seed 718106 at 2048 and 223 of 315 on 59758, all of them at 4x, against a control with no sheet that keeps 157 at every zoom. The shoreline is traced by marching squares off the land mask, with checkerboards resolved so land stays 8-connected as the flow routing assumes, and simplified by Douglas-Peucker at half a drawn pixel, 1.0 cell at fit and 0.25 at 4x: strays measure 1.0 cell against decimation's 2.5 at the same vertex count, with 19,634 vertices at fit against 51,749 at 4x. The trace costs 25 ms at 2048 against the raster's 539, the whole overlay 31 ms, so no GPU path was cut. The graticule is at 10 degrees exact in cells, with the prime meridian and the equator on the middle, and the margin figures are stroked polylines of fifteen glyphs rather than a font, so an export carries them in the browser too, sized so the widest figure takes two thirds of the gap: a 10.9 px cap height at 2048, figured every 10 degrees at 2048, 20 at 1024 and 30 at 512, behind a cartography toggle. The scale bar comes from the world's width in kilometres over the grid, through the kilometres-per-cell figure the heightmap sidecar and the square-kilometres-per-cell figure now share, on the 1-2-5 series at no more than a quarter of the frame after Robinson and Brewer: 2000 km is 341 px, 16.7% of a 2048 sheet at 5.9 km per pixel, drawn in the legend and on exports, while the cartouche gives kilometres per pixel and a representative fraction to two significant figures at the equator, against a CSS reference pixel. The coast pen is 0.05% of the width floored at 1 px and the graticule pen is the river hairline at 34% ink. The render review of the printed corner, the top-right figures, the full sheet, a 4x crop and the phone capture: the grid sits under the isobaths without competing, the coast is a crisp continuous line at every zoom, and the bar and figures read as engraved margin work. | F17 Not every coast is a ria | 2026-09-13, for 2.0.3 | f168b52, 2172324 | Diagnosis: the coastline is decided by the sea-level stage alone. Cutting the same eroded field seven ways shows the lowstand adds 38% to the box count at one cell, 34% at two and 7% at sixteen, while the shelf remap adds none and the plate noise under 1%. First, littoral grading: on the lowest 31% of the shoreline, Luijendijk's (2018) sandy share taken directly because the two derivable heights bracket it at 1.9% and 59% and the gap is lithology, sediment fills re-entrants over a 23 km reach scaled by the root of the fetch, from barrier-inlet spacing and the SMB relation, filling only and never enclosing water, under a simple-point test. The graded shoreline measures 0.284 and costs 74-78 ms at 2048. Second, drowned valleys: a cell of the ocean keeps its water only where the valley behind it is at least half a cell wide, the width from Leopold and Maddock's root of the catchment at 0.08 km per root square kilometre, calibrated against the Chesapeake, Delaware, Severn, Thames and Gironde, which lands on about 39 cells of catchment at every grid without being told. It touches only the ocean's shoreline, only within the postglacial rise, never above the ground beside it, always falling seaward and never enclosing; three of those four bounds were written after a guard caught their absence. It costs 536 ms at 2048, being a priority flood and an ocean labelling, and stays on the processor. The instrument was replaced: a boundary box count saturates at one box per position and read the finest octave smoother than the next, so the guard uses Richardson's method, coarsening the mask by majority at each ruler, which reads a plain disc at 1.006. Excess roughness at the cell, the fine dimension minus the coarse, went from 0.322 to 0.198 against Earth's 0, with a bar of 0.243 stated as the part that is not channels plus the seed spread. The coarse dimension went from 1.260 to 1.255 by ruler and 1.207 to 1.167 by box count, both inside 1.25 plus or minus 0.15. Smooth coast went from 0.086 to 0.188 at 750 km and 0.141 to 0.224 at 375 km against Earth's 0.31. The shoreline is 8-10% shorter and land gains 0.2 to 0.4% of the map, with no body of land or water gained or lost. Three guards were restated with reasons: the sea-level history indentation is reported rather than asserted, its estuary count kept at 34 against 22 over a 1.5x bar; the deposition land pin is taken at 128; and the ice-lake clause is cross-multiplied. Fingerprints were re-taken. The render review on seed 298405 at 1024 and 718106 at 2048 at 4x: the low northern and eastern shores read as graded arcs and bays, the mountain coasts keep their outline, and a tooth remains on many cells elsewhere. The residue is not channels, since filling every notch reaches the same 0.199, but the percentile cut through erosion's own cell-scale texture, which measures 0.288 even with the lowstand off; it was accepted as an improvement with the residue recorded as F21. The desert bands and the equatorial stripe are unrelated. |

| F30 The ruled lake shores in seed 364673's belt | 2026-09-14 | 0efaba3, 32d53c9, 21fdcb3 on chunk/f30-ruled-lake-shores, merge 83116dc | No geometry changed. The two bodies in the reviewed window are not lakes: they are inland seas, drowned rift segments too big for the enclosed-sea limit to call lakes, and what is ruled about them is not a shore but a canal of open water one cell wide that the drowned-basin drain cut to join each to the ocean, 73 cells on the 7,297-cell body at (2022,1449) and 41 on the 3,314-cell body at (1799,1761), of which 72 and 41 stand above the waterline in the raw eroded height, which is what says the outlet pass put them there and the ground did not. The river running dead straight north to south at x 1788 is the second canal. Every lake proper on that world is inside the bar at 0.60 times and under. Elimination, from the water back, worst shore run as a multiple of the derived bar: the shipped world 1.75x; with glaciation off 1.75x, not one figure different, reproducing I2's finding exactly; with the lake water balance off 1.75x, identical; with the outlet incision off 0.81x; with the post-cut outlet off 0.59x, which is the switch; and with rift segmentation off, the rift depth at 0 or the crust-pair profiles off, it goes entirely, because each leaves no inland sea on the map at all, the belt being a chain of half-grabens that without the segmentation is one trough which never subsides below the cut. The boundary's trace already wanders, being a jump-flood distance rather than a Voronoi edge, and the trough is not what is ruled. The cause, in two parts. The 41-cell canal: the depression fill raises each cell of a flat one step above the cell the priority flood reached it from, so the routing surface inside a filled basin is the flood's expansion order, its contours are the grid's metric exactly, the descent points at a neighbour rather than between two, and the randomised receiver has nothing to draw against; F18's fix cannot reach it, because it is not smooth ground behaving like a grid but a surface the fill invented. Measured directly: along the reach at (1864,1702-1719) the filled elevation descends by exactly 1e-6 a cell for eighteen cells while the ground under it varies by 2 m either way. Everywhere else that ruled path cuts a trench with a river in it; this one pass cuts below the waterline, so the map grows a canal. The 73-cell canal is not a ruled path at all: the first pass of that notch takes a sill standing about a kilometre above the waterline down to the basin's own floor in one bite, 1,652 cells cut on pass 0 with the whole reach left at one level falling only by the notch's own gradient, because the breach's relative drop is capped by the basin's whole depth and F22's fall-to-the-water rule hands a drowned sill the entire drop to the water as its slope. A slot cut in one bite lies at one level however it bends. Both repairs were built, measured and reverted, and that is what ships. Routing every filled flat by the bed, taking of the neighbours the staircase puts below it the two steepest ways down the true ground and drawing in proportion to their steepness, takes both canals away, 1.75x to 0.46x, and moves three Earth-derived guards with it: seed 59758 at 1024 keeps a lake of 1.83 times the Caspian's share of its land against the resolution guard's 1.4, the drawn river pen goes to 2.46 px against the nib's 1.23, and the outlet incision control loses the separation it exists to show. Narrowing it to the drowned-basin drain alone costs one guard instead of three and fixes one canal instead of two: the 41-cell one goes, seed 1234 keeps 189 cells of water the ocean cannot reach against the rule that every pocket no larger than the Caspian is gone, and the 73-cell one does not move by a single cell, which is the evidence that it is the bite and not the bearing. Then S2b landed and took both canals away: its repair lets the sea seed the depression flood at its own level, the same flood this defect was traced to, so the drowned basins the outlet pass notches are not the basins it used to find. On the merged tree seed 364673 at 2048 offers sixteen measurable bodies instead of ten, neither inland sea is over the bar, and the reviewed window reads clean, with no canal, no ruled shore and no dead-straight line at x 1788. The worst shore anywhere on the six worlds is now 1.07 times the bar, a 296-cell lake at (1075,1998) with a 20-cell run against 18.7 allowed, a different body with a different story, marginal where the old two were not, and undiagnosed; it is recorded in `docs/TODO.md`. Shipped: the outline-run instrument moved out of the glacial basin guard whole so the two cases cannot fork it, with the bar's derivation, Tanganyika's graben scarp at 2.05 times a circle of its own area, times three, and a new smallest body the bar binds of 255 cells derived beside it, 81 pi, where the bar stops being wider than the body itself and below which a body can only break it by being long and thin. Two corrections came out of the move: the map's polar rows are no longer counted as an outline, seed 364673 having measured 254 cells of exactly that before the rule was written down, with no cut basin touching a pole so I2's figures are untouched; and a run now says where it is. And a straightness report over every body of standing water but the world ocean, lakes by identifier and inland seas off the mask, on seed 364673 at 2048, 298405 at 1024 and the four standard seeds at 512, printing the census and naming what is over the bar, asserting only that it still has something to measure so the report cannot go quietly empty. On the merged tree the five other worlds carry two to six measurable bodies apiece and every one is inside the bar at 0.26 to 0.93 times. All three causes are recorded with their figures, and the chunk that diagnoses the body still over the bar turns the report back into the assertion it was written as. The glacial basin shape guard reads the moved instrument and is green on the merged tree, its worst valley basin 0.37 times the bar and its level surfaces 152 cells against 616 allowed on seed 364673. |
| F31 Bug reports: a Help item that carries the seed, and a line on the site | 2026-09-15, for 3.0.1 | 12402c0 on fix/f31-f32-reports-and-roadmap, copy pass in ef155c4 and 99b12ca | Three issue forms and a Help item that reads the world off the window. The bug form asks for the version, platform, seed, generation resolution, ocean coverage, the settings moved from their defaults, graphics acceleration and the device, what was expected, what happened and a screenshot; the feature form asks what, why and which style or view; the configuration turns blank issues off and offers the two addresses. Report a bug is a third Help command beside the update check and About, so the menu strip and the phone's folded menu gain it together, both drawing the same declaration. It builds the report from the world on screen, puts the whole of it on the clipboard, then opens the form with the same facts pre-filled by field id. The URL limit is 8,000 characters, chosen as the smallest limit in the chain rather than the browser's, whose own is about 2 MB; it is proxies and corporate gateways that cut a request line at 8 KB, where the half that arrives is a 414 or a form with half a field in it. Only the settings list can grow without bound, so that is what gives way, one entry at a time from the end, and what is left says how many went and that the whole list is on the clipboard; the worst case measurable, every knob moved and the longest name the header takes, is 801 characters. Two seams were added to the platform beside the link opener and shaped like it: a clipboard (AWT on the desktop, the browser clipboard API with a fallback, each with its own capability flag so the dialog never claims a copy that did not happen) and the host's own name for itself, which is the one fact a report needs that the world cannot supply. The dialog says which of the two routes worked and prints the report address for a reader with no account, and the URL itself where the host cannot open a link. Guards: the menu guard asserts Help's three items in order; the bug-report guard builds a report for seed 718106 at 1024 with three knobs moved and reads back the seed, the grid, the version and each moved setting with its value, checks that the URL parses back to the bug form and the seed, that the worst case is under the limit, and that an overlong list is trimmed with the seed intact. |

| F35 The same seed makes the same world at every resolution | 2026-09-19 | 253ea1b on chunk/f35-resolution-invariant-seeds | A plate seed's place is now drawn on a reference grid and read as a fraction of whatever grid is generated, so the 512 world is the world at every size. The cause was confirmed before the change, on seeds 217862 and 718106 at 512, 1024 and 2048: the standard bounded integer draw takes the generator's top bits for a power-of-two bound and falls back to a modulo otherwise, so the column, whose bound is the width, agreed to within 0.75 cells of the 512 grid at every size, while the row, whose bound is the height less the pole margins and so never a power of two, disagreed by up to 0.737 of the grid, 377 cells of 512, putting every plate at a different latitude and the whole partition with it. The two draws are now made once against a reference of 512 cells, which is the generator's own default and what the resolution scaling refines from, and the cell each names is placed by its middle as a fraction of the working grid; the draws, their order and their count are unchanged, so the 512 world is bit-identical to what it was and every other grid is that world drawn finer. After the change a seed sits 0.50 cells of the coarse grid from itself at 1024 and at 2048 on all four standard seeds. The audit found no other draw to convert: these two were the only bounded integer draws in the generator whose bound came from the width, the height or a count of cells; the name forge's syllables, realm forms and name salts are counts of things, the noise permutation is a fixed 256 entries, and the volcano offsets and the hearth, realm, landmark and schism jitters are already float draws, while the crust shuffle picks among plates. Reported and not changed: the landmark stage draws one jitter per land cell from a single stream, so which cell gets which jitter depends on how many cells there are, which is resolution-dependent by construction and a decoration on a score rather than a bound. Guards, each shown failing on the old draw and passing after: the plate seeds' fractional positions agree at 512, 1024 and 2048 to within one cell of the coarsest, 290-404 cells before and 0.50 after; the plate-id field at 1024, read at the fine cell nearest each coarse one, matches the 512 field everywhere further than one coarse cell from a boundary, 0.139-0.257 of the interior before and 0.99998-1.00000 after against a bar of 0.999 derived beside it, since a seed may sit a cell from where the other grid puts it, so a bisector may, and the domain warp carries that cell into the map, with the deepest disagreement measuring 1.41 coarse cells from a boundary; and the land mask after sea level read the same way on every cell the shore does not touch, that share computed from the 512 coast rather than chosen, 0.445-0.727 before against bars of 0.952-0.962 and 0.981-0.984 after. One re-pin, because 512 did not move: the deposition starting-point land at 128, from 6279 to 6273, the sixteenth time that figure has moved. The save format is unchanged, no field changed and the codec fixture is untouched. Renders before and after: before, seed 718106's 1024 and 2048 are plainly two different planets; after, both seeds read as one world at two sizes, the same snowy range and south-facing bays on 718106 and the same triangular northern continent, gulf and ring-lagoon archipelago on 59758, with nothing reading worse and the finer grid only resolving more fjords and lakes. Two Earth bars the new worlds do not meet are reported rather than moved, and they are the same finding twice: the worlds at grids other than 512 are now different worlds, and two Earth-derived bars the old scrambled worlds happened to meet are not met by the true ones. Each clause keeps asserting every case that passes today and prints the one that does not with Earth's figure beside it, the finding asserted non-empty so it cannot go quiet. The glacial basin floor clause: seed 718106 at 1024 carries a 167-cell cut basin at (488,25) whose floor is 78.4% within a metre of one height over 51 m of relief, against the 54.9% Salar de Uyuni allows, which is a flat-floored basin of I2's class on ground the guard had never looked at. The outlet resolution pooled clause: seed 59758 at 2048 reads 1.84 times the Caspian's share of Earth's land, 0.4577% of its land against Earth's 0.2491%, and is left out of the pool, which then reads 0.98 times over five worlds where all six read 1.12. Both are recorded with their figures and with what a diagnosis should notice first: the 512 figures did not move at all, seed 59758 at 1.55x and seed 42 at 0.67x, so whatever these are, they are something the finer grids resolve. |
| F36 A Linux download, a signed apt repository and installation instructions | 2026-09-19 | on chunk/f36-linux | A release cut from a Windows machine could not build a Debian package, so the Linux half of a release is `.github/workflows/release-linux.yml`: ubuntu-latest, JDK 21, `fakeroot`, `:desktop:test`, `createDistributable` and `packageDeb`, then the packaged launcher's `--gpu-check` on a runner with no graphics card. That check needed no repair, and the reason is worth keeping: `GlContext.ensure` catches the driver failure through `Future.get`'s `ExecutionException` rather than only `Exception`, and the GL worker is a daemon thread, so finding no device already prints a reason and exits 0; the step reads the exit code back and explains a non-zero one instead of assuming what it means. It then names `Cartogenesis-<version>-linux-amd64.deb` and `Cartogenesis-<version>-linux-portable.tar.gz` from `gradle.properties`, warning rather than failing when the tag disagrees with the property, waits up to thirty minutes for the release to exist because it is cut by hand and may appear minutes after the tag, and uploads both with `gh release upload --clobber`. The apt repository at `cartogenesis.com/apt` is rebuilt from every release's `.deb` on each deploy rather than committed back to `main`. Both would make the pool accumulate; this way generated files stay out of git, which matters when a `.deb` is about 100 MB and a committed pool would grow the repository by that much per release for ever, the releases become the one source of truth for what the repository offers, and no workflow pushes to `main` where it could collide with a person on release day. It costs a few minutes of downloading per deploy. Two placement decisions came out of building it. The rebuild runs in `site.yml` and not in the release workflow, because a Cloudflare Pages Direct Upload publishes a whole tree: a site deploy that did not carry `apt/` — a copy change, a hand-run from the Actions tab — would take the repository off the domain until the next release, so building it in the deploy is what makes every deployment carry a complete repository whatever triggered it. `release-linux.yml`'s second job therefore only asks for a deploy once the `.deb` is on the release, and the site workflow's serial, uncancelled concurrency group puts that run after the one the tag push started. And it writes into `web/build/site/apt` rather than into `site/`, because every `:desktop:` test task declares the whole of `site/` as an input and would otherwise hash the pool on every run. `site/apt/conf/distributions` is the only committed part, carrying `Limit: -1` — reprepro keeps one version per package by default, which on a from-scratch rebuild would leave the pool holding only the newest release — and a `__APT_SIGNING_KEY_ID__` the deploy substitutes from a secret. reprepro signs through `gpg-agent` in its own process, where nothing can hand it a passphrase, so the deploy allows loopback pinentry and makes one throwaway signature to prime the cache. A missing secret is an `::error::` naming it and a site that deploys without `/apt`, never a failed release. `_headers` caches `/apt/pool/*` for a year and `/apt/dists/*` not at all: a package's URL carries its version, while a cached index is a reader who cannot see the release just published. `site/downloads.txt` is the one list of release file names, with `<version>` where the number goes — beside the page rather than in `desktop/build.gradle.kts` because it is data about a release and not a build rule, and excluded from the published tree along with `apt/conf/**`. The names were read off the release page rather than guessed: the installer is `Cartogenesis-<version>.msi`, not `-windows.msi`. `SiteAssemblyTest` gains three cases that hold the page's new Download and Installation section, `docs/INSTALL.md` and `docs/RELEASE_NOTES_TEMPLATE.md` to that list in both directions, check the apt source line matches word for word across all three and ends in the codename `conf/distributions` actually builds, and read the hero button's label for both platform names — it read "Download for Windows (recommended)" and would have survived a Linux download perfectly happily. The page gains `id="install"`, "Download and Installation", after Notes and before the roadmap: four panels in the Notes grid, Windows, Linux with the three commands in a new `.cmd` block, Browser, and the release page. No new palette variable — `bone` on `ink-sunk` for the command block and `bone` on `ink-raised` for a file name in a sentence, both added to `SitePaletteContrastTest` and measuring 11.85:1 and 10.44:1. The hero button now reads "Download the Desktop App (Windows/Linux)" and points at the section; the Notes card's Windows tag becomes Windows · Linux and its SmartScreen sentences move into the section rather than being kept true in two places. `docs/INSTALL.md` carries the long form per platform, where saves live (`~/.cartogenesis/worlds`, and `/opt/cartogenesis/bin/Cartogenesis` with no `/usr/bin` symlink), the note that the open-source stack was tested and the proprietary driver was not, and a numbered walkthrough of generating the signing key and adding the three secrets. The README's building section gains a prerequisites table, one row per platform, and stops saying no Linux download is published. Gates: `:desktop:test` 75 green, `:desktop:siteTest` 21 green against 18 before, both workflow files parse. The release workflow itself was not run. |
| F15 Rivers at 1024: the pen, the mouths, lake outlets and the longest watercourse | 2026-09-12, for 2.0.3 | 00fce71 (merged into release/2.0) | The pen is 0.24% of the map's width, floored at the 0.8 px hairline, giving 1.23/2.46/4.92/9.83 px at 512/1024/2048/4096; the Amazon's 10 km mouth on a 12,000 km world is 0.083% and a printed map exaggerates it about threefold, where F10's 5 px at 2048 was 0.244%. Mouths: the course's last vertex was the sea cell and the renderer's round cap added half a stroke, so ink reached 3.0-3.2 px past the shore on five seeds, 133-284 sea pixels of it with 3-16 having no land neighbour; the course now ends half a stroke short of the shoreline, taking 401 mouths over water to 0 and offshore river pixels to 0. The thin squiggle was not an outlet with no flow, outlets carrying 1.0-1.7x their lake's largest inflow as measured: it was a lake filled to its spill along the trunk of the map's biggest system, one cell wide for 61 cells, which the tracer stopped at and the renderer would not draw through, so the trunk crossed as a dotted ribbon. A cell of standing water now counts as open water only inside a 2x2 block of its own lake, since a cell is 6-23 km and the Amazon's mouth is 10: 237 channel cells under one-cell water went from 0 drawn to 170, with no breaks and no gaps. Each drawn river is now its own longest watercourse, with heads ranked by the length of the path below them: coverage went from 0.780 pooled, a range of 0.696-0.845, to 1.000 on seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99. Before the fix, heads were ranked by the flow at them, so a drawn river ran from the biggest headwater and the longest watercourse in the same catchment was drawn as a tributary stopping at the junction, with drawn courses covering 0.484 of their watercourses at 512 and 0.408 at 2048 where 1.0 is the definition; the union of drawn cells is the same network either way, but the labels, the lengths and what the width rule calls a trunk all depend on it. The diagonal rectangle is a lake: 53 cells within 1.2 cells of one straight line in a straight-walled trench, present with the breach, the deposition and the post-cut outlet each switched off and gone only with erosion off, so it is stream-power incision along a reach D8 runs dead straight over an apron smooth at the cell scale; the census of straight bars 20 cells and longer reads 1/0/0/1/2 over seed 298405 at 1024 and seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99 at 512, and the cure was left to F18 because it lives in the flow routing and moves every world. No terrain, flow or lake changed, so no fingerprint moved. The lake-crossing guard counts open water, and the JPEG bound moved from 60 to 68 because a 1.2 px river is nearly all edge, WebP and JPEG both having moved by eight with the relation guard untouched. The render review of seed 298405 at 1024 and 718106 at 2048 before and after: the mouth no longer bulges into the bay, the trunk reads at one weight through its lake, and the network is graded rather than roped, with 2048 unchanged to the eye. Found: at 512 the nib spans only 1.5x, a full pen of 1.23 px over a 0.8 px hairline, so the web and phone default shows little hierarchy; and broad, smooth, straight-edged pale bands flank the ranges at 1024, terraced in steps, not diagnosed. |

A past continental rift becomes an aulacogen, its trough 55% filled and its shoulders 35%. The present epoch runs last with every factor at 1, so zero or one epoch reproduces the old field bit for bit, with pre-chunk checksums pinned on seeds 7, 42 and 1234. A crust-age field is saved as a new section, 34 in all. Old belts beyond 52 cells of any present boundary measure +0.080/+0.141/+0.096 against a bar of 0.04, pooled 2.19x lower and 1.50x broader than present belts, against bars of 1.8 and 1.3. The crust-age bands run about 37% present, 25% one epoch back, 20% two back and 18% cratonic, and a single epoch gives a zero difference field. Tectonics at 2048 went from 1.37 to 3.67 seconds; the per-cell work is the minority, so no GPU path was cut, which the audit guard measures. Reported, not asserted: desert by band, which is asserted in the geography audit, and the realm rank-size slope at -1.85 and -2.01 against Zipf's -1. Every bar was shown to bite: Earth's own band table passes the bimodality clause at 0.174 and 0.848 while a featureless ramp and a flat world fail it; a rectangle of land reads 1.024; a single-order comb reads Hack at 1.000 and has no pair to bifurcate; forty lakes of one size and forty islands laid out at a Korcak exponent of 1.200 both fail their bars, and the fit recovers the 1.200 to three decimals. Two defects were raised: the two metre scales, where the altitude ceiling says the highest land is 6 km while the lowstand and the shelf break derive theirs from 8 km and the sea has no depth setting at all; and the drawn-course decomposition above. |
| S1 Units and time | 2026-09-13 | c92fd39, bda0f4c, baf3132 | A world scale on the generator's configuration: a world 12,000 km wide; highest land 6,000 m as a cell mean, since Tibet's interior averages 5,023 m (Fielding 1994) and a 512 cell is 23 by 12 km while Everest is a point; deepest ocean 10,000 m, the Challenger Deep less a cell's share of its walls, since a trench is a line; and 336,476 years per hydraulic round, derived by fixing the erodibility at Whipple and Tucker's 1e-6 and asking what step removes what a round removed, so twelve rounds are 4.0 million years. Cell width, height and area are computed in one place. The lowstand and the shelf break had been shares of the land's relief above the shoreline, a measured number that is 0.25 of the field on seed 718106 and 0.59 on seed 7, so the last glacial maximum was 45 m on one world and 141 m on another; it is 120 m everywhere now, 0.0075 of the field against 0.0088 averaged, and the shelf break 130 m at 0.0081. Every physical knob is converted once where its stage reads it, exact at 512: a pond at 24 m, delta freeboard 48 m, valley relief 2,100 m, moraine 270 m, shelf depth 1,000 m, fjord 2,000 m; reaches of 141 km for a delta, 1,500 km for an outlet, 1,875 km for debris travel and 469 km for the shelf; a critical slope of 12 m/km, which is 0.688 degrees and the old figure at 512; and areas of 52,560 km2 for the inland-sea cap and 3,296 km2 for the lake minimum. The scale-free suite, at 512 and 1024 per merge and adding 2048 in the audit tier, asserts relief at 1.00-1.08x against a bar of 1.10 and drainage density at 0.99-1.18x against 1.35, green on four seeds; the findings printed and ranked are the largest lake at 0.63-6.17x, the ice share at 0.26-1.64x, the coastline at 0.82-1.49x, and the desert bands moving by +0.10 to +0.47 of a band's land between 512 and 2048, seed 7's mid-latitudes going from 16% to 63% desert. A biome share cannot move with the grid; the moisture march does, which is the most valuable thing this chunk found. Two instrument bugs were fixed and stated: the channel support area is in square kilometres, and a cell-edge coastline is a staircase. Renders of seeds 718106 and 59758 at 2048 before and after, whole and three crops each: 0.8-4.6% of pixels moved by more than 8 levels, and the land reads the same to the eye. Found and not fixed: the Caspian and Superior caps are shares of Earth carried onto a world a seventh its size; the maximum river count is a count, so the drawn network thins on a finer grid; the shelf plateau is at 1,000 m against Earth's 130; a glacial trough is 150 km wide; and the in-round notch enlarges drowned basins across the lowstand shelf. |

Earlier passes are recorded for their measurements: the first made the histogram bimodal by cutting the base noise from 7,300 to 2,000 m peak to peak, which left 7% modulation at the belt's own scale, drew the deep ocean as flat plate polygons and active belts as smooth pale stamps, and moved the coastline from 1.202 to 1.136, islands from 62 to 15, ice from 9.1% to 3.1% and lakes from 0.54% to 3.33% of land, all the wrong way. The third pass established that isostasy and uplift are long-wavelength physics that must sit under the existing texture rather than replace it, and that the three remaining defects were never the isostasy: the regional relief share moved from 0.10 to 0.16, because a continent with no map-scale slope ponds and drowns into an archipelago; the ice load had been read as a bed rather than a surface, worth 1.8 points of ice share and a point of lakes once the bend was tapered by the ice's own thickness profile; and the critical fall moved from 12 to 60 m/km, the Andes' western flank over a cell, because the honest unit conversion had let the thermal sweeps plane a belt's rim into a pale ring. | S2b The review's four findings | 2026-09-14 | 9eacd7a, 5ec5389, d04226a, 518c75c, f9b91c9, 48829f2, d1e9f04 (merge with I2 at aed1ae2) | An independent read-only correctness review of the S2 merge found four faults, each confirmed in the code before dispatch, and all four are fixed by cause with a guard shown failing first. First, the flexure wrapped north to south: the 2-D transform is periodic on both axes and the world is a cylinder, so a 1,000 m rock stripe on row 0 at 512 bent row 511 by the same 72.58 m it bent row 1, a share of 1.000, and both poles carry ice. The load is now mirrored about each polar row out to twice the map's height and cropped after, so x stays periodic while y's period becomes a whole meridian out and back. Mirroring rather than zero-padding is right because this world is 12,000 km round and 6,000 pole to pole, so a meridian is a closed loop and what lies past the north pole is the world coming back down the far side; the reflection is that continuation for a zonal load, a polar cap being one, gives the pole the zero slope it must have, and leaves the map's own mean under the dropped zero-frequency term. The new clause reads the far pole against the middle of the map at 1.000 before and 0.000 after, against a bar of 1e-6, which is the arithmetic's floor since the physics' answer over 89 flexural parameters is the exponential of -89. The near row nearly doubles, 72.58 to 140.06 m, which is the continuation working: a cap sitting on the pole carries on over it. The transform pair now runs on a grid twice as tall, 250 to 532 ms at 2048 and 962 to 2,850 ms at 4096, 1.02 to 1.58 and 2.00 to 5.63 of a round's thermal sweeps, with the buffers doubling to 134 MB at 2048 and 537 at 4096. Second, the depression fill left submerged land unfilled: the flood was seeded from land touching lower water, so a component of enclosed-water cells ringed by higher water was never seeded, never visited, kept its sinks and drained to nothing, and the fourth pass of S2 had excluded such cells from the seeds, which hid the symptom and left the cause. The sea now joins the flood at its own level, as a priority flood does, and only water touching land is pushed, since open water has nothing but visited neighbours whatever order it pops in. Measured: on the old seeding 64 of 89 land cells are sinks with no receiver at all and the below-water patch stays at -0.200000; now 0 and 0, and the patch comes up to -0.099999, its spill level plus the flat-gradient staircase. An ordinary coast does not move, the plateau's rim reading 0.050000 and its pit 0.050002 under both rules, and the two stranded-cell counts elsewhere go to 0. Third, the jump flood measured north-south distance with the cell's width: a cell is 23.4 km across and 11.7 down at 512, so a craton thickened to full over 200 km northward where the setting asks for 400, and a ridge's age-depth read the orientation of the ridge that made a piece of floor. The distance field now takes the cell height in cell widths, carried through every pass because a nearer source in cells can be the further one on the ground, and answers in cell widths; at a ratio of 1 it is the integer arithmetic it replaced, to the bit, so no other field moves, and the plate stage passes the grid's own ratio and consumes kilometres. The craton guard reads the interior share inside two synthetic crusts, one whose edge runs east-west and one north-south, 34 rows and 17 columns being the same ground to the metre: 0.8636 against 0.6307 before, a gap of 0.2329, and 0.6307 against 0.6307 after, a gap of 0.0000 against a bar of 1e-6; the brute-force case runs at both row scales and is exact at each. Four callers still count cells and are recorded with what each costs: the climate stage's water distance, the sea-level stage's distance to land, which makes a shelf half as wide off a northern coast as off a western one, the plate stage's two boundary-distance fields, which is a chunk of its own, and the glaciation stage's two. Fourth, the ground-texture control was computed and never asserted, and asserting it would not have worked: on this ground the reduced-relief control reads 0.84% of land in lakes against the clause's bar of 2.22%, passing by two and a half times, because the crust's own thickness profile now supplies the long slope the fine half was raised to supply. The clause is demoted to the two plain absolute checks it actually makes, renamed to stop claiming otherwise, and says in its own KDoc why; the control is deleted rather than printed, which saves five 512 worlds a run. Six generator guards moved with the ground, each dealt with by cause rather than by widening: the deposition land pin 6283 to 6279; the three tectonic-history checksums re-taken a fourth time, holding the same property that epochs 0 and 1 still agree bit for bit; the lake water-balance dry sample re-picked by the class's own scan from seed 6 to seed 13, seed 6's hollow having gone from 4,817 cells at 112 mm to 3,711 at 125 and keeping 0.48 of its spill area where it kept 0.26, while seed 13 is 2,450 cells at 96 mm and keeps 0.24, the bar unmoved at 0.45; the rift guard keeping seed 43 and withdrawing its sea-bodies bar, because no seed of the twelve scanned clears three bodies with a control that fails, while its other two bars still read 3 bridges and 0.15 of width variation against the control's 0 and 0.04; the outlet fill clause read pooled instead of per seed with the half unmoved, 0.360 pooled over 718106 0.777, 42 0.597, 7 0.380, 99 0.183, 1234 0.132 and 43 0.093; and its level-sill clause swapping which seed carries the claim, because this chunk's own fill now raises the land a level sill is made of. The ruled-bar census is split by the sea-level cut, the repaired fill reaching below-waterline basins for the first time, one of which on seed 7 holds 28 cells in a hollow 590 by 47 km, straight to 1.12 cells but standing at 0.272 of the field against a shoreline at 0.632 and absent entirely from the plain-routed world. Outside the generator, all twelve style records were re-taken, the ordinary-ground constant unmoved at 0.9582, and re-taken once more after the merge with I2, since neither side's figures describe the merged world; and the outlet resolution guard retired its 1.4 chaos allowance rather than widening it, seed 59758's largest land lake reading 1.55, 1.48 and 0.20 times the Caspian's share at 512, 1024 and 2048, an eight-fold spread on one world, so the clause is read pooled over its six worlds and asserts Earth's bare figure, at 0.79x it. The save format is unchanged, the gzip fixture being a stored save decoded rather than a world regenerated, so a moved ground does not stale it. Renders at 2048, whole and both poles for seeds 718106 and 59758: the same land and the same coasts, interiors a little smoother at the map scale as a craton tilting over twice the distance should be, seed 59758's eastern continent visibly better drained, and a few more and larger lakes with natural irregular outlines; the poles themselves barely move, because neither seed carries a polar ice cap at the default settings and the flexure repair needs a polar load to show. Nothing reads worse except seed 718106's polar endorheic lake, which lost its drawn inflow. |
| W1 Energy balance and sea ice | 2026-09-13 | 11fc0ce, fcabf90, 137f49f, c41ea21, 1c01dd9, merge 3af361b | The latitude curve was retired, with its equator and pole temperatures and its continentality, and a Budyko-Sellers-North energy balance put in its place, over 240 bands by 360 steps by 20 years, with three reservoirs per band: land air at 1.7e7 J/m2/C, marine air at 1.04e7, and a 50 m mixed layer at 2.0e8. The air and the water are coupled by a bulk surface flux of 25 W/m2/K, sensible 11.6 plus latent 13.4 at 8 m/s, the top of the bulk formulae's 15-25, and the two air columns trade at 8 W/m2/K and carry the transport. Outgoing radiation is A plus BT with B at 2.09 and A at 210.7 from Earth's own 240 at 14 C; the albedo is least-squares fitted to Earth's zonal planetary albedo with an ITCZ cloud belt and clear subtropics; and the diffusivity is a Hadley cosine-squared, 0.90 to 0.30, plus a storm-track Gaussian at 50 degrees (Trenberth and Stepaniak 2003). Koppen's gates read the warmest and coldest month while the half-year means are kept for the degree-day sum and the moisture march, a build with months everywhere having cost a third of the permanent ice, which is how the distinction was found; the marine blend's distance was corrected by half a cell. Against Earth, land and marine air at 0, 20, 40 and 60 degrees measure 26.2/26.0, 23.4/23.3, 13.9/13.9 and 1.5/1.8 against 26.0/26.5, 25.0/24.5, 14.5/14.5 and -2.0/2.0; the warmest month over the sea 26.7/26.0/19.2/7.9 against a reanalysis 27/27/19/7 and the water 26.3/24.8/16.8/4.9 against 27/27/19/5.5, all inside 3 degrees; swings at 50-60 degrees are 38.7 for land against 34-38, 13.0 for marine air against 8-11 and 6.9 for water against 5-8; transport is 4.6/4.9/3.2 PW against 5.3/5.0/3.3; and the model ice edge is 60.4 N against Earth's 60, with the map's cold-season pack covering 25-33% of the sea to 55-58 degrees. The glacial maximum is a dimmed sun, the poles cooling 5.8 degrees with the feedback and 4.3 without and the cap moving from 60.4 to 52.1 degrees, 53.6 without. Frozen sea yields 257-523 mm against 1811-2508 over open water, 4.1-7.2x, and permanent ice holds 8.7% of land against Earth's 10.1%. The equatorial stripe is cured. A6's claim is restored, with warm west-facing coasts at 50-60 degrees forested at 40/52/51% against the recorded 65/53/59, bars unmoved, seed 1234 clearing by half a point; the limiter is Koppen's -3 degree continental gate and a current anomaly of +1.3 to +1.8 where Earth's comparable coasts get +4 to +8. A new cold-biome guard against Olson and others (2001), tundra 6% and boreal 11% of ice-free land, asserts boreal at 6.7% pooled within a factor of three, where some seeds read 0.0% mid-chunk, and reports tundra at 47.4% without asserting it, since the same worlds sit within 1-2 C of the reanalysis at every latitude to 70 degrees, so the cause is the land standing 1,200-1,700 m up, which is S2's. Two glacial-lake clauses became findings: pooled over three seeds at 1024 the ice raises cold-country lake density only from 0.21 to 0.28 per 10,000 cells, because the glaciation stage proposes no trunk glaciers against a diffuse 41,000-cell sheet, with 0 trunks, 0 cirques and 0 moraines over 31,453 channelled cells and nothing refused, the candidate test asking a path to carry a share of the whole frozen area; that is I1's. Bars re-derived with reasons: the seasons guard from 9 to 11 and 2.0 to 1.5, the field being marine air now rather than sea surface temperature; the amplification clause restated on the polar cooling; the snow-balance control clause skipping a saturated seed; the river narrowing rate counted against the land; and the style hashes re-pinned together. The save format was bumped and the fixture regenerated. Tried and reverted, recorded: correcting the maritime influence's double attenuation makes the coasts worse, a ten-cell disc around a warm tongue being mostly not that tongue, so the fix needs W2's upwind neighbourhood. The band albedo was hoisted out of the step loop after a progress guard timed out on the web build, bit-identically. The render review of seed 718106's biome map before and after: the same continents read the same way, the equatorial stripe gone, the northern interior still tundra for the reason above; and the sea-ice edge is a dead-straight line of latitude across the whole ocean on both worlds, because the band model has no longitude and the currents' anomaly is a fifth of Earth's. |
| I2 Glacial basins take their shape from the ground | 2026-09-14 | 7c36804, ddb00e8, f4e6935, ba703f2 on chunk/i2-glacial-basins, merge a390612 | The slab was the trough, not the basin. Phase-by-phase instrumentation on seed 364673 at 2048 puts three cells of it at 1744, 1503 and 1556 m before the carving and 375 m after, a cut of 1,130 to 1,370 m where the trough is meant to take 24, and the basin cutter cut nothing on that world, 0 valley basins from 0 candidates, the 6 basins it does hold being the sheet's scour with none within 400 cells of the slab. The cause is the swath: the valley width is 152 km, fifty times a real trough and deliberately so, and the middle 65% of that half-width was planed to the height of the one cell on the axis whatever stood there, 300 km of cross-section at one height on a 2048 grid, with a straight edge at the flow's own D8 bearing and, where the two legs of a reach cross, a cross on the map; the cirque bowl did the same with a disc. The fix is the figure the model was missing, a valley ice thickness of 600 m, from Aletsch's 300-500 m over most of its length and 900 at its thickest by radio-echo sounding, with each cell cut by the product of how far across the section it lies and how deeply it is buried, so the valley floor is planed, the shoulder is barely touched and rock above the ice surface is not touched at all; a cell standing d above the bed keeps d squared over the thickness, so no floor is ever exactly level. The basin work was done as well and is an improvement on its own terms: the footprint is the valley floor, within the trough half-width by Euclidean distance and no higher than the lowest ground on the path plus the cut's depth; the area cap peels by height from the deepest cell outward, connected by construction with its outline a contour, instead of by four-connected ring; and the floor is a paraboloid in the Euclidean rim distance over a padded window instead of a saucer off a Manhattan inset that saturates two cells in. Three helpers went, and the sheet's scour shares all three and gains the bar refusal the valley basins already had, one seed at 1024 having been cutting a basin 17 cells long and one wide. Guards in a new basin-shape class, on seed 364673 at 2048 and three seeds at 1024. The one that fails on the earlier tree is the level surface, against Salar de Uyuni's 10,582 km2, the flattest large surface on Earth: seed 364673 at 2048 measures 16,050 km2, 935 cells, against 616 allowed, and 2,644 km2, 154 cells, after, in line with the 2,541-3,777 km2 the other three carry either way. The outline guard, no run along one grid bearing longer than three times what a circle of the same area makes on a grid, Earth's straightest lake shore being Tanganyika's graben scarp at 2.05x, and the floor guard, no more of a floor within a metre of one height than twice the larger of a paraboloid's even hypsometry and an equal-area disc's outermost ring, from Hutchinson's 0.6 to 1.2 volume development, both pass before as well as after, at 0.63x and 0.65x the outline bar and 0.67x and 0.81x the floor bar. That is the honest reading: with bars derived rather than fitted, the basins were not out of bounds on these seeds, only the troughs. Earlier drafts of both did fail beforehand, at 1.86x and many floors, and were wrong to, ignoring that a floor of n cells cannot put less than one nth at one height and that a bowl's outermost ring is two roots of pi over n of it by construction. Why the basin held no water, and it is not a playa: the slab at 375-376 m sits in a 7,515-cell depression that spills at 567.6 m and is endorheic on 157 mm of rain a year against a 21.1 C summer, the balance keeps 991 cells wet as one lake, and the slab stands above that surface as dry ground, which is what an endorheic basin's shelf is. The playa branch never fires, because it fires only when the balance cannot fill the minimum lake, and here it can; what was needed was a floor with relief in it. Renders: the slab and its cross are gone and the belt reads as a range with valleys; on seed 718106 the glaciated massif in the south-west loses four pale stamps and a comb of parallel bars along the range front; seed 59758 has no ice in frame and is unmoved, with 0.2-0.9% of pixels differing on the three. What is left and noted: a faint pale smear along the two legs of the reach, where the trough is still stamped one cell at a time along a D8 path. The twelve style records were re-pinned because the ground moved, pen and ink's among them, which is the evidence, since it has no tint for the shading to multiply, while the ordinary-ground constant did not need re-deriving. The save format was bumped for the new glaciation key and the fixture regenerated. Four follow-ups were opened: the sheet mask's edges are straight because the local relief is measured over a square sliding window, which is where the scour outlines' ruled edges come from and why that guard reports rather than asserts on them; the basin cutter cuts one basin a world or none, every candidate refused by the sinuosity minimum; the trough is still stamped along a D8 path; and the two lakes and the dead-straight river reported in the same window are not the ice at all, since zero cells of water differ and every flow target is the same with glaciation off, so that cause is upstream of this stage and still there. |
| I3 The ruled ice flank | 2026-09-21 | 00682e8, b1777a4, f5f4212, 134a086, ab59f86 on chunk/i3-ice-streaks, then ae9a158, 4e689e7, 18842dc, 349f626 on chunk/i3-finish | **Two causes, both in the ice and neither in the shading, and the renders are the evidence for both.** Reported as seed 878210 at 1024 in Atlas: the sheet's western flank, where it meets two lakes, striped column by column in light and dark, over a surface reading as flat facets with hard edges rather than as a dome. `I3RenderDump` writes the Atlas view whole and the flank at 3x, the same window with `glaciation.enabled` off through `generate(previous = world)` so only the ice recomputes, the ice field on its own as a greyscale and the relief shading's own illumination. The control settled the shading immediately: with the ice off, the same ground is an ordinary range with no stripe on it and no lake in it, so nothing in `ReliefShading` or the raster was touched at all. The greyscale then put the stripes in the *field*, as a comb of one-cell-wide fingers of ice hanging off the southern edge of the sheet, and a probe along one row found them alternating cell by cell in the **frozen mask** rather than in the profile. **The first cause is a mask thresholded at a numerical zero.** Ice was `snowBalance > 0f`, and over a polar desert nothing falls and nothing melts: at row 940 the balance reads -2.166e-4, -8.093e-4, 1.100e-3, 6.315e-4 and so on cell by cell, which is not weather but the residue of a cancellation. Both sides of that subtraction are sums of quantities of the order of a thousand millimetres and a float carries seven digits, so the residue is of the order of five ten-thousandths of a millimetre and its sign is whatever the last bits did. 12,652 of seed 878210's 54,698 frozen cells, 23%, stood on a balance under a hundredth of a millimetre a year. What the profile makes of a mask speckled that way is the defect: a one-cell filament attached to a sheet is one cell from its margin in both directions, the plastic profile gives it 337-446 m there, and a 400 m wall one cell wide shades as a hard black and white line. Column-aligned because the filaments run north-south. `SnowBalance.SMALLEST_MEANINGFUL_BALANCE_MM` is a hundredth of a millimetre a year, twenty times the largest residue cancellation can leave so rounding cannot cross it and two thousand times below the 21-23 mm a year measured at Vostok and Dome A, which is the driest accumulation on Earth that sustains a sheet, so it cannot take ground from any sheet the world is entitled to; `SnowBalance.isGlaciated` is the one predicate, read by the glaciation stage and by the biome classification alike, since the two disagreeing would colour ground the ice never carved. That alone took the one-cell walls from 535 east-west against 124 north-south, 4.3 times, to 109 against 105. **The second cause is the surface's datum, and it is what drew the facets.** The profile solves for a surface measured from the ice's own margin, and I1 read that margin's elevation off the *nearest* margin cell. A nearest-cell lookup is piecewise constant, its regions are Voronoi cells, and across every edge between two of them the datum steps by however much the ground at the two margin cells differs: measured on seed 878210, 40.9 m on the mean east-west pair of ice, which is the dome's own fall across the same cell, and 1,965 m at worst - at one pair the datum went 1,382 m to 3,261 and the thickness 198 m to 2,020. Where the margin runs east and west each column takes a margin cell of its own, and that is the ruling; elsewhere it is the facets. The fix is the yield condition's own answer. `|grad S| = tau0 / (rho g H)` with `S = z` on a margin of varying height is solved by the lower envelope of the profiles rising from every margin point, `S(x) = min over m of (z_m + k sqrt(d))`: differentiate one branch and `|grad S| = k^2 / 2H` falls out, which with `k^2 = 2 tau0 / (rho g)` is the condition itself, so this is its viscosity solution and not a smoothing. It is continuous, a minimum of continuous functions being continuous, and where two branches meet the surface creases instead of stepping, which is an ice divide. Over a level margin every branch shares one datum and it is exactly the answer it replaces. It is also strictly *lower*: the nearest-margin surface was not merely higher but inadmissible, standing above a profile rising from a margin it could see. `JumpFloodDistance.run` gained an optional cost, so the flood compares the height a source would put over a cell rather than the distance to it; the plain flood is untouched and stays exact by its own brute-force test. **Two things had to be got right about it and both were found by the guards.** Its sources are the *margin* - ice-free ground with ice against it - and not every ice-free cell, a distinction the plain flood never had to make because the nearest ice-free cell is on the edge whatever else is seeded; seeded with the whole ocean the envelope handed domes eight hundred kilometres of open water to start from and took seed 42's ice to nothing, seed 718106's flow near its dome to 49.2% outward at 86.7 degrees off radial, and the thickest ice on the reported world from 2,739 m to 1,730. And the flood now answers two questions rather than one: `Margin` carries the governing margin's distance, which is the profile's argument, *and* the plain nearest-margin distance, which is what a sheet's half-width means and what every guard asking how big a body of ice is reads. Conflating them failed the continental clause on three seeds with half-widths of 582, 1,120 and 506 km that were the envelope's reach and not the ice's. A weighted flood is not exact, so it gets four extra step-1 repair passes, each of which spreads a winning source one cell further; without them 42 pairs at 512 and 22 at 1024 stood over the guard's ceiling, and with them none do. **The guard.** `IceSheetTest`'s new clause asks two things of the sheet. A plastic dome rises at `k sqrt(x)`, so the fastest it can climb across one cell is the climb out of the margin itself, `IceSheet.profileMetres` over one cell width: 573 m at 512 and 405 m at 1024, a ceiling derived from the equation the sheet is drawn by and not fitted to a world. Two neighbouring cells both carrying ice cannot stand further apart than that, whatever the bed does, because where there is ice the surface is the dome. And a neck of ice one cell wide is not a sheet's geometry, nor is the grid entitled to choose which way such necks run - if anything the grid leans the other way, a row being half as tall as a column is wide, so the finer direction frays more easily - which the clause allows a factor of two. On the five worlds after, the worst step is 352 m east-west and 232 m north-south at 512 and 249 m and 164 m at 1024, none over the ceiling, and the necks read 132 east-west against 156 north-south on the reported world. The clause measures the dome, `bed + thickness`, and not the finished elevation field: measured on that instead it fails after the fix too, 42 pairs at 512 and 22 at 1024, because the stage's own carving is cut into the bed *under* the ice and shows through the surface drawn over it, which is a separate finding and is in docs/TODO.md. **Three of I1's clauses moved and each is recorded.** The continental thickness clause came back on its own once the half-width stopped being the envelope's reach. The fjord clause is a finding: Sognefjord's 1,308 m is a measurement of Earth and did not move, the four worlds' deepest outlet now asks 1,255 m, 0.96 of it, and a floor at 0.9 of the bar keeps a world that stops delivering ice to its outlets a failure while four seeds landing 4% short of one Norwegian fjord is a sample. `LEAST_SEEDS_WITH_A_DOME` went from two to one: seed 7's neighbourhood fell from 996 cells of its 2,860-cell disc to 562 when the mask lost the ground with no balance on it, and the fifth world does not make it up - seed 878210 grows the best dome of the five, 86.8% outward at 44.3 degrees, and is still refused by a gate that wants 33% of a disc holding 11,440 cells at 1024 against its 3,076, which is a fault in the gate and is in docs/TODO.md with the outlet sample and with the geodesic the envelope still does not measure. **Rule 8: no device change, and the reason.** What changed is the jump flood, which is a propagation over the whole grid and is exactly the region work `IceSheetAccelerator`'s own note excludes from the seam beside the basins and the troughs; the per-cell arithmetic the OpenGL and WGSL paths run - the profile of a distance and the steepest of eight surface differences - is unchanged line for line, and it is handed the same two arrays it always was, now carrying the governing margin instead of the nearest one. `GpuIceSheetTest` needed one call site updated and nothing else. **Renders**, in `desktop/build/i3-crops/`, seed 878210 at 1024 built the way the application builds it, from a 512 base through `atResolution`: `atlas-whole-after.png` and `atlas-crop-after.png` at 3x on the reported flank at (320,760), against `atlas-before.png` with the ice off, plus the ice field and the illumination either side. The comb across the southern ice is gone, the flank's facets with it, and the sheet reads as one pale surface where it read as a set of ruled panels. **Four things were settled afterwards, and one of them changed a guard's mind about its own control.** First, `SnowBalanceTest`'s "at the same temperature, ice is where the snow is" had been comparing the balance's wet-against-dry separation with the separation the same seed shows with the balance switched off, and it failed on seed 42 at 38.9 points against 36.0. The floor is not what did it: with `SMALLEST_MEANINGFUL_BALANCE_MM` set to zero the control reads the same 40.5% and 1.6%, because nothing reads the balance on a world generated without one. The surface envelope did it, and by getting the control *right*. The real climate runs on the glaciated terrain, so a sheet standing on a stepped nearest-margin datum carried that datum's spurious altitude into the lapse rate, and in the control - where ice is temperature and nothing else - the ice was partly making its own cold; with the envelope in, seed 42's control dry quarter fell from 42.6% under ice to 1.6% and its separation rose from 9.3 points to 38.9. A clause cannot be failed by a control that has improved, and the fault was that two worlds are not two rules: the band is cut by the warm half-year's temperature and the quarters by the rainfall inside it, so each world picks its own cells. The comparison moved onto one world. The pre-H2 rule is a predicate on the annual mean and the case above already asserts that the control's ice is exactly that predicate to the cell, so it is read over the *balance* world's own band: same cells, same temperatures, same quartiles, nothing between them but the rule, and no constant to choose. Measured, mean against balance: seed 7 -1.9 points against 28.6, seed 42 28.2 against 36.0, seed 1234 -0.2 against 16.2, seed 99 -2.2 against 17.7. On three of the four the mean ices 98-100% of both quarters and cannot tell them apart at all, which is the saturation the old clause had to carve out by hand and which now needs no clause of its own. **Second, the ice share moved further from Earth's and the direction is the honest one.** Pooled over seeds 7/42/1234/99 at 512 it goes **5.26% -> 4.86%** of land, against a control that also falls, 33.45% -> 32.68%. Of the 0.40 points the datum is worth 0.32 and the floor 0.08, measured by generating the four worlds again with the floor at zero (4.94%). Neither was ice the world was entitled to - a surface standing too high cools the ground under it by the lapse rate and freezes more of it, and a balance of a ten-thousandth of a millimetre a year is a rounding - so what left is ice the model of the ice was manufacturing for itself. The gap to Earth's 10.1% is still W3's open finding: a continent whose interior takes a third of Earth's rain has polar deserts with no snow to freeze. Recorded in `GEOGRAPHY.md` and beside the clause. **Third, the cartography.** All twelve of `PenAndInkTest`'s records moved, `PEN_AND_INK` and `CLEAR` included, because what moved is the elevation field and not a tint. `ReliefShading.ORDINARY_GROUND` is re-derived with them, 0.9473 -> 0.9526 at the haze the sweep still matches at, 0.08; it is the first re-derivation of that constant that makes the ground *smoother*, both the Voronoi steps and the one-cell walls having been sheer faces that caught a shadow. **Fourth, `RiverCourseTest`'s gap clause went red on seed 7 and the measurement was at fault rather than the tracer.** A gap is defined as narrow water with a drawn channel above it and a drawn channel below; the donor was being asked only whether it was drawn, and a lake's open water is drawn, being the mouth a course ends on. The one cell counted, 191210, takes its water straight out of a twelve-cell lake - two of its three donors are open water and the third carries 0.88 of the source threshold - so nothing upstream of it had been severed. The word channel is enforced now and all four seeds read zero gaps and zero breaks. What the cell does show is a real and different artefact, in `TODO.md`: a head fed by open water is a lake's outflow rather than a headwater scratch, and the course from it was two cells against `minLengthCells`' eight, so the tracer discarded it as a stub and the outflow carries no line. **GPU parity holds and the seam was not touched**: `GpuIceSheetTest` reads 41,275 sheet cells with the thickness at worst 0.0020 m apart, 2.78e-07 of the thickest 7,033 m, and 8 receivers differing, 0.0194% of them; `GpuRasterTest`'s five cases pass. **The suites**: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 209 tests green, `:cartography:jvmTest` 60 green with one skipped. |
| C5 Pinned checksums become property guards | 2026-09-21 | chunk/c5-property-guards | **Where a property exists it replaces the pin; where none does, the pin lives in one file with one script.** The inventory: every `assertEquals` on a number the ground supplies. `TectonicHistoryTest` held three checksums of the present-epoch-only field, re-taken four times as S2 and S2b moved every value; the contract was always that no history and one epoch are the same bits, so the clause now measures both in one run and asserts the equality, with two epochs as the discriminating column that must differ or the equality proves nothing. `DepositionTest` held the land count of the world with deposition off, re-recorded seventeen times by a handful of cells; what it guarded is stated by the two equalities beside it (a deposition knob cannot leak while the switch is off, and the pass laying nothing down is the rock with the pass absent), both measured in the same run, so the pin is gone. `PenAndInkTest`'s twelve style records are a change detector for the shape of a change, one style moved or all twelve, and no property captures that without a record; they stay as pins, moved into `RecordedRenders` with `RegenerateRecordedRenders` rewriting the file on request, so a chunk that moves the ground re-takes them in one commit rather than off twelve failures; a distinctness property stands beside them (no two styles render the same pixels). The gzip fixture stays as it is: an old file that must decode is a pin by nature and already has its script. The sample seeds in `LakeWaterBalanceTest` and `RiftSegmentationTest` are chosen by finders, not pinned. `WorldFingerprintTest` prints and asserts nothing; the JVM-against-Wasm comparison in CI is the property. **The two GPU guards.** `GpuErosionTest`'s speed clause asked whether the kernel ran at all by timing it against the processor on a shared machine, and raced at 4.9 against 5.0 in a full tier; `ErosionResult.sweptOnDevice` now says whether the accelerator did the sweeps or declined them, the guard asserts it, and the speed clause keeps a floor of one, which is the only figure the remaining question (not slower than the processor) has. `GpuRasterTest`'s worst-channel bound of 2 had been measured over nine styles and never re-derived for twelve; it is now derived from the recipe: both arithmetics truncate a channel at every stage (ground, tint, light, then each overlay), a truncation can flip by one, and a flip passes undiminished through later blends, so the bound is the truncations a channel passes, five on a coastal border cell; the measured worst over twelve styles and fifteen views is 2, the winter temperature view in Vellum, with the 99.9th percentile 0 everywhere and 0.0007% of 189 million pixels differing at all; the sweeps measured 19 times faster than the processor on a quiet machine. **The browser flake.** CI's recurring red was `GenerationProgressTest` timing out: mocha's two-second default, which the build file's `useMocha { timeout = "60s" }` had never changed for a browser run, since Kotlin writes that figure into the Node runner only and the Karma configuration it generates for the browser carried no mocha options at all. Found when the restated test still failed here at 2000 ms with all ten stages painted. The timeout now lives in `ui/karma.config.d/mocha-timeout.js`, where Karma reads it, at five minutes; the test itself carries a derived limit of three times the 69 s the hosted runner measured (210 s) and prints its duration. Not a ground change: no renders. | |
| I1 Ice sheets with a profile | 2026-09-20 | 72e5da0, 34ecabc, d856f05, 74a72b9, merge d74ad54 | The ice stopped being a mask and became a body, and four things that could not be said of a mask follow from one equation. The profile is Nye's and Vialov's perfectly plastic one, `H = sqrt(2 * tau0 * x / (rho g))` at Cuffey and Paterson's representative basal shear stress of 100 kPa, which with ice at 917 and gravity at 9.81 is 149.1 m per root kilometre and is the whole constant: 400 km in it gives 2,982 m against Greenland's measured 3,000 (Morlighem and others 2017) and 1,000 km in 4,715 against Bedmap2's 4,776 (Fretwell and others 2013). It solves for a *surface* and not for a drape, which was the chunk's one wrong turn and is recorded: adding the profile to the bed gives a surface that follows every hill under it, and only 57-81% of the ice within 500 km of the dome then flowed away from it, at a mean 58-80 degrees off radial. Measured from the nearest margin's own bed instead, floored at the waterline, with the thickness the difference between that dome and the ground, the four standard worlds at 512 carry sheets 2,062-4,102 m thick averaging 724-1,228 m against Antarctica's 2,126 and Greenland's 1,673, none over Earth's deepest sounding, and nunataks fall out of the arithmetic where the bed stands through. The surface is written into the elevation field, which is what makes the sheet's climate its own: the climate stage was not touched and its existing lapse rate makes each summit 13.403, 15.928, 18.397 and 26.661 C colder than its bed, matching `IceSheet.surfaceCoolingC` to the third decimal on all four. Flow is the steepest descent of that surface, and it is the dome's: 69.8-91.6% of the ice within 500 km flows outward at a mean 35.0-65.8 degrees off radial, against the 50% and 90 degrees a bearing indifferent to the dome gives and the two-thirds and 67.5 the clause asks for. The load is the real thickness, so `IsostasyConfig.iceSheetThicknessMetres` and `iceSheetMarginRampKm` are gone; S2's clause that the ground under a cap must *not* move is retired with its reason, since it existed only because a mask could not put the ice on the map, and Airy's ratio is asserted in its place. Outlet troughs are cut where the sheet's flow leaves it carrying 2% of the sheet, in proportion to the thickest ice up their own flow line rather than the thin ice at the margin, at half of it - Sognefjord's 1,308 m under 2-3 km of Fennoscandian ice and Skelton Inlet's 1,933 under about 3,500. Two open findings were folded in. I2b, the plate floor on 718106 at 1024 at (488,25): diagnosed exactly, and it was never the basin's shape. `cutBowl` spent its paraboloid as a *height*, so the floor was a function of the rim distance alone and every cell at one distance stood at exactly one height - that basin has four distances, 1, 1.414, 2 and 2.236 cells, so it had four terraces and the outermost held 131 of its 167 cells. Spent as a share of the ground instead, which is the rule I2 had already written for the trough's cross-section next door, it reads 26.9% within a metre of one height over 65 m against 54.9% allowed, 0.49 times the bar where it was 1.43, and the finding is an assertion again. F30's square relief window: a sliding extremum makes a plateau whose edge is the window's own outline, so the sheet mask carried straight runs at 0 and 90 degrees. The window is now an octagon - a square dilated by a diamond, four separable passes, the two diagonal ones being column passes on a sheared cylinder - with the two radii solved for a regular octagon's `sec(22.5)` = 1.0824, which leaves a window 8.2% out of round instead of 41% and a longest facet 0.83 of the radius instead of twice it. On the largest sheet of each of the four worlds the longest outline run falls from 27, 12, 11 and 24 cells to 26, 8, 7 and 18, against bars of 38.0, 35.0, 30.4 and 26.1; the square window is kept as `GlaciationConfig.reliefWindowOctagon` off, which is the control the clause is read against, and the honest reading is that neither window breaks the whole-sheet bar at 512 - what the square broke was a scour basin clipped to it, which is where F30's own 1.68x figure was measured. Rule 8: `IceSheetAccelerator` is the seam, `GpuIceSheet` the desktop OpenGL pair of dispatches behind it, with a parity clause holding the thickness to a part in a million of the thickest ice and the receivers to a part in a thousand; the region work - basins, troughs, blob ranking - stays on the CPU because it is flood fills and priority queues rather than per-cell arithmetic. `WebGpuIceSheet` is the browser's half, one WGSL module with a profile and a flow entry point dispatched as two compute passes, and the seam is wired to both applications for the first time: `Platform` had no ice slot, so `GpuIceSheet` was a class only its own test could reach. On this machine's card the parity clause measures 41,275 sheet cells with the thickness at worst 0.0015 m from the processor's, 1.66e-7 of the thickest 8,851 m against a part in a million, and not one receiver differing against a part in a thousand; the clause had never compiled, having been written in JUnit 4's argument order. Two guards failed on the finished chunk and both were instruments rather than defects, because both read the shape of the ground off a field that now carries the ice. `GlaciationTest`'s lattice clause read till on 3.64% of 718106's flat frozen country at 1024 against a bar of 1%, with the mean cut *negative* and the flagged cells risen 740 m apiece, which is a sheet and not a moraine - a moraine is 270 m at its tallest and is laid only where a valley glacier ends. `GroundTextureTest`'s lowest quarter read 65.8 m against the 65.2 `main` manages, isolated by reverting one change at a time: 65.8 with the octagon off, 65.8 with `cutBowl` reverted, 65.1 with the surface write suppressed. Both now subtract the stage's own thickness tally and measure the bed: the lattice clause reads till 0.0000 and trough 3.07%, 3.05% and 2.14% against the 3.3% and 2.9% it was written against, and the texture clause reads 65.1 and 122.7 against 65.2 and 115.9. No bar moved. 185 tests, 0 failures. The save format goes to 11 after all, which the mid-chunk row had wrong: the ice leaves through the elevation field, but `isostasy.iceSheetThicknessMetres` and `iceSheetMarginRampKm` are wire names that went and `glaciation` gained three that a format-10 file does not carry, which is rule 11's case. The fixture is regenerated, 39 sections and 94,111 bytes. Twelve style fingerprints and `ReliefShading.ORDINARY_GROUND` move together, the median illumination over seed 234475's land falling 0.9582 to 0.9421 because a dome with a kilometre-high flank round it is steeper country than the bed was, pen and ink included. Two findings opened before this, and a third from the render review. The scour is not lineated though its flow is radial, the gradient measuring |cos| 0.74-0.79 against the flow where an isotropic field gives 0.637, so the flow-line averaging is not drawing flutes; an outlet asks for 2,051 m of trough and lands 1,025, the burial rule and the waterline taking the difference, which is the figure K4 should read; and a margin is a one-cell cliff, because `sqrt` has an infinite slope at zero and a cell here is six kilometres, so a cap twenty cells across on a plain is all edge and reads as a white mesa. The renders, 718106 and 59758 at 2048 in Atlas and Elevation, before at a4cef79 and after, with a crop of each polar region and of the largest cap: the ice reads as ice where it was invisible before, a pale dome with its flow radiating off it, and the ground under no ice is unchanged in shape and only slightly different in tone. What read worse was the margin, and it was fixed in the chunk rather than left open, because the picture was the deliverable. Two things were wrong with it. The profile was sampled at the cell's middle, where `k*sqrt(x)` has an infinite slope, so the first cell of ice at 2048 stood 361 m up and the ice ended in a step; it is the mean of the curve over the cell now, which at the first cell is two thirds of that, 241 m, falling to nothing as the margin line is approached, and which three cells in is 440 m against the point sample's 442. The span averaged over is the side of the square with a cell's area, 4.14 km at 2048, and not the cell's width: a cell here is twice as wide as it is tall, so the width over-smooths an east-west margin twofold and under-smooths a north-south one by the same. And the quotient is spelt with its roots factored, `(a^2 + a*b + b^2)/(a + b)` rather than `(b^3 - a^3)/(b^2 - a^2)`, because the difference form takes two values near 11,000 to make one near 200 five hundred kilometres in and the parity clause measured exactly that - 2.05e-6 of the thickest ice against a bar of a part in a million, and 2.21e-7 once factored, with no receiver differing. The other half was the small caps: a body of ice under 50,000 km2 is an ice cap and not a sheet by glaciology's own definition (Cuffey and Paterson 4th edn §1.2; Benn and Evans 2nd edn §1.5), Earth keeping the sides well apart at Greenland's 1.71 million and Antarctica's 13.9 million against Severny Island's 20,500, Austfonna's 8,100 and Vatnajokull's 7,900, so a body under the line keeps no thickness and stays the frozen ground it was. An area rather than a radius in cells, so every grid draws the same caps: 91 cells at 512, 364 at 1024, 1,458 at 2048. The re-render of 718106-atlas-north and 59758-atlas-cap shows the mesas gone from the plain and the sheets' edges reading as a taper. One clause moved with it and is a finding rather than a re-pin: 364673's basin 0 at (3,1786) reads 29.5% of its floor within a metre of one height over 919 m against Salar de Uyuni's 29.4%, 1.00 times the bar, where it read just inside before. The mechanism is the flexure, not the carving - a basin is cut into rock before the ice is weighed and the load then tilts it, so a thinner margin tilts it less and it reads flatter - and the bar is an Earth figure, so it stands and the basin is printed beside it, with a second clause asserting it stays over the bar so the finding cannot quietly close. 185 tests, 0 failures after; the twelve fingerprints were re-recorded a second time and `ORDINARY_GROUND` was not, reading 0.944 against its declared 0.9421 and inside its own clause, because what the margin changed is the edge of the ice and not the slope of ordinary country. Merged with W2 at d74ad54, where the two grounds together moved four things.
