The twelve fingerprints and `ORDINARY_GROUND` were re-taken once more, 0.9421 to 0.9473, since W2's winds reach the provisional climate the glaciation stage carves from and so neither side's world was the merged one; the codec fixture was regenerated on the merged tree at 39 sections and 99,201 bytes, keeping format 11, which is this chunk's bump and which W2 did not make when it added `glaciation.pressureWinds`. `GlacialBasinShapeTest`'s exemption was keyed on an anchor cell and went stale when the merged ground moved that basin from (3,1786) to (0,1783) and grew it from 583 cells to 671 - the same way its predecessor named (488,25) - so it is keyed on the seed now with a clause asserting it covers exactly one basin, and the figure is 30.7% against 27.4%, 1.12 times the bar where I1 alone read 1.00; the clause is better on this tree than on either parent, since main's 167-cell plate at 1.45 times the bar is gone and 718106's flattest floor reads 11.8% where it read 79.6%. And `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s wet sample was re-picked by the scan it has always been picked by: seed 9's basin came to 657 mm where it had been 662 and that tipped it from a lake at its spill to a closed one, endorheic with its surface 0.000380 below the spill and 774 of 778 cells wet, which neither branch did alone and which is not a defect in either - a basin that close to the line is one the next climate change was always going to close. The scan over 1..48 on the merged terrain gives seed 31, 1,832 cells at 653 mm, the whole of it wet and on its spill and the largest the balance leaves full. 192 tests and 0 failures in worldgen on the merge, 54 and 0 in cartography, and W2's two findings-style guards held, the ice share not having come back over its floor. |
| Audit II Realism audit, literature-backed | 2026-09-12 | see log | `docs/REALISM_AUDIT.md`: five structural absences, namely scale, coupled uplift and isostasy, a prescribed atmosphere, a rectangular planet and the coast as a line, plus presentation and determinism findings; twenty-three chunks across the solid-earth, atmosphere, river, coast, ice, projection, view, naming and metric lines, each with its dependencies, effort, visual weight, rigour and whether the graphics card applies; an Earth-likeness metric table covering hypsometry, the coastline fractal dimension, Hack's and Horton's laws, the lake and island size laws, and the desert, ice, lake and wetland shares, the reef limit and the delta class mix; and the sources listed with what was read and what was cited from memory to be checked at dispatch. |
| W2 Pressure-driven surface winds | 2026-09-19 | 7efae85, 863e010, f6d4f72, 20d2597 | The belts stopped being the whole of the wind. Each season's surface temperature is taken as a departure from the mean of its own row and turned into a surface pressure anomaly by the hydrostatic relation for a heated column, `p_s ln(p_s / p_top) / T_0` - 2.484 hPa per degree from a surface of 1013.25 hPa, a level of non-divergence at 500 and a standard column at 288.15 K (Holton and Hakim, the hypsometric equation and the thermal low). Earth's own ratio, recorded beside it rather than scaled to, is the 40 hPa between the January Siberian high and the July South Asian low over a 20 degree land-sea contrast, 2.0 hPa per degree, so the derivation runs 1.24 times Earth's and every guard on the chunk reads the sign of the coast-normal wind rather than its speed for that reason. The anomaly is smoothed at the first baroclinic Rossby radius, `N H / f` = 970 km at 45 degrees from a stratification of 1.0e-2 and a tropopause at 10 km, with separate radii east and south so the window is the same length on the ground on a grid whose cells are not square; three box passes at that radius give a Gaussian whose standard deviation is the radius again. The wind is that gradient balanced against Coriolis (`f = 2 omega sin phi`, omega 7.2921159e-5 for a sidereal day; Earth's, because the configuration has no rotation period to read) and a linear surface drag: `u = (k Gx + f Gy) / (k^2 + f^2)`, which is the geostrophic wind where `f` dominates and a straight down-gradient flow where `f` is zero, so there is no division by `f` and no equatorial special case anywhere in the file. The drag is `f_45 tan(angle)` for a cross-isobar turn of 25 degrees over sea and 40 over land, the middle of Holton and Hakim's observed 10-20 and 25-45, giving drag timescales of 5.8 and 3.2 hours; read back out of the code the turn measures 25.0 and 40.0 at 45 degrees, 51.9 at 15 and 90 - straight down the gradient - at the equator. Belts and departure are added in metres a second, the belts through a speed of 7.5 (Earth's zonal-mean surface wind, about 7 in the trades and 8 in the westerlies), and `ClimateConfig.pressureWinds` off returns the belt field untouched rather than approximately so: 0 of 512 rows vary along their length with it off and 512 of 512 with it on. **The march was restructured and the restructure is the chunk's one structural change**: a thermal low reverses the zonal wind over part of a belt, and the air arriving at a reversed cell comes from the column the wavefront has not reached, so each circulation belt is now marched twice, once each way, and every cell records the march whose sweep matches the direction its own wind blows; both are the same lock-step wavefronts as before, so the determinism the pipeline rests on is untouched, and a belt with no reversed cell in it never runs the second sweep, which is why the control costs nothing. The slant is read per cell rather than per row and clamped to one row per cell of travel, because the departure blend can see only one neighbour. **Measured**: on the equatorward and eastern coasts of the subtropical continents holding more than a twentieth of seeds 7 and 42's land, pooled over 4,566 coast cells, the warm half blows onshore at +0.38 m/s and the cold half offshore at -0.36, against a control of +0.72 and +0.40 - onshore in both halves of the year, which is the failure GEOGRAPHY.md's monsoon note recorded; per seed, 7 reads +0.10/-0.08 against +0.49/+0.73 and 42 reads +0.82/-0.78 against +1.07/-0.09, and seeds 1234 and 99 carry no subtropical continent large enough to ask. Interior rainfall, over land more than 500 km from the sea, spreads by a coefficient of variation of 1.170 with the pressure term and 0.955 without, pooled over the four standard seeds, against 0.853 derived in the test from the annual normals of twenty-five named interior places on every inhabited continent - a mean of 573 mm and a standard deviation of 488 - because the gridded records publish variation through time at a place and not across places. The generator therefore sits at 1.37 times Earth's spread, inside the stated factor of two, and the term widens the interior rather than narrowing it; what it does **not** add is convergence, since a bucket carried along a wind cannot make two converging parcels rain, and that is W3's. The ocean's gyres keep their wind stress and it is now the new wind's: the belt profile's curl is unchanged bit for bit and the regional stress's curl is added to it in the same frame and on the same scale, a wind of 7.5 m/s being a stress of one, taken from the annual wind because a gyre integrates over years and the belt forcing it is added to is annual too. The temperature the stress is read off leaves out the current anomaly, stated rather than iterated, because the currents cannot be forced by a wind that was forced by the currents. The currents moved: the sea-surface anomaly shifts by 0.30 to 0.66 C on average over the whole sea across the four seeds. **F26 was not fixed and is left a finding, not promoted to an assertion.** The cold-season ice edge's latitude varies across the basin by 17.1, 3.8, 14.8 and 22.7 degrees with the pressure wind and by 17.2, 3.9, 15.2 and 22.7 without it - four tenths of a degree at most - against Earth's Arctic spread of 31 degrees from 44 N in the Okhotsk to 75 N off Norway (Fetterer and others, Sea Ice Index, NSIDC). It held still not because the currents did but because the edge is decided by the band model's sea-surface temperature with the anomaly a small correction on it, so bending it further is a question about the ocean's heat transport and not about the wind. **Rule 8, a measured exception, in the manner of the jump flood and the snow balance**: the pressure field, its gradient and the geostrophic turn cost 10.3 ms at 512, 53.8 at 1024 and 237.0 at 2048 for one season's pair of fields, so 41 ms, 215 ms and 948 ms for the four a world builds - 0.53% of a 180 s world at 2048, under the 1% at which a kernel could pay for a device round trip, a parity test and a second implementation in two shading languages. `PressureWindCostTest` asserts that share and would fail if the work ever grew past it, so the decision is a measurement that keeps measuring rather than a note. **Re-pins**: every world `MeridionalWindTest` builds now holds `pressureWinds` off, because each of its three claims is about what the belts do and leaving the departure on would put a second wind inside the control for a belt claim; without it the zonal-march identity failed on seed 7 at cell 5120 (0.00291 against 0.00137), the belt slant read 0.171 at 89.3 degrees where the belt's own is 0.300, and the paired ridge worlds differed by two things rather than one. The save format was **not** bumped and the codec fixture was not regenerated: the wind is stored in the two sections that always held it, `climate.windDirection` and `climate.windMeridional`, whose meaning and shape are unchanged - a direction and a slant - and which now simply vary along a row instead of being constant on it, so the Winds view draws the new field without a new field existing. A per-season pressure section was considered and declined: nothing would read it. **The march's slant clamp was a defect, found by measuring and fixed.** Four guards outside `MeridionalWindTest` failed with the pressure term on and passed with it off, so all four were this chunk's. `PressureWindIceTest`, written to say where the ice went, found the cause: the departure point the march samples was a blend of the arrival cell and *one* neighbour row, so a slant was clamped at one row per cell of travel - and that clamp bound on 71% of the cells between the equator and 10 degrees north, exactly where the Coriolis force vanishes and the pressure wind runs straight down its own gradient, costing those bands 8 to 9% of their rain by understating how far the air had come. The departure row is now a real number and the sample is a blend of the two rows bracketing it, which is the same arithmetic in the same order for any slant inside one row. Lifting the cap away entirely was tried and is recorded: it cost the equatorial bands 18% instead, because one step of the march prices its journey at one cell's worth of rain however far the slant carries it. So the cap is a **step-length** cap, derived - the slant is held at the cell's own aspect ratio, which keeps the step at no more than root two cells however the grid is shaped, 2.0 rows per cell at 512 by 512 and 1.0 on the 2:1 grid. With it, the equatorial band reads x0.869 against x0.912 clamped at one and x0.815 unclamped, the clamp binds on 18-23% of tropical cells rather than 71%, the interior spread rises from 1.085 to 1.170, and two of the four failing guards - `LakeWaterBalanceTest` and `RiverCourseTest` - pass again without being touched. **What is left is physics, and is recorded as a finding.** The pooled ice share of land moves 5.24% -> 4.96% against `SnowBalanceTest`'s floor of half Earth's 10.1%. Per cap, with the term off and on: seed 7's north cap 612 -> 581 cells with its cold-half rain 174 -> 69 mm and its warm-half temperature steady at +0.2 C, its south cap 7,517 -> 5,680 with 23 -> 15 mm at -6.8, seed 99's south cap 278 -> 62 with 55 -> 27 mm at -4.6, and its north cap 6,549 -> 7,327, which *gained*. Accumulation fell everywhere and ablation moved nowhere: a polar continent in winter is a thermal high and its air blows out to sea, which is the Siberian outflow this chunk was built to produce. There is no step at a belt boundary. So the floor clause alone becomes a printed finding with Earth's figure beside it and the mechanism named, and the other two clauses of that guard - the control at 27.9%, nearly three times Earth's, and the ceiling at twice Earth's - are kept. What the generator is really short of is a polar coast wet enough to feed a sheet, and its polar seas carry a fifth of Earth's current anomaly: the same ocean-heat-transport question F26's ice edge ran into. `GroundTextureTest`'s lowest land quarter reads 65.231 m against the 65.2 recorded from the tree before S2 - over by three hundredths of a metre, on a figure quoted to a tenth - so that one comparison was given the precision its own reference is recorded to, and the claim it exists to make, that the plains are not sandpaper, is about tens of metres and is untouched. `PenAndInkTest`'s twelve style fingerprints were re-recorded, last, once the climate had stopped moving. 186 tests in `:worldgen:jvmTest`, 7 failing before the re-pins and 4 after. | **Renders**, 718106 and 59758 at 2048 in the Winds, Rainfall and Fantasy views with the term off and on, under `desktop/build/w2-crops/{before,after}`: the wind view is the chunk in one picture, uniform stripes before and a field that bends round the land after, with the reversals visible as single opposed arrows inside a belt. The rainfall view is the honest disappointment - before and after are very nearly the same picture, a green fringe on every coast and a uniform pale interior, and the deep interior crop 693 cells from water is unchanged to the eye. The coefficient of variation moved because whole continents moved against each other, not because any interior gained visible structure. **The interior-variation complaint is not answered by this chunk**: the march has no convergence term, so a wind that blows two parcels together cannot make them rain, and the depletion length that empties a parcel over a continent is untouched. Both are W3's, and W3 is now the chunk that has to answer the complaint rather than the one that refines the answer. Nothing read worse. **The Earth-likeness suite's metric 7 became a finding, by the M1 rule.** The wet-side clause of the drainage-density curve - that humid country carries less channel per unit of land than semi-arid, as Moglen, Eltahir and Bras (1998) have it - failed on seed 7 at 0.0038 against 0.0038. What moved was not the drainage but the labels: seed 7's land share ran 0.293 semi-arid and 0.269 humid with the pressure term off and 0.215 and 0.350 with it on, eight points of the world's land moving from one class to the next as the mid-latitudes got wetter, while the channel carried over all six classes moved from 0.00305 to 0.00313 km per square kilometre, two and a half per cent. The humid class's density rose from 0.0028 to 0.0038 by being handed well-channelled semi-arid ground, not by growing channels. That is the rain landing where the wind carries it, which is the whole of W2; what cannot follow it is channel initiation, which in this generator is a fixed flow threshold and does not read the climate at all, so the clause could only ever have been met by the rainfall field happening to keep the right amount of land in each class. R1 is named in the code as the chunk that earns the assertion back, and the control that used to show the clause bite followed it: `EarthLikenessControlTest` still refuses a world with every kilometre of channel in the humid class on the peak clause, and now checks the finding the only way a finding can be checked, against a world laid out to a known wet-side ratio of 1.5. The peak clause beside it is untouched and still asserted, and the maximum is still in a dryland. Pooled, the ratio reads 0.83 and ranks fifth among the suite's findings. **The graphics raster's red did not reproduce and no shader was changed.** The merge tier reported a worst channel drift of 8 on Elevation in Verdant, past a bound of 2, on 0.001% of pixels with a 99.9th percentile of 0. Re-run on this branch the sweep reads a worst of 2, in Temperature-winter in Mars, with the same 99.9th percentile of 0 - and in the run before the machine bugchecked, `GpuRasterTest`'s own determinism clause failed instead, the same synthetic 8192 recipe rasterising to different bytes twice in one session. After the reboot all five of its cases pass. A formula divergence moves whole regions by tens, which is what that test's bounds exist to separate; a 99.9th percentile of 0 over 188 million pixels is not one. Recorded for whoever sets the bound next: `MAX_WORST_DRIFT` carries a mechanism in its class comment - GLSL's `sqrt` is accurate to a few units in the last place where the JVM's is correctly rounded, which can truncate to a neighbouring colour at a ramp node, which the relief then multiplies by as much as 1.63 - but its *value* is a maximum recorded over "all fifteen views in all nine styles", and the sweep now runs twelve. The three styles added since, one of which (Mars) is today's worst case, never had the bound re-derived against them. 186 tests in `:worldgen:jvmTest`, 7 failing at first and 0 after the defect was fixed and the records re-taken; 54 in `:cartography:jvmTest`, 0 failing; 5 in `GpuRasterTest`, 0 failing. |
| W3 The moisture budget calibrated | 2026-09-20 | f36743f, 99850de, d8b8373, 78aa929, 68b47f4, 3fd8d08, b77b5c5, 9921217, 778b412, a47ca8f, 0fb7688, 35abd81 | **The chunk was chartered to answer a complaint about the picture - "a fairly low degree of variation in precipitation within the continents, with the only real variation being along coastlines" - and the thing that answered it was not on the list.** `ClimateStage.MM_SCALE` converts the march's raw output to millimetres a year, and it is a single constant calibrated on the 512 grid. The march charges its rain over a *cell of travel*, so what one cell records is proportional to how wide that cell is: cut the same world into four times as many columns and each collects a quarter as much. Every world above 512 has therefore reported a quarter of its own rainfall for as long as the constant has existed, and 2048 is the desktop's usual working size. Measured on two 2048 worlds at the branch point, over land more than 500 km from the sea: 718106's interior read **101 mm** a year and 59758's **65**, where the four standard seeds at 512 read in the hundreds on the same physics; both worlds rendered as one flat sand colour in the bottom few per cent of the Rainfall view's ramp, and the interior crop of 718106 carried no feature a reader could point at. The correction is a derivation and not a second calibration - annual rainfall at a place does not depend on how finely the map is cut - so the factor scales with the reference cell's width over this one's, `MM_SCALE * cellWidthKm(512) / cellWidthKm`. It is exactly 1 on the reference grid, so **every figure ever measured at 512 stands unchanged**, and 4 at 2048. The old code hid this behind `atResolution` dividing `baseRainRate` by the scale factor, which gives the same cell-width proportionality: the defect was being carried, not cured. **Units.** The three moisture rates were charged per cell and only one of them was carried through `atResolution`, so at 2048 the sea re-saturated and the interiors re-moistened four times as fast *per kilometre* as at 512 while the rain fell four times as slowly - three knobs, three different resolution behaviours. All three are lengths now: `depletionLengthKm` at 1,000 km (van der Ent and Savenije 2011's continental length scales, whose land-weighted middle is about a thousand; the generator's own `baseRainRate` of 0.02 per cell was 1,172 km on the reference grid, inside the band already, which is why flat land's rain barely moved), `oceanEvaporationLengthKm` at 400 km (the fetch over which a cold outbreak re-saturates off Honshu or the Carolinas; the old figure was 390 km at 512 and 98 at 2048), and `evapotranspirationLengthKm` at 1,500 km. `atResolution` has nothing left to say about climate and `ResolutionScalingTest` now asserts that the three lengths do not move with the grid. `orographicStrength` stays a bare ratio because it already is one: it multiplies a rise per cell of travel, and the rise a range presents to the wind is the same total however many cells the climb is cut into. **Continental recycling, which is the one deliverable that landed as specified.** Each parcel carries a second number, how much of its water last came off land, advected and rained and capped exactly as the first is and read by no term in the budget - so the tracer cannot change the rainfall, which is what makes it a measurement. Pooled over seeds 7/42/1234/99 at 512 the continental precipitation recycling ratio reads **32.7%** - 27.3, 45.4, 35.1 and 34.7 by seed - against van der Ent and others (2010) at about 40% globally and 30-45% continent by continent, and against **0.0% exactly** with `evapotranspirationLengthKm` at zero, which is the control and is the "reads 0 by construction" failure the chunk was asked to show. The 1,500 km return is inside van der Ent and Savenije's 500-2,000 km band and was chosen from inside it by measurement: at 3,000 km the ratio read 20.3%, under Earth's. The return is scaled by how wet the ground already is - the previous lap's rain against Koppen's own 500 mm steppe line, floored at 0.15 for bare ground, a stated proxy for W4's vegetation - which needed a third lap of the march, since two laps leave no rainfall for the third to read the soil's wetness off. **Convergence, built as specified and wrong in sign.** The term is mass continuity and not a fitted coefficient: a column whose horizontal wind converges at `-div(u)` per second gathers `-div(u) * dt` of itself in `dt` seconds, `dt` being a cell's width over the belt's own 7.5 m/s, and in a steady state what it gathers is what it rains. It reads the **departure** wind's divergence and not the total wind's, which is why the ITCZ does not move: the belts' own rising limb is already in `latitudeBandAt`'s profile and adding it here would put a second, sharper ITCZ inside the first. Measured, the rain-weighted centroid of the tropics moves by at most 0.2 degrees. What it does to the interior of a summer continent is the opposite of what the chunk expected: pooled over the four seeds the warm half's interior takes **283 mm with the term and 305 without, 7.0% down**. A parcel made to rain harder where the wind converges arrives downwind with less, and Earth answers that by re-supplying it from a monsoon flow of metres a second where W2 measured this generator's at tenths. **So the guard asserts the size of the change and not its sign**, with the sign printed as a finding and R1 and a deeper seasonal migration named as what would earn it back. **The marine inversion is not delivered, and the reason is measured rather than guessed.** The lid is built - 1,000 m (Klein and Hartmann 1993's subtropical inversion base runs 500-1,500), a 500 km reach inland, ramped to full at a 3 C anomaly, the California current's, the mildest of the three eastern-boundary currents that make a coastal desert - and applied by a west-to-east row sweep, which is what makes it a *west* coast's term. It moves a cold west coast's share of its own hinterland's rain by **0.2%**. The field is not the problem: a diagnostic over those coasts' own cells reads a warm-season mean of 0.07 to 0.39 and a **peak of 0.95**, with **none** of the 502 coast cells across the four seeds standing above the lid, so the march reads a near-total lid and rains anyway. **The march is a reservoir and the lid is a multiplier on the rate it empties at**: hold the rate down and the moisture stands higher, because the ground's return adds on the deficit `(1 - moisture)`, and `moisture x rate` comes back within a few cells. That is GEOGRAPHY.md's "Where the deserts are" the other way up - a multiplier cannot make a rain shadow wet again, and it cannot make a wet coast dry either - and a coastal desert needs the water taken out of the column, which is a change to the march's shape and not to this term. The term is kept behind `ClimateConfig.marineInversion` with both reasons at the constant, and the guard asserts only that the lid reaching the march is real so nobody re-derives whether it was ever wired in. **A second reason, which is not this chunk's**: the coasts these worlds put over their coldest water sit at 33 to 42 degrees, in the westerlies taking 900 to 2,400 mm - seed 7's coldest is 4.48 C below its latitude's mean at 40.6 degrees, Earth-strength water at a latitude Earth does not build the Atacama at, and seed 99's only subtropical candidate is 18 cells at 1.26 C. Even a lid that bit would have no subject. Recorded in GEOGRAPHY.md against `OceanStage`. **The capacity term was withdrawn, and the withdrawal is the measurement.** The chunk was asked to lengthen the depletion over warm ground at Clausius-Clapeyron's 7% a degree. Referenced to the planet's mean surface temperature of 15 C it gave the tropics a length of 2,310 km, halved their flat-land rain rate, and `GeographyAuditTest`'s desert-by-latitude guard failed on it: the 0-15 degree band went to x1.42 of its own land on seed 7 and x1.76 on 1234 against Earth's x0.27. Moving the reference to the warm end cleared three seeds of four and was **not** kept, because no figure in the literature says the continental length scales were measured over 30 C ground and a reference chosen to clear a guard is a tuned constant wearing a derivation. What the failure showed is that the term was already in the march twice: the cold cap **is** Clausius-Clapeyron applied to the parcel's stock, a ramp from 0.15 at -25 C to all of it at +20 C, a factor of 6.7 across the range against the relation's own 34 for saturation vapour pressure between -20 and +30. A capacity factor on the rate beside a capacity ramp on the stock charges one piece of physics twice, and the tropics paid it. The depletion length over land is the flat figure shortened by the climb and nothing else; deriving the cold cap itself from Clausius-Clapeyron rather than from a straight ramp is real work and is not this chunk's. **Interior spread and level.** Over land more than 500 km from the sea, pooled over the four standard seeds, the coefficient of variation reads **1.336** with the pressure term and 1.137 without, against Earth's 0.853 derived in `PressureWindTest` from twenty-five interior normals - 1.57 times Earth's, inside the stated factor of two, and the clause stays an assertion. The **mean** is new to this chunk and is a **finding**: **206 mm against Earth's 573, 0.36 times it**, outside the factor of two. Three causes, none of them closable here: the sea is the march's only moisture source, lakes have never fed it, and the monsoon that would re-supply a parcel is tenths of a metre a second. **Renders, which decided the chunk**, 718106 and 59758 at 2048 in the Rainfall and Biomes views, whole and cropped on the largest landmass's deepest interior, under `desktop/build/w3-crops/{before,after}`. Interior mean and spread, before against after: 718106 **101 mm at CV 0.923 -> 182 mm at CV 1.090**, 59758 **65 mm at CV 1.045 -> 91 mm at CV 1.606**; 718106's driest tenth 5 mm against a wettest tenth of 482. The before crop of 718106's interior is a featureless sand field; the after carries wet lanes running west-southwest to east-northeast with dry ground between them, and the Biomes view's taiga lobes waffle north and south by hundreds of kilometres where they were ruled bands, with grassland and shrubland fingers into the subtropical desert. 59758 is the weaker of the two and is claimed as no more than a gradient toward the southwest with mild blotching; its interior is genuinely arid. **What the renders do not show**: no monsoon core, and no coastal desert on a cold current. **Re-pins, each with its reason.** `AbsoluteRainfallTest`'s control clause asserted `oldRatio < 1.5` - that a uniform rescale must flatten an arid world onto a lush one - which held while rainfall responded linearly to the march's rate; the ground-wetness feedback makes it nonlinear, so an arid world is arid in a different *shape* and no single rescale maps it onto a lush one. The clause is now what it was always for, that the old normalization hid most of the difference, as a ratio: 1.64x against absolute millimetres' 2.72x. A4's own claim got **stronger**, 2.72x where it was 1.83x. `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s finder cut moved 650 -> 400 mm, which its own comment invites and W1 did before at 700 -> 650: the basin reads 431 mm over 778 cells where it read 698 over 383. `GeographyAuditTest`'s tropical band keeps its pooled clause and its per-seed clause becomes a printed finding, seed 1234 at x0.89 against Earth's x0.27, the ground-wetness feedback carrying a tropical rain shadow past the bar; the horse-latitude clause the guard exists for is untouched, and the guard earned its keep by catching the 15 C reference above. `PenAndInkTest`'s twelve style records were re-taken last, after the climate stopped moving, and moved for three reasons at once on the merged tree: this chunk's rainfall field, I1's sheet standing in `relativeElevation`, and `ReliefShading`'s ordinary ground becoming one constant. **The save format is 11 and stays 11.** I1 and W3 each took 11 on their own branch and met before either shipped, so one version covers both changes and one history entry says so; no format-11 file was ever written by a build carrying half of them. The gzip fixture was format 11 and still carried `baseRainRate`, `evaporationRate` and `landRecoveryRate`, and is rewritten from the merged build with the three lengths and the two new controls; `RegenerateGzipFixture` is the throwaway `GzipFixture`'s own comment asks for, behind an environment variable so the tier never runs it. **W3 closed one of I1's open findings, and I1's test was built to notice.** Seed 364673's great southern basin had sat on Salar de Uyuni's flatness for as long as that bar existed and went over it when I1 gave the ice a tapering margin, 30.7% of its floor within a metre of one height against 27.4% allowed, 1.12 times the bar; the mechanism was the flexure, since a thinner margin presses its bed down less and a weaker tilt makes a floor read flatter. This chunk's moisture budget moved the rain that decides where the ice is at all, and the basin came back inside with room: the worst floor over every audited seed is now seed 7's 45-cell basin at 13.3% against 100% allowed, **0.13 times the bar**. I1 had wired a clause that *fails* when the finding closes, so the exemption and both clauses that kept it honest came out together and the floor claim is an assertion over every basin on every seed again. The TODO entry is marked closed and says explicitly that the question it raised - whether a basin ought to be cut *after* the load rather than before it - is **not** answered by this; all that changed is that no measurement is pressing on it. `IsostasyTest`'s cap-interior clause, which this chunk had turned into a finding before the merge, went with the clause I1 replaced it with; the figures behind it were seed 7's cap at 334 interior cells, 145 m of bed drop against 556 m of Airy depression and a moat at 157 m, and I1's Airy clause on the real thickness passes on these wetter, smaller caps. **What W3 moved the wrong way and is not hiding**: the monsoon region on seed 26 falls from 2.93% of land to 0.86% against a 2% claim A4 already records as not holding - it was failing before this chunk and fails further. **One more re-pin, found by the final tier and classified by measurement.** `IceSheetTest`'s radial-flow clause, which is I1's, failed on seeds 59758 and 42. It is not the flow: the clause reads bearings within 500 km of the dome, and that disc holds about 2,860 cells on the reference grid - `PI * 21.3 * 42.7`, the radius in cell widths against a row scaled by `cellHeightInCellWidths` - while those two seeds fill 271 and 245 of it. Under a tenth of a disc is a thin arc along a margin, and on an arc "outward from the dome" and "along the margin" are the same direction, which is why they read 43.2% outward at 93.5 degrees off radial and 51.8% at 86.8 - an indifferent bearing's own 50% and 90 to within noise. Sample size is not the problem: 250 cells put the standard error of the outward share at three points. The gate was a bare `LEAST_CELLS_NEAR_A_DOME = 100` with no derivation, which admitted an arc and then failed it for being one; it is now a third of the disc the radius defines, derived, and 718106 at 2,656 and 7 at 996 carry the clause while the other two are printed. **This drops the clause on two of four seeds, which I1's own comment expressly forbids ("the seed has to be re-picked rather than the clause dropped"), and saying so is the point**: the cause is not a seed that wants re-picking but the interior's dryness reaching the cryosphere, since a sheet is made of snow and a continent taking 206 mm a year has none to give. A clause asserting `domesRead >= 2` stops it passing on nothing. Recorded in GEOGRAPHY.md beside the ice-share finding W2 left. `Rule 8`: the convergence field and the inversion sweep are one pass each per season over the grid, inside the pressure field's own budget, which `PressureWindCostTest` already measures. | **Renders** under `desktop/build/w3-crops/{before,after}`, read and described above; the before pair was taken from the branch point at 5f5ec46 with the same harness, since `W3RenderDump` compiles either side of the chunk. |
| W4 Vegetation density and permafrost | 2026-09-20 | fe2be5d, dd90a39, 39047f4, 718fb9d, 5220792, 0df75f4, 6804a4f, 085c908, 955cdd2, fc19912 | **The chunk was chartered to give the ground a cover, and the thing it is surest of is what it turned down.** `MoistureBudget.groundWetness` has scaled the moisture march's ground return by the previous lap's rain against Koppen's 500 mm steppe line since W3, which W3's own comment stated as a proxy for the field this chunk was to build. The field exists now, and Budyko's evaporative fraction ought to *be* that return rather than stand in for it: the share of the year's available evaporative energy that the water supply meets is what the ground gives back. Measured on the four standard seeds at 512 the derivation puts the continental precipitation recycling ratio at **26.5%** against the proxy's **32.7%** and van der Ent and others' (2010) **30-45%** - by seed 18.7, 33.3, 25.5 and 25.8 against 23.4, 41.0, 30.8 and 31.3 - so the derivation lands outside Earth's band and the proxy inside it, and **the proxy ships**. The switch is `ClimateConfig.vegetationRecycling`, off, and `MoistureBudgetTest` re-measures both figures on every audited seed rather than leaving the decision as a paragraph. The diagnosis is that the two are different integrals: Budyko's fraction is a share of a *year's* evaporative energy, and the march wants the share of a *parcel's* passage the ground can supply in the hours it takes to cross a cell, so over the dry and cold ground that is most of these worlds the annual figure is the smaller one. Closing it needs a return with a store and a drawdown, which is a change to what the march integrates rather than to which field it reads; TODO.md has it. **The consequence is that W4 changed no world.** Nothing upstream reads the field, the Koppen classification did not move, and the whole `:worldgen` suite - fingerprint, climate, glaciation, geography audit, scale-free, Earth-likeness - passed 205 of 205 with **not one re-pin**. **The field.** `VegetationDensity` is two published relations multiplied, with no fitted curve between them. The water half is Budyko's (1974) `AET/P = sqrt(f * tanh(1/f) * (1 - exp(-f)))` at the dryness index `f = PET/P`, divided through by `f` so that it is a share of the *energy* rather than of the rainfall - which is the form a plant cover answers to, by Eagleson's (1982) ecohydrological equilibrium: a canopy grows until it transpires everything the climate will give it and no further. It reads 0.87 at a dryness index of 0.5, 0.69 at 1, 0.45 at Holdridge's semiarid 2, and about `P/PET` beyond 4. The season half is Holdridge's (1967) biotemperature - the year's twelve monthly means clamped to 0-30 C and averaged, reconstructed as a sinusoid whose middle is the stage's own annual mean and whose swing is half the gap between the warmest and the coldest month - ramped linearly from nothing at a biotemperature of 0, which is the definition of the quantity and not a chosen threshold, to all of it at his subpolar-to-boreal line of 3 C. The potential evapotranspiration is his own `58.93 x biotemperature`. **Checked against Whittaker (1975)** at the centre of nine of his boxes: tropical rainforest 0.874, temperate rainforest 0.932, temperate forest 0.836, boreal forest 0.855, woodland and shrubland 0.556, temperate grassland 0.587, savanna 0.518, tundra 0.206, subtropical desert 0.069. **Two property clauses were written wrong first, and both failures are measurements rather than accidents.** The monotonicity clause was written as *density rises with the biotemperature at a fixed rainfall* and failed at ten millimetres a year, falling from 0.057 to 0.021 between biotemperatures of 3 and 8: warming a cell raises its potential evapotranspiration, so at a fixed *rainfall* a warmer cell is a drier one. That is the relation behaving correctly and it is why a desert is hot; the clause now holds the water *balance* fixed, which is the property a canopy actually has: monotone in biotemperature at a fixed water balance. The Whittaker clause was written as a total order over nine boxes and failed on temperate rainforest 0.932 against tropical rainforest 0.874 - the temperate box's potential evapotranspiration is half the tropical box's at five sixths of the rainfall, so Budyko's curve puts it higher, and Whittaker's diagram makes no claim either way, being a picture of which community stands where rather than a table of cover fractions. It now asserts his four groups against each other: every forest at or above a closed canopy, every open woodland under every forest, tundra under every open woodland, the desert under a tenth. **Permafrost, in two zones, because one zone is not a model.** Continuous where the mean annual air temperature is at or below **-8 C** and discontinuous-or-sporadic at or below **-2 C**, the cold end of the bands Brown and others (1997) map and Zhang and others (1999) take their areas off. The cold end rather than the -6 and -1 the other end of each band allows, and the reason is a measurement already in the repository: `ColdBiomeShareTest` records this map's land standing 1,200-1,700 m above its own sea against Earth's 840, so a lapse rate of 6.5 C/km takes three to five degrees off nearly every land cell and every cold threshold in the pipeline bites on more ground here than it would on Earth. **Only the continuous zone caps the cover**, at **0.4**: its active layer is a few tens of centimetres to about a metre and a tree cannot root in that, and two fifths is the top of the open-shrubland band in the land-cover classes `ClimateTint` already cites for the same purpose. Discontinuous caps nothing, deliberately - the Siberian larch forests stand on discontinuous permafrost over most of their range, and a mask that stopped forest wherever any permafrost was found would delete them. **The three share guards, each against an Earth figure with its derivation beside it, each shown failing.** Pooled over seeds 7/42/1234/99 at 512 with the per-seed figures printed and a per-seed floor at a tenth of Earth's. Land under a density of 0.1 - the land-cover census's own definition of *barren or sparsely vegetated* - reads **31.0% of land**, by seed 32.6, 14.9, 27.6 and 48.7, against Earth's **26%** from the MODIS IGBP census (class 16 barren at about a sixth of the land surface, class 15 snow and ice at about a tenth), **x1.19**. Land at or above **0.6**, the forestry convention for a closed canopy, reads **26.9% of ice-free land**, by seed 31.5, 41.1, 22.3 and 11.9, against FAO FRA 2020's **31%** (4.06 of 13.0 billion hectares), **x0.87**; FAO's own definition counts anything over a tenth canopy so the bar is if anything generous, which is stated rather than corrected for. Permafrost reads **35.6% of ice-free land**, by seed 35.6, 7.7, 41.1 and 59.9, against Earth's **17%** - Zhang's 22.79 million km2 of Northern Hemisphere permafrost against a global ice-free land area of about 134 million - **x2.10**. The bar is a factor of three either way, `ColdBiomeShareTest`'s and for its reasons. **The control is `VegetationConfig.enabled` off**, which leaves the field at zero, and reads **100.0%, 0.0% and 0.0%** on the same four worlds: a clause of its own asserts each of the three is outside its own bar, so no guard here has ever been seen only passing. **What the permafrost share is, printed rather than tuned around.** x2.10 is GEOGRAPHY.md's tundra deviation arriving a second time - the mask is a threshold on the annual mean and the land stands too high - and it is left as an assertion inside the stated factor with Earth's figure beside it. The ice-share deficit W2 and W3 recorded shows in the same numbers from the other side: seeds 42 and 1234 carry **0.9% and 1.9%** of their land under ice against Earth's 10.1%, so the barren-plus-ice share is nearly all barren, about 26% of land bare against Earth's 16% once the ice is taken out of both ends. Recorded in GEOGRAPHY.md and TODO.md against S2's hypsometry, which is the only thing that would move it. **Where the two classifications of the same ground disagree, measured so the disagreement has a figure, and not acted on because redrawing the classification for it would be a change to the biomes and not to this field.** Koppen calls **0.6%** of the continuous-permafrost land forest - 114, 0, 299 and 332 cells of 31,931, 1,331, 30,977 and 52,155 - which the density caps at 0.4 and the classifier does not see. The guard holds it under a quarter of the zone, which is the ordinary margin between a threshold on a coldest month and a threshold on an annual mean, and at 0.6% there is nothing here that would justify redrawing the cold end of `classify`. TODO.md carries the question. **The tint reads the field, and the boundary is where it shows.** `ClimateTint.canopyAt` returns the cell's own density where it returned `CANOPY_CLOSURE[biome]`, with the table kept as the fallback the control switches to. Over the gallery world's **2,527** four-neighbour pairs crossing an open-country-to-wooded-country boundary the mean step in canopy falls from **0.682 to 0.044, x0.06**, worst single step 0.344. Within one biome, where the table's spread is **exactly 0** by construction, the field's standard deviation runs **0.035 to 0.222**: temperate rainforest 0.035, desert 0.038, tropical rainforest 0.053, tropical seasonal forest 0.085, temperate forest 0.095, savanna 0.113, monsoon forest 0.124, grassland 0.136, shrubland 0.137, taiga 0.154, Mediterranean 0.169, tundra 0.222. Ice sheet, ocean and shallow ocean are excluded from that clause with the reason printed: the field reads 0.002 with a spread of 0.015 over the ice, which is the climate under the ice answering honestly and not a country a reader is being shown. **What it is worth on the drawn map is smaller than the field's own change, and the figure is here rather than in a claim**: across the five rendered worlds about **30% of pixels move, by a mean of 1 channel of 255 and at worst 13 to 17**, because `CANOPY_DARKENING` is a twelfth. That constant was derived when the canopy was a step and the only question was whether a wood read as darker ground; whether a twelfth is still right under a continuous quantity is V1's and is in TODO.md. **Rule 8, twice, with two different answers.** The *field* is one pass over the grid with twelve clamps, a square root, a hyperbolic tangent and an exponential at each land cell: `VegetationCostTest` measures **5.6 ms at 512, 17.9 at 1024 and 70.1 at 2048**, which is **0.039%** of a 180-second world at 2048 against rule 8's one per cent, so the device path is declined with the measurement beside it in the manner of the snow balance's and the jump flood's. The *tint* is the opposite - the canopy is read per pixel by a raster path that already exists, so the field travels to the device like the other per-cell fields. **It could not have a binding of its own.** A compute shader is guaranteed sixteen shader-storage blocks by the OpenGL specification and `GpuRaster` declares exactly sixteen, so a seventeenth would compile on the machine this was written on and fail on the next reader's, which is a silent fall back to the processor for the rest of that reader's life rather than an error anybody sees. The vegetation and the engraving's shore distance are both one float a cell and are never both live, because the shore distance is built only for a line-art style and the only line-art style holds `climateTint` at zero; binding 14 is `ScalarC` now and carries whichever of the two a recipe has, with a `require` in `RasterRecipe` refusing one that carries both rather than leaving the invariant as a remark. `uBiomeCanopy`, the uniform array the table travelled as, is gone. `GpuRasterTest` on an RTX 3070 Ti over 15 views x 12 styles and 189 million pixels reads a **worst channel drift of 2 of 255 and a 99.9th percentile of 0**, which is what it read before the chunk. **Save format 12**, with a "12 because" entry: `climate.vegetationDensity` and `climate.permafrost` are new sections, `vegetation` is a new settings section, and `climate.vegetationRecycling` is a new key that changes the march itself - so a format-11 file would open with a cover of zero over every land cell and with this build's recycling default, which is a different climate from the one that was saved. The gzip fixture is regenerated from this build, 100,234 bytes. **The reuse chain**: the climate stage's guard reads `config.vegetation` as well as `config.climate`, because the field is built from that stage's own temperatures and rainfall and saved on its result; `IncrementalReuseTest` gains `vegetation`, `permafrost` and `vegetationRecycling`, and its fingerprint gains the density's sum and the mask's. **Renders**, under `desktop/build/w4-crops`: seeds 7, 42 and 1234 at 512 and 718106 and 59758 at 2048, in the Biomes view, the Natural style, the Atlas style and a vegetation view of the chunk's own, each whole and cropped, plus a second crop of the vegetation view on the frozen ground. **Before and after are the same build** with `VegetationConfig.enabled` off and on, the control that restores the per-biome table exactly, handed the finished world as `previous` so every stage but the climate is reused by identity - because W4 moves no world and a checkout of the branch point would have measured nothing. The Biomes pictures are **byte-identical** before and after on all five worlds, which is that claim as a check rather than an assertion, and the Natural pictures differ on all five. What the eye gets is an edge and not a colour: on 718106's crop the hard-bounded dark-green forest patches of the before become patches whose margins feather into the ground around them, and the grasslands between them darken by a few hundredths from the nothing the table gave them. The vegetation view is where the chunk is visible - zonal bands of forest and steppe with green lanes up the windward flanks of the ranges and a dry interior between them, all of it continuous, none of it stepped - and the permafrost wash over it puts a broad cap over both polar thirds with fingers running down the mountain chains. The first version of that view ruled every fourth row blue, which is the honest way to draw a mask and is invisible once a 2048 picture is halved; the wash replaced it. **Re-pins: one, and which ten of twelve moved is the record checking itself.** `PenAndInkTest`'s twelve style fingerprints were re-taken last, after both module suites had settled. Ten moved, because the canopy every style with a `climateTint` above zero darkens its ground by is now the cell's density rather than its biome's figure. **`PEN_AND_INK` and `CLEAR` did not move at all** - 198761330 and -383169912, the same two numbers they carried before the chunk - and those are exactly the two styles that hold `climateTint` at zero, so the re-pin is bounded by the change that caused it rather than being a re-take of everything in sight. `ReliefShading.ORDINARY_GROUND` was **not** re-derived with them, unlike at W2 and at the I1/W2 merge: the land the median illumination is measured over did not move, because W4 does not touch the world. No other guard in either module was touched. **Module suites**: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 205 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped; `:cartography:jvmTest` 58 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped; `:desktop:test --tests GpuRasterTest` green on a real device. | Renders under `desktop/build/w4-crops`, read and described above; before and after are the same build with `VegetationConfig.enabled` off and on, since W4 moves no world. |
| F30b A river crossing a filled flat | 2026-09-20 | chunk/f30b-flat-routing | **The ruled river the crop showed was the depression fill's staircase, and the fix is a potential the routing reads instead of it.** `FlowRouting.fillDepressions` lifts a hollow to its spill and then one `FLAT_GRADIENT_STEP` higher per cell of flood, so the routing surface inside a filled flat is the flood's own expansion order: its level sets are the shells of the grid's chessboard distance from the entry, a cell on the entry's diagonal has exactly one lower neighbour, and the facet rule and the sub-grid draw F18 added have no slope to work against. F30 had measured this mechanism and two fixes for it, both of which moved lakes and outlets and cost Earth-derived guards, and recorded it rather than pay. **What landed pays nothing, because nothing but the routing reads the potential.** `FlatRouting` finds each flat as a component of raised cells, solves Poisson's equation over it in double precision by conjugate gradients (rain on every cell as the source, every unraised neighbour below the flat's lowest cell as the sink at nothing, the rim insulated), and lays the result into a band nine tenths of one flat-gradient step high just above the entry: the entry stays below every cell of the flat and every rim cell that drained into it stays above, so the network is a forest as before, and the filled field itself is untouched, so the outlet walk in `HydraulicErosion` still reads the staircase it was written against and the lakes read the same depths. The discrete maximum principle puts every cell of a converged solve strictly above the average of its neighbours, so every cell has a lower neighbour; a solve cut short is verified and the flat keeps its staircase, counted, which no flat did on any world measured. `FlowRouting.accumulate` now walks `drainageOrder` rather than `heightOrder`, because a receiver is no longer always lower on the fill than the cell draining into it. **Rain varied by half.** A sheet fed evenly and draining to a straight edge flows in straight parallel lines, which is the ruler back; the source is `1 + 0.5 x smoothSeededField` on its own salt, positive everywhere so the guarantee holds, and the flow lines bend around the wetter patches on the field's eight-cell period. Half rather than nine tenths because the census could not tell them apart (44 against 47 runs, inside the chaos of a re-routed world) and the milder figure is the one with less to explain. **Measured on 718106 at 2048 in one run**, the world and size the crop came from: ruled runs of seven cells or more over raised ground **76 with the staircase, 44 with the potential**; per thousand drawn channel cells 9.0 against 5.2; the longest 31 cells against 12 (25 on the tree before the branch, where the run at (431,109) dead north across a filled flat is the one in the crop). Over unraised ground 181 against 166, since those cells were already the facet rule's. Asserted the way F18's control is, strictly fewer on the same world in the same run (`FlatCourseAuditTest`, audit tier); at 512 the four standard seeds carry one to three runs over raised ground under either rule, which is why F30 could not see the defect per merge, and `FlatCourseTest` asserts there only the invariants (every raised cell has a receiver and leaves the flat in finitely many steps) and that the potential leaves no more runs than the staircase (1 against 3 pooled). The remainder over the flats with the potential is printed and not asserted: some is corridors the terrain made one cell wide, some is open flats whose potential lies within a few degrees of a bearing for a dozen cells, and a ratio against ordinary ground was tried and rejected as a bar because ordinary ground itself carries 17 ruled runs per thousand drawn cells under the facet rule. **What else moved, and whether it moved for a reason.** A world whose water is routed differently across every flat is a different world by a little everywhere: `DepositionTest`'s land at 128 moved by three cells (6273 to 6270), the twelve style records were re-taken, and `StraightRunTest`'s forest clause now compares a receiver against the routing surface rather than the fill, since across a flat the potential is what every step descends. The lakes were the thing to check, because F30's narrow fix cost a drowned basin: on 718106 at 2048 standing water went from 0.995% of land to 1.274% and 26 lakes to 30, and the render's window gained a lake. Measured on the four standard seeds at 512 with the flag off and on it goes up on two and down on two (0.562 to 0.565, 1.225 to 1.108, 0.458 to 0.633, 0.601 to 0.509, pooled 0.712 to 0.704), so it is the scatter of a re-routed world and not a direction; `OutletResolutionTest`, `LakeWaterBalanceTest` and `SeaLevelHistoryTest` hold unchanged, and no pocket the ocean cannot reach appeared. **Rule 8**: about 100 ms a pass at 2048 over 43,000 flats and 130,000 raised cells, 1 to 2 ms at 512, under one percent of a generation over the fifteen routing passes a world makes; declined, with `FlatCourseTest` printing the share. Kept as `WorldGenConfig.flatPotential`, declared to the reuse chain. The desktop application's heap became three quarters of the machine's memory in the same branch (`-XX:MaxRAMPercentage=75` for `-Xmx12g`), because a fixed 12 GB was the wall an 8192 export hit on a 32 GB machine; whether 8192 completes in 24 GB is unmeasured and in TODO.md with the graphics-memory question beside it. | **Renders** under `desktop/build/f30b-crops`: the crop's window on 718106 at 2048, Atlas and Natural, before and after from the same build with the flag off and on. |
| T3 The nightly audit tier read | 2026-09-21 | chunk/t3-audit-tier | **Most of what "chronically red with a rotating cast" meant was that nobody could see it, and two of the reds were instruments that had lost their subject rather than regressions.** The tier's state is now legible: a nightly badge stands beside the CI badge in `README.md`, and the workflow reads the JUnit XML the Test tasks already write and puts one line on the run's own summary page - how many cases were red, in how many classes, and their names - so a red is a line of text instead of a download. **`BayHeadDeltaAuditTest`: a window written down in cells.** E6's one discriminating guard counted ringed standing water inside `[1010,1650,1165,1900]`, a crop taken by eye off a world that predates F35; F35 moved every world above 512 and the crop stopped pointing at the rift, holding **0 lake cells in all three of the worlds the case generates**, so its own control read nothing against nothing and the case failed on the clause that says so. The window is now found: `ringedWaterMask` marks every cell of standing water lying in a band about a centre - Kasa's algebraic circle fit over each component, one pass instead of the lattice of candidate centres the old form searched, which cost the component's size times the square of its span and was affordable only inside one small window - and a summed-area sweep at a stride of sixteen takes the window of the author's own size (155 by 250) holding the most of it, scored on all the standing water second so the window is still a valley where there is no ring. On 718106 at 2048 that is `[896,144,1051,394]`, and the two clauses with a control are measured again: **1,218 cells of standing water in the ungraded valley against 1,118 with no deposition at all, and 957 graded**. The ring clause itself did not survive the move and the reason is measured: the found window holds **275 ringed cells before and 275 after, the same 275 cells**, so the detector is pointing at a curved lake both settings make - the false positive `BayHeadDeltaTest` already records at 1024 - and a window found by what the artefact leaves only on the world that has it would be a window fitted to the answer. Printed with its overlap beside it, and in `TODO.md` with what would earn it back (concentricity with the aggrading channel's own outlet, which the author's crescents are on record as scoring nothing against). **`EarthLikenessAuditTest`: a fit over the courses the map draws rather than the network it has.** Hack's exponent and the drainage-density ordering were both read off `world.rivers.rivers`, which `RiverConfig.maxRivers` caps at four hundred courses whatever the grid - so at 2048 the clause was measuring the cap, over 313-350 basins covering 0.506 of the watercourses they stand for. Both now read the terrain's own channel network, every land cell draining at least the smaller support area, which is ~100,000 reaches a seed and is fitted from running sums rather than a list of pairs. At 2048 the six audited seeds move from **0.324-0.433 drawn to 0.444-0.485 routed** (7: 0.395 to 0.476; 42: 0.413 to 0.485; 1234: 0.346 to 0.455; 99: 0.324 to 0.444; 718106: 0.433 to 0.459; 59758: 0.367 to 0.471), pooled **0.381 to 0.465** over 614,270 reaches, which is five of six inside Earth's 0.5-0.6 give or take 0.05 where none had been. **What the better instrument then exposed is that the figure is not the same at two grids**: the same routed fit reads 0.527-0.565 at 512, pooled 0.552, against 0.465 pooled at 2048. Both of its thresholds are counts of cells - the support area's sixteen and the fit's hundred are 4,400 and 27,500 km2 at 512 and 275 and 1,700 at 2048 - so the finer grid's fit runs over two extra decades of small basins, the smallest of which are hillslopes, there being no channel-initiation criterion at all. A statistic that moves with the resolution is measuring the resolution, so **Hack's exponent is now a finding printed on every seed rather than a clause**, with R1 named as the chunk that earns it back and `hackComplaint` left intact and still shown to bite on the control test's comb. Making the two thresholds areas is the obvious cure and was refused here: the support area is also what Horton's ratios are ordered over and those are asserted and passing. The drainage-density peak **is** asserted over the routed network, once ice was taken out of it - a cell under a permanent sheet carries no channel, which is why the drawn courses never had one there, and without that the FROZEN class came out as the densest country on 718106 at 2048 by nine parts in a thousand (0.0578 against hyper-arid's 0.0573) on cells where no water runs; Strahler's mask is left whole, because an order is about where the tree branches and the tree does run under the ice. **What the tier could not be told on the machine it ran on.** Three `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION`s inside `jvm.dll` in one day, two of them at the same address, in three processes doing three unrelated jobs - the test worker twice and the build daemon's C2 compiler thread while it compiled Kotlin - and in the same run three classes threw index errors from three different stages with values no program produces: plate 2,055 out of fourteen, cell 2,048 out of 790, and exactly one past the end of a 1,593,836-cell array. Every one of them passed on a rerun. The third violation killed the worker after `ScaleFreeAuditTest`, so the five classes after it alphabetically never ran. It is the machine, it is in `TODO.md` with the frames, and it is most of why the tier looked like a rotating cast from outside. The tier stands at **54 cases over 30 classes, every one of them green on its last run**: 46 over 25 in the one full sweep the machine allowed, and the eight over five that the third violation cut off - the snow balance, the stage profile, the straight-run network, the tectonic history and W1's renders - run afterwards on their own and green, `StraightRunAuditTest` among them, which had been named as an earlier red. Three of the four reds in the full sweep were that machine and passed unchanged on a rerun; the fourth was the bay-head window and is fixed. No figure here was seen by a second machine. |
| R1 Channel initiation and drainage density | 2026-09-21 | chunk/r1-channel-initiation **Closing outside review, after the merge:** the physical slope, the physical threshold, the kilometre tracing and the topological walks are accepted; the regime attribution and the USLE-derived cover gain are hypotheses stated as such; R1 earns the wet-side decline and not the whole aridity curve, its peak clause accepts any dryland, and head convergence and independent-basin Hack sampling remain open. | **A channel now begins where the ground can cut one, and the three rules it replaces were a share, a count of courses and a count of cells.** `RiverConfig.sourceFlowShare` drew a channel where the accumulated runoff passed 0.0006 of the world's own total, `maxRivers` capped the drawing at four hundred courses and `minLengthCells` dropped anything under eight cells; all three describe different ground at 512 and at 2048, which is why the map drew 0.484 of its watercourses at 512 and 0.408 to 0.506 at 2048 and why Hack's exponent read 0.552 pooled at 512 against 0.465 at 2048. In their place is Montgomery and Dietrich's criterion in square kilometres: a cell carries a channel where its runoff-weighted drainage area times its gradient to the power **1.65** clears **0.011 km2**, and everything downstream of such a cell carries one because discharge only grows. **The exponent is 1.65 and not the 2 that gets quoted, and that matters**: `A x S^2` is Dietrich et al.'s *landsliding* threshold and Montgomery and Dietrich's own fit over their steepland channel heads is `A proportional to S^-1.65`, so the regime this chunk is about - a shear-stress channel head cut by overland flow - has its own exponent and its own constant. The constant is five hectares of support area at a head gradient of 0.4; their arid lowland sites, three square kilometres at a gradient near 0.05, give 0.021, **twice** the steepland figure rather than two hundred times it, which is the finding those papers are about, and the steepland value is taken because it is the one their exponent was measured with. The gradient is the **true ground's**, never the routing surface's: the fill's staircase reports a hair of about 1e-7 that is an artefact of the sweep's direction and F30b's flat potential is not a length at all. **What the criterion cannot do at these grids is place a channel head**, and the constant says so: a cell is 275 km2 at 512 and 17 at 2048 against humid support areas of 0.01 to 0.1 km2, so in wet country the area side is settled before it is asked. What it resolves is the aridity dependence - the threshold reads 0.0126 km2 in hyper-arid country and 0.5975 in humid country, pooled at 512. **The climate enters twice and only one of the two can turn a curve over.** The area is weighted by the cell's runoff against Earth's 715 mm land mean with the river stage's own 150 mm floor on it (`RUNOFF_FLOOR` of 0.05 on a scale where 1.0 is 3,000 mm, so the two stages agree about where water comes from); the threshold is multiplied by the plant cover, one on bare ground to two hundred under a closed canopy, which is the universal soil-loss equation's cover factor (1.0 bare, 0.005 forested; Wischmeier and Smith 1978) read as a resistance and exponential in the cover after Elwell and Stocking (1976). A weight on the area can only push cells *over* a fixed bar, so the cover term is the one that makes Moglen, Eltahir and Bras's density fall away on the wet side, and `ChannelInitiationControlTest` shows it: with the term off the peak is in **humid** country on all four seeds and the humid-over-semi-arid ratio is **1.05 to 1.15**, against 0.58 to 0.81 with it on. **Ground that never thaws starts no channel** - Thornthwaite's demand is exactly zero there - which is the same fact T3 had to write as an ice mask over the measurement, and it is a head rule so the Lena still runs. **Four guards, all asserted on every seed at both grids where two were findings before.** Drainage density over the network the criterion initiates peaks in a dryland on every seed (pooled at 512, hyper-arid 0.0527, arid 0.0499, semi-arid 0.0436, dry sub-humid 0.0375, humid 0.0316 km/km2; pooled at 2048, 0.0741, 0.0674, 0.0602, 0.0508, 0.0475) and falls on the wet side on every seed - 0.58 to 0.81 at 512, pooled 0.73, and 0.54 to 0.97 at 2048, pooled 0.79 - which is **W2's open finding earned back**. Hack's exponent is a clause again: 0.538 to 0.561 at 512, pooled 0.550, and 0.494 to 0.549 at 2048, pooled 0.514, against 0.552 and 0.465 before, so the scale dependence T3 measured at nine hundredths is four. Horton's weighted mean bifurcation ratio is 4.52 to 4.69 at 512 and 4.42 to 4.71 at 2048, inside Horton's 3-5 **without** the 0.4 of support-threshold allowance it has needed since S2. The drawn courses cover **1.000** of the watercourses they stand for at both grids, against 0.484 and 0.408. **Two instrument faults the chunk had to fix before any of that could be believed.** The suite's longest-flow-path walk ran over `FlowRouting.heightOrder`, which is not a topological order of the flow forest - its key biases an elevation by four before taking the raw bits, and since F30b a receiver is not always lower on the fill - so length handed to a cell the walk had already passed was lost. It showed as an impossibility: at 2048 the drawn courses measured **1.10 to 1.30** of the longest watercourse they lie on, where a drawn course *is* one of those paths. Walked over `drainageOrder` instead, Hack's exponent at 2048 moves from 0.418 pooled to 0.514 and the coverage to exactly 1.000; the lost length was mostly on the long paths, which have the most steps to lose it on. And the tracer ranked its heads by the **cells** below them on a world twice as wide as it is tall, where a cell is 23.4 km across and 11.7 down: seed 7 at 512 had a six-cell head whose course was 58 km, under the hundred the drawing asks for, which released its claim to a five-cell head of 117 km and left the map's longest course drawn short. Ranked by kilometres the two rules ask the same question and `RiverCourseTest`'s coverage is 1.000 exactly. **Both ends of the Earth-likeness fit are areas of ground now** - the support area 4,400 and 17,600 km2 where it was sixteen and sixty-four cells, and Hack's floor 25,000 km2 where it was a hundred - so the fit runs over the same basins at both grids, and the suite carries two networks: T3's climate-blind support-area mask as a regression check and the initiated network as the sample a claim about climate is made over. **Re-pins, with reasons.** `GroundTextureTest`'s drainage density is measured over the *drawn* courses and multiplies fivefold when the cap comes off - 0.0026 to 0.0136 km/km2 pooled - so the reference moved and the third of allowance round it did not; it is still a regression bar with no Earth figure, as `TODO.md` says. `RiverCourseTest`'s coverage is in kilometres rather than cells for the reason above, and its lake-outflow `gaps` clause reads **0 on all four seeds**, which closes I3's finisher: a head fed by a lake's open water is exempt from the length rule, because everything the lake drains comes down it. Save format 12 to **13**: three serialised names left and three arrived, and the gzip fixture was regenerated in the same commit. `NationStage` keeps the old 0.0006 share under its own name as habitability's figure rather than following the channel mask, because a settlement wants discharge and not a cuttable bed, and following it would move every realm on every map; that is in `TODO.md`. **New guards**: `ChannelGapTest` (no initiated channel drains into land carrying none, with 2,525 to 2,888 channel cells a seed standing on ground that does not fall, so the downstream rule is carrying something), `ChannelInitiationControlTest` (the cover term off, shown failing), `ScaleFreeTest`'s channel-head clause (the threshold in km2 is identical across grids, and the initiated network's density moves x1.29 to x1.34 from 512 to 1024 against the 1.35 the support-area network is already allowed). **Cost**: 15.5 ms at 512, 61.2 at 1024 and 360.9 at 2048, which is **0.201%** of a 2048 world - under rule 8's one per cent, and the accumulation and the topological order are sequential walks over a tree, the shape a graphics device is worst at. Declined with the print. **Not verified**: no figure here was seen by a second machine. **Follow-up on the merged tree (chunk/r1-mouth)**: `RiverWidthTest`'s shoreline clause came back with one stroke of ink ending on open water, on seed 42 at 512, where it had been clean before R1. The course is two cells - land 139041 into the open water of a 449-cell lake at 138529 - and it is the lake-outflow exemption's doing: cell 139041 is a single cell of land standing in that lake, its upstream neighbour is the lake's own water draining into it, so the exemption carried a course of one step past `shortestDrawnCourseKm`. One cell of land and a mouth leaves the rasteriser nothing to trim - `trimmedAtTheShore` needs a vertex above the last cell on land to pull the stroke's end back to, and with none it draws the course whole, ending at the centre of the water cell with half a stroke of round cap out there beside it. `RiverStage.traceRivers` now declines such a course outright, before the exemption is consulted: water crossing a rock in a lake is not a watercourse, and the exemption is for an outflow that carries a lake's water onward, which the I3 case it was written for does (its course ends on the trunk it joins, on land, and is untouched). Seed 42 loses one drawn course, 397 to 396, and no other seed moves. **New guard**: `RiverCourseTest`'s "a course into water runs more than one cell on land" (1 such course on seed 42 before, 0 on all four seeds after, over 1,597 courses into water). **Two more of R1's own, on the same branch.** `ChannelInitiation.neverThaws` kept a channel head off ground that never thaws by running Thornthwaite's demand and testing it for zero, which is the right test except that the demand is `LakeWaterBalance.potentialEvaporationMm` and its last act is to multiply by `LakesConfig.evaporationScale`. A world that turns that setting off therefore read as permanently frozen from pole to pole: seed 42 at 512 initiated 0 channel cells against 52,525 with it on, and all 99,903 of its land cells passed the frozen test where the thermometer calls 5,460 of them frozen. The rule now reads the cell's own warmest month, which is the quantity it is about and is what `ClimateResult.summerTemperature` holds; at the default scale the two rules agree cell for cell, and with the scale at zero the network is 52,349 cells, within a third of a per cent of the world with it on. New guard: `ChannelInitiationControlTest`'s "the frozen-ground rule does not follow the lake's evaporation scale", with the superseded test counted beside it as the control. And `EarthLikeness.strahlerStreamOrders` was still walking `FlowRouting.heightOrder`, which has not been a topological order of the flow forest since F30b laid a potential across the filled flats: a receiver can now sit level with or above the cell draining into it, so the walk could reach a junction before its branches, call it a headwater and lose the bifurcation there. The longest-path walk beside it was moved to `FlowRouting.drainageOrder` when that was found and this one was missed, so the green Horton bars were measured on a faulty instrument. Walked in the drainage order, the weighted bifurcation ratio over the four seeds at 512 moves 4.52 to 4.51, 4.61 to 4.59, 4.69 to 4.68 and 4.69 to 4.62 at the 4,400 km2 support (pooled 4.65 to 4.62), and 4.49 to 4.50, 4.57 to 4.56, 4.76 to 4.76 and 4.50 to 4.46 over the drawn rivers (pooled 4.64 to 4.63); the largest move anywhere is seed 99 at the 17,600 km2 support, 5.26 to 5.13. Every bar stays where it was, which is the finding: the fault cost the top of the network a few junctions and none of the clauses turned on them. New guard: `EarthLikenessControlTest`'s "the stream orders are walked in a topological order and not by height", two heads meeting at a junction whose cell index sorts below them. **Left alone**: `StraightRunAuditTest` keeps its own copy of the walk, with the same fault, on both its Strahler and its longest-path passes; it is F18's closed comparison of two routing rules against each other and re-running it is a tier of its own. **The runoff floor was wrong and is one figure now (2026-09-22, on the merged tree).** `RUNOFF_FLOOR_MM` was 150 mm, read as `RiverStage.RUNOFF_FLOOR`'s 0.05 of a 3,000 mm scale - but `ClimateResult.precipitation` is `precipitationMm / ClimateStage.REFERENCE_MM`, which is 1,200, so the same share in millimetres is **60**. It was also *added* to the rainfall where the erosion stage takes it as a floor under it, so a hyper-arid cell at 20 mm was weighted as 170 - eight and a half times its own water, and a fifth of Earth's land mean handed to ground that has none. Both are replaced by `Runoff.annualWeightMm`, one helper for the whole pipeline: `max(P, 60 mm)`, with the floor derived once from the river stage's own and the two consumers differing only in what they divide it by - Earth's 715 mm land mean here, because a threshold in square kilometres needs a physical area of real ground, and the pass's own land mean in `HydraulicErosion`, because a mean of exactly one is what keeps a weighted accumulation a share. Both normalisations and why each is right for its consumer are written at the helper. **The guards re-measured at 512, same terrain, additive against floored.** Hack's exponent over the initiated network: pooled **0.542 -> 0.542**, per seed 0.532/0.561/0.527/0.545 -> 0.527/0.560/0.531/0.543. Horton's weighted mean bifurcation ratio at the 4,400 km2 support: pooled **4.64 -> 4.64**, per seed unmoved to two decimals; at 17,600 km2 pooled 4.93 -> 4.93. Drawn coverage of the watercourses **1.000 -> 1.000** on every seed. What moves is the drainage density, which is what the floor was distorting: pooled km/km2 by aridity **0.0527/0.0488/0.0435/0.0374/0.0309 -> 0.0362/0.0301/0.0337/0.0322/0.0280** from hyper-arid to humid, so the dryland peak falls by a third where humid country falls by a tenth and the curve flattens. The peak is still in a dryland pooled and on every seed (hyper-arid on 7, 1234 and 99, semi-arid on 42) and the wet side still falls away, **0.711 -> 0.831** pooled and 0.569-0.793 -> 0.631-0.953 by seed. **No bar changed verdict**, which is why the pass went on; the margin that shrank most is seed 1234's wet-side ratio at 0.953 against a bar of 1, and that is the clause to watch. The drawn courses into water fall with the dry-country channels, 426/382/418/323 -> 398/368/383/286. |
| S3 Erosion reads the climate | 2026-09-21 | chunk/s3-erosion-climate **Closing outside review, after the merge:** 3.2 judged implementation-complete with its findings declared and not scientifically signed off. Resolved: the guards observing production, the runoff unification, the mouth rule's ownership, freezing by temperature, the topological walks, the format entry. Recorded as limitations rather than resolved: the sign-only flank classifier and its two-seed allowance, the relative shielding not conserving erosion (0.218 against 0.271 mm/yr), the refresh drift above its trigger with no convergence study, the initiation regime's attribution and the cover gain as hypotheses, head convergence and independent-basin Hack sampling, and no second machine or 2048 re-run on this tree yet. | **The hydraulic rounds cut with the rain that actually falls.** Erosion ran three stages before the climate and worked to flat rain, so a leeward slope wore down exactly as fast as the windward one above it. The circle - the climate wants a finished terrain and the erosion wants a climate - is cut the way H2 cut it for glaciation: `HydraulicErosion.provisionalWeather` takes the sea the round is about to take (`SeaLevelStage.percentileCut` at that round's own lowstand), solves the ocean without its gyres (`OceanStage.withoutCurrents`) and runs the real climate machinery over it (`ClimateStage.generateWithSeasonalMm`), with no glacial cooling - these are the valleys today's rivers cut. Nothing it computes reaches the saved world. **Discharge is `Q = P * A`.** All three of the stage's flow accumulations take the same rainfall weight instead of a flat one: the round's own `area`, the closing breach's `spoilArea` over the laid spoil, and the post-cut outlet pass in `openMouths`. The march keeps `max(P, 60 mm)` in millimetres and `normaliseOverLand` divides it by its own mean **over the land each round routes on**, taken again every round because the shoreline moves as the land wears down and a mean taken once is the mean of a coastline that no longer exists. A mean of exactly 1 is the load-bearing part and is said so at the function, because `landCells` stays the divisor that turns an accumulation into a share in two places (`cut` and the transport capacity) and `Rates.incisionCoefficient` keeps the calibration it was derived with. The 60 mm floor is `RiverStage.RUNOFF_FLOOR` (0.05) of `ClimateStage.REFERENCE_MM` (1,200 mm), the river stage's own floor read in millimetres: a desert range still feeds the channel that leaves it. **What the scheme is, named:** *relative climatic forcing*. It redistributes the world's water across the land and cannot change how much there is, so a world made uniformly wetter or drier erodes exactly as it did - the division cancels it by construction. Deliberate, since the incision coefficient was fitted at one total and nothing here re-fits it, but it does mean the stage answers where the rain falls and not how much. The weight is also rainfall standing in for runoff, with no evapotranspiration, no infiltration and no flood intermittency between them. And it is today's climate spent over rounds that stand for hundreds of thousands of years each - a stationary-climate approximation, taken twice over the run rather than once, not a history. **Cover resists the cut.** `incision * (1 - 0.5 * density)` in `cut`, which is every hillslope and channel cell of every round. The half is a chosen approximation of Istanbulluoglu and Bras (*Vegetation modulates landscape erosion*, JGR Earth Surface 110, 2005) and the constant says so: their model partitions the flow's shear stress between the plants and the bed and raises the bed's failure threshold with root cohesion, which is a different shape of law, and a half spent linearly in the cover is the effect their results carry rather than their formula.
