| Fix A The map drawn at the world's true shape | 2026-09-24 | chunk/a-sheet-shape | **Every picture — the pane, the exports on both front ends and the site's figures — is the true-shape sheet: a pixel covers the same ground east-west as north-south.** The world is 12,000 km by 6,000 on a square grid, so a cell is twice as wide as it is tall, and since Fix 2 the ground is round where it is round; drawn a cell to a square pixel it read twice as tall as it is wide. **One geometry**, `SheetGeometry`, derived from `cellWidthKm` and `cellHeightKm` and never from a written two: pixels a cell takes each way, the sheet's size, where a cell lands and which cell a point of the sheet (or a fraction of it, as a label is stored) is over, wrapped east-west; a grid whose cells are square on the ground gets one pixel a cell from the same arithmetic, which is what the later 2:1 grid reuses. **The raster keeps one colour a cell** on both paths and is copied out cell for pixel (`MapImage.sheetBitmap`, a band at a time so no second copy of the sheet is held); the ink — coast, rivers, flow arrows, glyphs, graticule, figures, the scale bar — is placed through the geometry at its own width, pens as shares of the sheet's width. **What the raster decides a cell at a time, audited**: the relief, the normals and the categorical fills carry no direction and are copied as they are; the patterns carried a bearing and a pitch laid out in cells, which copying would have leaned and stretched, so each is now laid out on the sheet and asked at the cell's own pixel of it, on the processor and in the compute shader alike — the realm hatch (45 degrees on the sheet where it would have leaned to 27), the hachures (the ground's fall line, the lattice and the gradient stencil a pitch of the sheet in each axis's cells, four columns and eight rows), the stipple, the lake rulings, the dotted border, and the vignette, whose shore distance is measured in sheet pixels; the isobath is held to its width per sheet pixel. A mark along a column cannot be narrower than the cell, two pixels, and the raster's own coast and border cells are two pixels wide where they run north-south and one where they run east-west (`TODO.md`). **One scale**: the cartouche quotes one figure (`2.9 km per pixel · about 1:11 000 000 at the equator` for a 2048 world) and the printed bar is measured against the sheet's width, 500 km at 170.7 px where the squeezed sheet's arithmetic drew 85.3; `MapSheet` carries the reader's pixels to one of the sheet's, the coast is simplified on the sheet, and the rivers are selected at the sheet's representative fraction, so an export of a 2048 world is at 1:11M where it was 1:22M (the pane's scale is unchanged). **Guards, each shown failing on the tree before**: `TrueShapeSheetTest` draws a disc of land 1,500 km in radius through `MapImage.toBitmap` — 66 by 130 pixels on the squeezed sheet at 256, 130 by 130, 258 by 258 and 514 by 514 at 256, 512 and 1024 in every style after (Pen and ink's vignette 208 by 210 to 592 by 594) — and two rivers at the full pen, 2.9 px across both ways but 17.2 and 34.5 km of ground before, 5.0 and 5.6 px, 29.0 and 32.6 km after; `SheetExpansionTest` holds the biome sheet to its sixteen palette colours (none other; the interpolated control invents 106) and the political sheet to its raster cell for pixel (0 of 524,288 differ), and a square-celled grid to one pixel a cell; `SheetCameraTest` returns the cell under a click at the corners, the centre and across the seam, zoomed and panned, where the square reading lands on another row; `GeneralisationTest` holds the one-figure cartouche and the printed bar. The pane's label names now follow the camera as their pins do. **Figures that moved**: the render records, every style; Pen and ink's aspect error read on the sheet, 25.5 degrees against the comb's 41.8 (20.0 and 47.0 cell for pixel), the tenth percentile of the land slope still at the floor, the seventy-fifth now 0.47 (the ink gain's known failure re-recorded from 0.43); `RiverSelectionTest`'s two-grid control is asked in an 1800-pixel pane, because in the 900-pixel one the radical law's cut on the 1024 world landed within 1% of the 512 world's by coincidence (20.2% apart at 1800). **Memory** at the top size: a 4096 world is an 8192 by 4096 sheet, 128 MB of pixels; on screen the raster (64 MB of heap), the sheet (128 MB of Skia's memory) and Compose's copy (128 MB) are the new picture's peak, beside the picture it replaces until the swap, about 450 MB where the square sheet came to about 320; the JPEG export adds its 128 MB read-back and 96 MB of AWT image. Skia's wasm heap is capped at 2 GB. **Sizes in words**: the README, the site and the export row say a picture of an N world is 2N by N; the data exports keep the grid, one sample a cell, and the sidecar now says so (`gridCellsAcross`, `gridCellsDown`, `samplesPerCell`, `sampleSpacing`). The site's three windows were re-picked on the new contact sheet. |
| Fix 3 The erosion's units | 2026-09-25 | chunk/3-erosion-units | **The incision has one unit: each limit on the cut is spent in the unit it is measured in, the clock's years are the years a round's cut is worth, the outlet notch starts at the basin's lip, and a converged flat potential cannot be refused by the flood's rounding. What it exposes is that the explicit update's cap, not the stream-power law, sets the cut on every drawn channel.** **B-D1, the caps.** `HydraulicErosion.cut` read the drop and the height above the sea on the shoreline-relative field, 6,000 m a unit, and spent them on the height field, 16,000 m a unit: half the drop was spent as 1.33 times it and the shoreline cap let a cell lose 2.67 times its height above the water, and a drop to a sea receiver mixed the land's ruler with the sea's. Measured on the tree before, every round (a mouth is a land cell whose receiver is sea; the drawn network is the cells carrying at least `DRAWN_RIVER`, 0.0006, of the land's water): 55 to 58% of mouth cuts ended below that round's shoreline over the twelve rounds (42 to 67% a round at 2048), every drawn mouth did, a median 10 to 28 m and up to 980 m deep, on seeds 7, 42 and 1234 at 512, 42 at 1024 and 969495 at 2048; the half-the-drop cap set 95.0, 95.5 and 95.3% of the drawn network's cuts at 512, 99.3% at 1024 and 99.5% at 2048. Now the half-drop cap is converted once, in `cut`, the shoreline cap is read off the height field so a cut to it lands on it to the bit, the drop to a sea receiver is to the shoreline in both walks (`fallToReceiver`), and the notch's falling surface below the lip stops at the shoreline inside the rounds. After: no mouth below the shoreline on any of the five worlds, and **the cap sets 100.0% of the drawn network's cuts on all five**: the honest cap is three eighths of the one that was spent, and the explicit update's Courant number, about `0.24 sqrt(A in cells)` on a grid as many cells tall as wide (0.34 on square cells, twice as wide as tall; Q2), is 1.9 where the drawn network starts and 7.5 to 30 on the trunks. No solver was built; the report on the review branch sets out Braun and Willett's implicit update for the maintainer, who has chosen it for the next erosion chunk. Guards, `ErosionUnitsTest`, through `IncisionWatch` (the ordered pass's three terms before the receiver clamp): no mouth ends a round below the shoreline, 22,721 of 39,631 mouth cuts did on seed 42 at 512 before, none after; no cut is asked for more than half its drop in the field's unit, 472,659 were before. `ReceiverClampTest`'s control depended on the defect and is restated: without the clamp the incision now makes no hole, and what the clamp still does is refuse to deepen a basin floor, which without it leaves more channel cells under water, pooled over three seeds. **B-F1, the clock.** The slope's rise and the cut are on different rulers, so `highestLandMetres / reliefSpanMetres` belongs in the coefficient; S2's second pass took it to cancel and labelled a round 126,178.65 years. Step one restores 336,476.4 years with the factor back in the coefficient, the same float at 1,001 sea levels tried, and restates the four uplift rates by three eighths (collision 0.775 to 0.290625 mm a year) and `IsostasyTest`'s synthetic belt with them. It is not bit for bit: a restated rate times the restated clock rounds differently in the last place, and on seed 42 at 512 the eroded height moved at 103,465 cells, a median millimetre, 57 cells past a metre, 36.5 m at most, eight cells changing side of the shoreline; seed 7 five cells past a metre and none. Guard: one production round on a plane of known slope and catchment below the cap removes `K T sqrt(A) S`, 22.30 m of 22.30 m after and 2.667 times the law on the old clock. Step two, the collision rate re-derived from the honest-clock denudation, was measured and not committed: with the uplift off the belts lose 0.074 mm a year (0.069, 0.091, 0.059, 0.075 and 0.074 on seeds 7, 42, 1234, 99 and 718106), which would make the collision rate 0.574 and the Andean, arc and rift rates 0.230, 0.081 and 0.035; that denudation is the cap's and not the law's, so the derivation waits for the implicit solver and `IsostasyTest` records it as a known failure. **B-D3, the notch.** `FlowRouting.spillways` read a basin's exit as the first non-ponded cell its water drains to, which on a shelving margin is a cell of the filled flat raised by less than the pond depth, and the breach walked from there and stopped at once. `Spillways.spill` is now the pour point, the first cell on the outflow's path the fill did not raise, and `Spillways.entry` keeps the old exit; the outflow's power is measured from the lip, over its catchment and the channel below it, and the falling surface is carried back across the margin. Guard (`OutletIncisionTest`): a basin with a 30 m core, an 18 to 20 m shelving margin and 10 m of outflow power, built through production's fill and spill selection and notched on its own, the old exit lying in the margin: the lip lowered 0.00 m before and 9.37 m after. The fill case now asserts the depth it is named for, the fill's depth over the land (`RoundMass.fillDepthOverLandMetres`): 0.074 of the control's pooled at the last round, the largest basin's area 0.239. The three notch clauses Fix 2 recorded pass and are armed. **Review point 2.4, the flat's floor.** `FlatRouting.layInBand` anchored the band at the flat's lowest raised level less the nominal flat-gradient step, but the flood raised that level in float and its rounding can go down; the band is now laid between the highest entry's own height and that level. Guards: a trench 107 cells down one column with its entry at 1.5, where a float's step is 8.39 last places and rounds down, the column-run preconditioner making the solve exact, kept its staircase before and does not after; over every routing pass of seeds 718106 and 59758 at 2048 (33 each) 179 and 102 flats kept the staircase before and none after (`FlatCourseAuditTest`). **What the new terrain moved**, recorded and not loosened, most under the new finding that the cap sets every drawn channel's cut (`CAP_SETS_EVERY_CUT`): the valley notch at 1.68 times the bare ground against its 1.9 pin; the coast's box-counting dimension 1.028 pooled by box and the Earth-likeness suite's 1.036, under Mandelbrot's 1.25 +/- 0.15, with the littoral pass's smoothing gain 0.740 against 0.596; the lowstand's estuaries (seed 7 10 against 31 with the sea held at today's level); the ground texture's highest quarter 68.5 m against 103.8 recorded and a belt's flank 101 m against 113; the rain's dissection contrast on seeds 42 and 1234; the drylands' drainage density on seed 99; and in the map's own guards, the haze at which the relief keeps the lamp's contrast, 0.12 against the declared 0.10, and ordinary ground's median light, 0.9489 against the declared 0.9225, both recorded rather than re-derived because the implicit update moves the relief again. The census at 512 is re-recorded; the raster coast's facing figure rises on all four seeds, 2.56 to 2.83 before and 3.55 to 4.48 after, and seed 7's and 42's isotherms gain a straight run each. Under the per-step finding kept from Fix 2: the coast's projection ratio 1.12 pooled where it was 1.39, still over on seed 1234 at 1.19, and seed 99's steep ground 18.0 m/km down a column against 14.9 along a row. The two B-D1 known failures are not resolved: the cap is still per step and now binds on every drawn channel. Re-recorded with their new figures: the valley notch's depth, 0.0077 of the field against the 0.0148 the tree before S2 cut; the rain-dissection pin, now under on seeds 7, 1234 and 99; the rainfall calibration, the wet flank (1.12 under 1.49), the cold-current coast, the tropics' desert, the glacial lakes, the ice sheet's edge and thickness, the foreland, the shelf's jump flood, the recycling ratio and the realm share; armed: the ice bed under Airy's share, the notch's clauses, seed 298405's coast by ruler and the flat potential's cost, which at 1.00% of a generation on seed 7 sits on rule 8's line. | Fix 3b The implicit incision | 2026-09-25 | chunk/3b-implicit-erosion | **The stream-power law, not a numerical cap, sets every cut: the incision is Braun and Willett's implicit update (2013, Geomorphology 180-181) on the processor, and the collision uplift is re-derived from the denudation the law sets.** **The update** (`HydraulicErosion.incise`). `F = incisionCoefficient / landHalfOfField * cellsAcross * sqrt(share) * erodibility / step` (`Rates.courantCoefficient`), dimensionless, the law's cut over the drop, both in the height field's unit; about `0.24 sqrt(cells of catchment)` on a grid as many cells tall as wide (0.34 on square cells; Q2), median 4.4 and 1.8 to 13 (tenth to ninetieth percentile) on the drawn network of seed 42 at 512, every drawn cell over one. `FlowRouting.drainageOrder` walked backwards on the post-notch actual surface, discharge and cover held for the pass; each cell `z' = (z + F z_r') / (1 + F)` toward the level it grades to: its receiver's new ground, the round's shoreline where the receiver is sea (the shoreline cap becomes this boundary condition), and the water's surface where the receiver stands under a filled basin's water by more than the pond depth (a river entering a lake grades to the lake; without it 3,841 cells in seed 42's first round would have been cut into lake beds); a cell at or below that level is left alone. The half-the-drop cap is gone. Replaces the ordered incision pass and nothing else: uplift, flexure, routing and the notch before it, deposition fed its cuts after it. **Guards** (`ImplicitIncisionTest`, each shown failing on chunk 3's capped update re-expressed in the new pass, on a pass that cuts nothing, on eligibility judged against the receiver's old height, or on the explicit law clamped at the base): the bounds and the eligibility over seed 42's twelve rounds (1.2 million cuts, 182,107 at `F` over one each cut past half its drop, 40,236 mouths none below the shoreline, 31,010 cells left alone, 2,468 cut only because their receiver was; the capped update breaks the half-the-drop clause, the no-op cuts nothing) and a three-cell fixture (a cell 10 m below its receiver cut to 50.00 m where `F / (1 + F)` puts it; 90.00 m under the old-height rule); the law on a plane of known slope and catchment against a recursion in metres, `F` 0.024 to 66, every cell within a centimetre; the steady state on synthetic islands run to balance (routing held after a thousand rounds, because a few divide cells cycle between receivers under the facet routing and D8 alike): concavity 0.5000 in every zone, steepness 200.0, 50.0, 400.0 and 100.0 m against `U / (K e)`'s 200, 50, 400 and 100 across two rocks and two uplift rates, and wet over dry 0.5000 against the law's `sqrt(1/4)` across two rain zones; the capped update balances at 0.31 and 0.16 concavity; a knickpoint read as a sharp break of equal relief retreats 2.0000 cell widths a round east-west, north-south and in two rounds of half the time (the capped update 2.75 and 2.875, the no-op 3.0), its steepest-change point 1.25 to 1.60 and its smear 4.1 to 5.4 cell widths, which is the scheme's numerical diffusion. `ErosionUnitsTest` reads the law's rate the pass is handed (`K T sqrt(A) S` within 7e-8) and its half-the-drop guard is retired into the bounds. `ClimateFedErosionTest` is restated on the law's rate: the cover's quotient is the factor within 3e-7 on 89,000 to 92,000 cells a seed, the realised share where `F` is under a tenth within 9e-4 of `e (1 + F) / (1 + e F)`; the wet flank reads the rain alone against the unchanged `sqrt(P) * 0.8`, seed 1234 1.67 against 1.49 (1.42 with the cover's factor in too), and is armed; the dissection contrast is armed. `ReceiverClampTest`'s control is the eligibility rule (588, 1,286 and 1,036 holes without it, none with). **The uplift, re-derived.** With every uplift rate and the flexure off, the collisional and Andean belts lose 0.224, 0.261, 0.220, 0.231 and 0.257 mm a year on seeds 7, 42, 1234, 99 and 718106 at 512, 0.238 pooled (0.074 under the cap, with the flexure on; 0.134 on the implicit update with it on). Rock uplift is surface uplift plus denudation: collision 0.5 + 0.238 = 0.738 mm a year, a sum that holds for the flexure-off measurement it was taken from and does not establish that production, with the flexure on, gains half a millimetre a year of surface on its belts; and by England and Molnar's ratios Andean 0.2952, island arc 0.1038 and rift shoulder 0.04476 (from 0.290625, 0.11625, 0.040875 and 0.017625). `IsostasyTest`'s derivation runs with the flexure off and is armed; its tolerance by its own rule, half the five seeds' spread, is 0.02. **Calibrations re-examined**, none re-tuned (`TODO.md`): the transport capacity's derivation reads no cap and stands, not an Earth figure; the delta share, freeboard and minimum catchment and the notch's 1.125 were set by what they did to worlds under the cap, which is setting a value to make a world pass, and are recorded as open. The deposition keeps 0.8 to 0.9% of what is cut. **Generated worlds** (`RiverProfileReportTest`, reported): the traced rivers' whole-profile concavity median 0.60 (quartiles 0.39 to 0.83) against 0.47 (0.21 to 0.72) on the capped update; over homogeneous reaches median 0.42 (0.22 to 0.66), 31% in Earth's 0.4 to 0.7 (Whipple and others 2013), against 0.47 (0.03 to 0.88) and 21%; steepness `S sqrt(A)` 1.6 to 1.9 times higher under active uplift than off it (1.2 to 1.6 under the cap), and following erodibility and rain in the law's direction on some seeds only. **Cost** at 2048 (969495, 1.59 million land cells): the implicit pass 100 to 114 ms a round, one routing 1.23 s, the stage 112.8 s. **What the terrain moved.** Armed: the valley notch (4.64 times the bare ground, 0.0229 deep, and 0.0225, 0.0230 and 0.0229 along a row, down a column and on a diagonal on seed 7), the steep ground's facing (B-D1's, 25.9 against 26.7 m/km on seed 99), the lowstand's estuaries (1.70 times pooled), the belt's flank and the ranges' texture, the dissection contrast and the wet flank, the ice sheet's bed, the ice's comb, the monsoon's cold half, the cold-current coast, the tropics' desert and the collision rate. Re-recorded with figures: the coast's projection ratio (1.12 pooled, seed 99 1.16, under a finding of its own now that the per-step cap is gone), the coast's box dimension (1.082, ruler 1.187) and seed 99's drylands (1.00) in the Earth-likeness suite, the littoral smoothing gain, the plains' texture record (98.1 m against 68.6, not re-taken on the world it would have to pass), the recycling ratio (0.295), the rainfall calibration, the glacial lakes, the ice sheet's thickness and edge, the foreland, the shelf's jump flood and the largest realm. New known failures, each with its figures in its finding: the channel-head network grows 1.38 to 1.48 times denser from 512 to 1024 (1.15 to 1.23 under the cap; not the round's length, which the review round measured); the ice's flow near two domes; seed 99's lake 2.1 times the Caspian's share with the notch on, and the fill 82% as deep as the control's; the estuary control reaching the ceiling on one seed; the flat potential 2.02% of a generation on a quiet machine. The lakes are fewer: with the notch off at 1024, 1,676 to 7,175 lake cells against 12,257 to 24,088, and about a tenth of them in thin parallel grid-bearing bars where it was a hundredth (rule 13, in `TODO.md`). **To the eye** (renders of 7 and 42 at 1024 and 969495 at 2048, on the review branch): the dissection the cap took away is back and denser than main's, the ranges stand higher and whiter under the re-derived uplift, and the flanks of east-west ranges are combed by straight north-south gullies a cell apart, which a count shows is the routing's bearing on all three trees (59 to 64% of steep cells drain down a column) made visible by the law's deeper cut (`TODO.md`). **The graphics-card path** is specified in `TODO.md` for the next session: per-cell maps `min(c, a + b x)` that compose, pointer jumping on `(a, b, c)`. **Review round.** *A lake falls with its outlet*: the pass had held a lake at its filled level for the whole pass, so an inflow graded to water its outlet's cut had already drained; now a lake's surface for the pass is the lower of its filled level and its outlet's new height, carried upstream through the lake receivers first, a cell under it is neither cut nor raised, a cell it uncovers is graded like any other, and an inflow grades to the water as it now stands. Guarded by a draining-lake fixture (outlet cut to 10 m: the bed uncovered and cut to 16 m, the inflow to 26.4 m; outlet cut to 80 m: the 50 m bed kept, the inflow to 84 m; the frozen lake puts the inflow at 102 m in both). *The bounds guard* holds the half-the-drop clause strictly (28 of 190,908 cells at `F` over one clear half their drop by under two float steps and are counted, not judged) and computes each cell's base itself, walking a ponded receiver down to what its lake spills onto; on the frozen lake it fails in round 0. *What it moved*: fourteen recorded clauses re-recorded, three armed (the ice sheet's edge, 31 against 36.1 allowed; the graded coast's gain, 0.553 against 0.412; the largest realm, 29%), and seed 99's lake falls under the Caspian's share while seeds 718106 and 7 keep more than half their water. The cost: the thin parallel bars' share of the standing water with the notch off at 1024 rises from about a tenth to about a sixth (0.078 to 0.168 on seed 42 with the ice off), which puts the ice's comb clause over its fiftieth on seed 42 (2.48%, the ice's own share no higher than the bare world's) and gives the ruled-bar census a 27-cell bar on seed 42 at 512; both recorded under one finding (`TODO.md`). *The network's growth with the grid* (reported): at 1024 with twenty-four rounds of half the years the channel-head density is 1.004, 1.019, 1.000 and 0.999 times the stock 1024's on seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99, against 1.42 to 1.54 from 512; the time step has converged and the growth is the grid's, cause not isolated, and the finding is renamed from "first order". *The comb* (reported): on synthetic ridges running east-west and north-south under the same uplift the channels follow the fall line on both bearings under both updates (89 to 99%), and a parallel channel within two cell widths of ground stands beside 97 to 99% of channel cells under the capped update on both bearings and 56 to 71% under the implicit one; the update does not make the comb. *The comb's cause* (reported, and restated by the comb round): the steepest third of seeds 7 and 42's land at 1024 drains down a column 47 to 51% on an isotropic synthetic surface routed over the same land, 42 to 45% on the terrain before erosion and 59 to 61% on the finished world. The review round read these against 35%, the nearest bearing's share, and put the cause in the routing; the router's own rule (Tarboton's facet with the Rho8 draw) expects 44.87% down a column over isotropic bearings, and production takes 45.04% on planes at 360 bearings on three seeds, within 1.5 standard errors (`RoutingGroundTest`). So the router has no residual on planes, the terrain before erosion sits at its rule, and the 14 to 16 points over it are the rounds'. *Comb experiments, second round*: candidate A (the incision cut off below a channel head) is reverted, since at this grid every cell passes a head. Two forms of the sub-grid transport net of the resolved incision are built behind settings, off by default, and derived in `ErosionConfig`'s KDoc: `subGridTransportAcrossTheFall`, `-D_sub (I - u u^T) grad z` with `u` the bearing to the receiver, because along the receiver the resolved cut already carries a head cell's own sub-grid flux; and `subGridTransportUndrainedShare`, `D_sub (1 - p)` with `p` the facet share the Rho8 draw sends down the drawn receiver. At 512 on seeds 7 and 42, against stock and against B at `K dx dy`: the column comb is 0.102 and 0.109 km per 1,000 km² across the fall, 0.322 and 0.423 by the undrained share, 0.077 and 0.110 under B, and 0.408 and 0.519 stock (at 1024, 0.19 and 0.18, 0.42 and 0.50, 0.16 and 0.18, 0.44 and 0.48); the network 24.5 and 29.2, 30.2 and 34.3, 20.8 and 25.3, against 31.6 and 36.6; the valley notch 0.0159 (3.24 times the bare ground), 0.0230 (4.62), 0.0108 (2.43, failing), against 0.0244 (4.89); the ranges' texture 125.6, 166.5 and 97.3 m (under B's 103.8 bar), against 185.9; the plains' texture 77.4, 89.1 and 60.6 m against the record's 68.6 and stock's 100.9; the network's growth from 512 to 1024 1.48 and 1.42, 1.52 and 1.48, 1.36 and 1.32, against 1.54 and 1.51. Across the fall stops the rounds' turn toward the columns as B does (diagonal to column 19 and 22% against 39 stock); the undrained share halves it at most. No form removes the comb while keeping the valleys and the network: `CombGuardTest`, given a floor from its control of 0.01 km per 1,000 km², fails every one. *Comb experiments, third round*: `WorldGenConfig.clampedDescentDraw` chooses a clamped cell's receiver by Fairfield and Leymarie's Rho8, the drawn diagonal uniform between its longer leg and the sum of its legs; off by default. Its derivation says a cell clamped to its cardinal keeps it under every draw, and the census bears it out: of the comb's cells down a column on seeds 7 and 42 at 512, 86.9 and 84.8% are clamped to the cardinal, none to the diagonal, and the rest already drawn inside the facet. On planes due north-south, 22.5 and 45 degrees off it and at 360 bearings the shares are unchanged; on a plane at the diagonal's own bearing it sends half the cells along the row and turns the mean bearing 12.3 degrees toward it; on isotropic ground it moves about 1.8% of receivers from diagonals to rows. The column comb is 0.413 and 0.519 with it against stock's 0.408 and 0.519, and 0.111 and 0.111 with across the fall against across the fall's 0.102 and 0.109; the network 31.5 and 36.5, and 24.4 and 29.5; the notch 0.0243 and 0.0159. It does not remove the comb. *Integration with main*: the comb experiments' code is removed (the ledger and the review keep what they measured), the comb is recorded as the half-height cell's known failure with square cells as its fix, and `origin/main` (4d0a3da, with chunk 6) is merged in. Six files conflicted, all tests or records apart from this ledger; two generator clauses moved under chunk 6's water (the notch's seed 7 water, 1.1550% to 0.8455%, and the ice's bars on seed 42, 2.50%), the twelve render fingerprints were regenerated, and seed 1234's lake combs and realm rectangles changed sides of the geometry guard's measurable line. The graphics-card path accelerates only the thermal sweeps; the hydraulic rounds, the implicit incision among them, run on the processor, and on the software device GPU mode's sweeps match the processor's exactly and its finished terrain to the six places the guard prints. |
| Site 4 Numbered cards that reveal along a line | 2026-09-25 | chunk/site-reveal-cards | The three figure sections of the landing page became rows of cards: a picture, a numbered brass square on one hairline across the row, and a name with one line under it. "How a world is made" is six cards on the line, "Realistic map styles" three and "The data behind the map" four; the last two were a composite strip each, with naming bands drawn into the file and a second, stacked file for phones. Each card's picture is its own file cut by `SiteImagery` from the same world, seed 718106 at 2048: the three styles from the same 640 square window and the four views from the same 480 by 600 window the strips used, at 1:1, named by the card's heading rather than by lettering, so the band tints, the dividers and the typeface argument left the renderer and its Gradle task. The six steps are pictured by the view that shows what each step makes, every picture 480 by 320: tectonic plates over a 1920 by 1280 window reduced by four, elevation at 1:1, Natural and biomes over the same 960 by 640 window of the eastern island reduced by two, Natural at 1:1 on the southern continent's east coast, and political over the west coast reduced by two. A reduction is repeated halving with bilinear sampling at exactly half, which is a box filter. The windows were chosen off full sheets to keep three generator artefacts off the page, which are reported here rather than fixed: the ice caps are drawn with a straight edge down a column and a fan of rays (the plates window moved 80 pixels east off one), the dry belt crosses the northern lowlands as a band ruled along a row (the river card went south to avoid it), and the styles window's estuary sea has a straight west edge and a straight top. Bytes: the thirteen card pictures are 494,588 (steps 162,238, styles 176,030, views 156,320). The strips they replace were 180,206 and 168,548 wide and 181,508 and 171,078 stacked, 701,340 published and 348,754 fetched by a wide reader, so the tree publishes 206,752 less and a reader of the three sections fetches 145,834 more: the six step pictures, less 16,404 saved on the other two rows. The reveal is the page's own CSS and a few lines of script, no dependency. A class set in the head before the first paint, only when the browser has an `IntersectionObserver` and the reader has not asked for reduced motion, scopes every hiding rule; each card is watched once, and the cards that arrive together go in turn, 180 ms apart, their stretch of line drawing across, the square appearing and the picture and words fading up 14 px. With scripts off or motion reduced nothing is ever hidden, and a reduced-motion block stills every hiding selector for a preference set after load. Opacity and transform only, so nothing moves the layout: a headless Chrome measured a cumulative layout shift of 0 and no sideways overflow at 375, 768, 1280 and 1920. Below 600 px the cards stack and the line runs down the left from square to square; from 600 the styles sit three to a row and the steps and views two; from 900 the steps sit three and the views four. The page has one colour scheme, the application's dark one, so its light and dark screenshots are the same page. Guards: `SiteAssemblyTest` holds the fourteen pictures to a written table of names and sizes, to the page's width and height attributes and to the published folder; each comparison row to one window, distinct readings, 1:1 and headings that are the reading's own label; and no picture to fewer than 32 colours. `SiteSourcesTest` holds every card picture to a width, a height and its own non-empty `alt`, every hiding rule to the script's class with no scroll listener, and the reduced-motion block to every hiding selector. `SitePaletteContrastTest` measures the card number, ink on brass, at 7.63:1, and the line and square, brass on ink, at 7.63:1 against 3.0. Each was shown failing on its defect: a reserved width of 600 for a 640 picture, a card headed Classroom over the Schoolroom picture, a flat picture, a missing picture, a stale stacked strip left in the folder, a card picture with no `alt`, a hiding rule outside the class, a reduced-motion block that forgot the squares, and the card number's pair taken out. |
| Fix 2c The browser's library in a folder on the disk | 2026-09-25 | chunk/2c-browser-folder | **In Chrome and Edge the browser's library can live in a folder the reader chooses, the same `.cgw` files under the same names as the desktop's; Firefox and Safari keep the browser's storage, unchanged.** Offered where `showDirectoryPicker` exists, asked of the feature and never of the browser's name (`BrowserFolderTest` hides the picker and finds no folder offered and every other platform answer the same; with the check removed it fails). **The folder library** (`FolderWorldLibrary`, `:web`) is a `ByteWorldLibrary` over a `FileSystemDirectoryHandle`: a save over a file that is there goes through the file's own writable stream, which Chrome writes to a `<name>.crswap` beside it and moves over the file only on `close` (seen in the origin private file system: `new.cgw.crswap` holding the bytes and `new.cgw` empty while a stream was open; an aborted stream left an existing file as it was); a new save is written as `~<name>.<token>.tmp` and moved to its name with `move` once whole, because `getFileHandle(create)` makes the empty target at once and an aborted stream leaves it there (both seen). Its name is chosen again immediately before the move, so another tab, the desktop or a sync client that takes the name during the write keeps its file and this save takes the next free one; ordering is one tab's mutex, and nothing gives a page an exclusive create, so the window left is the one `move` call. Where `move` is missing or answers `NotSupportedError`, the temporary file is copied into the name and removed, the name visible and empty while that runs. Reads are `Blob.slice`s of a part. **Guards, each shown failing on its defect first**: the contract suite in a real headless Chrome (`FolderLibraryTest`, eleven cases); with a new file written straight to its name, its name chosen before the write, and a failed stream closed instead of aborted, four failed — a failed save of an existing file committed 8,192 of its 182,278 bytes, a failed new save left `w1.cgw`, a save still being written listed as `w1.cgw` (empty, incomplete), and another writer's `w1.cgw` was written over. **Memory**: the library counts what it holds for a save (`BufferGauge`), its part buffer and the copy the stream has not yet taken, each write awaited; a 32 world's raw save of 182,284 bytes in 16 KiB parts, twelve parts, peaked at 32,768 bytes writing and reading, two parts, the bound (`FolderMemoryTest`); a sink gathering the whole save before one write peaked at 444,428. The count is the library's, not the tab's heap, which the collector moves. **Across the two front ends**: a save `DesktopWorldStore` wrote opens from the folder library and saves back under its name, and the folder library's save is the codec's bytes under `<id>.cgw` and opens and saves in `DesktopWorldStore` (`FolderInteropTest` on each side, from two checked-in saves `RegenerateInteropFixtures` remakes when the format moves); naming new files as the download does failed it. **Remembered across visits**: the handle and the choice, as two facts, in IndexedDB (`cartogenesis-library-place`), so "use this browser's storage" survives a handle kept; at start `queryPermission` decides — granted and listable, the folder; prompt, a Reconnect button, and nothing listed, saved or deleted until it is pressed or the browser's storage chosen; denied or gone, the browser's storage stands in and says so. A failure mid-visit asks again and moves the library the same way. The picker and `requestPermission` run first in the click, the coroutine started undispatched. `LibraryPlacesTest` holds the states with a fake folder; with prompt read as a fall-back it failed, and `LibraryFolderPaneTest` in a window found this browser's storage listed where the folder was waiting. **A key belongs to its library**: `DocumentIdentity` keeps the library a key came from and Save into any other files the world as new; carried across, a Save after switching wrote over a stranger's same-named file in the other place (the pane test, 91,916 bytes written over 91,909). This holds on the desktop too, whose library moves with its folder setting. Copying the browser's worlds into the folder is one click and copies bytes a part at a time (`ByteWorldLibrary.copyFrom`), offered only for worlds the folder has no copy of. The origin private file system stands in for a picked folder everywhere but permission (always granted there), the operating system's file system under a picked folder (Windows refusing to replace a file another program holds open; a check Chrome describes making on close, not seen here) and sync clients; a folder picked by hand was not exercised. **A second reading of the built branch found six more, each fixed and shown failing with its defect put back.** Rows: the pane kept the last place's rows while the new one was listed, and a row's Delete went to the library in use, so deleting this browser's `w1.cgw` from the row still on screen removed the folder's (`LibraryFolderPaneTest`); a listing now carries the library that made it (`LibraryShelf`), the pane draws it only while that library is in use, and a row's Open and Delete go to it. The fallback copy: `getFileHandle(create)` opens another writer's file as readily as it makes one, and a failure to remove the temporary file after a finished copy was handled as the save failing, which removed the save; the name is now looked up again immediately before the file is made, a file found with anything in it is passed over for the next free name, a failed copy removes its file only while it is still empty, and letting go of the temporary file is a step of its own after publishing (three `FolderLibraryTest` cases, failing as the other writer's file taking this save's name, `w1.cgw` left after a failed copy, and the stuck temporary file's error ending the save). Cancellation: the waits on the browser could not be cancelled, so a save cancelled while a part was with the browser handed it all 44 remaining parts and was then made whole, new or over a file; waits are cancellable now, and the close and the move, the commits, are seen to their end once begun, so a cancelled save is not made or made whole (three cases, the cancel landing on the third part through a hook in the sink, not at a stand-in compressor; IndexedDB's commit and cleanup were put under `NonCancellable` for the same reason). A finished save was matched to the document on screen by id, and a conflict copy opened meanwhile carries the same id, so it was given the earlier file's key; saves now belong to the opening they were asked in (`OpenDocument`, `SavesAndExportsTest`). A slow answer about a folder left behind moved the library back to it after the reader had chosen another (`ReconnectNeeded` over a newer `InFolder`); every change of place after a wait is now made only if no request has come since (`LibraryPlacesTest`). The failure, visibility, cancellation, name and memory guards also run with `move` taken off the handles: there the save is copied into its name and holds four parts at once, not two — the part being gathered, the copy the stream is taking, the slice being read back and the buffer it passes through — measured 65,536 bytes writing a 182,284-byte save in 16 KiB parts against the move's 32,768; asserted at two, as the first round claimed, it failed. Two gaps stay, written in the README: a file another program creates in the moment between the last look and the move is replaced, and the fallback shows an empty `.cgw` while it copies. **A third reading found two more.** A cancel landing while the copy's file was being made left it, empty and listed, and one landing while a stream was being opened left the stream open, its `w1.cgw.crswap` beside the save and the save undeletable (`NoModificationAllowedError` on remove and on move with a stream open, both seen in the private file system); making the file, looking at it and opening a stream are now seen to their end, and the cancel is met just after, where they are cleared (two `FolderLibraryTest` cases, each failing on the plain wait). And a request was numbered when the picker or the permission question answered, so a slow Allow outranked the choice of this browser's storage made while it was open; requests are numbered when the click starts them (two `LibraryPlacesTest` cases, `InFolder` over `HostStorage` with the defect back). A third gap stays, in the README: in the fallback, a file another program writes into the name after the copy has found it empty and before the copy starts is written over. |

The world has no desert coast beside cold water, and its two rift lakes lie by the polar ice and among the ice cap's straight edges on the southern continent, so neither of those candidates was used.
