| Chunk 6 Water and realms | 2026-09-25 | chunk/6-water-realms | **Rivers and lakes get the water that falls on them, and realms and peoples are built from catchments that are one piece of ground each and no larger than their share.** Nine findings of an audit, each verified on the tree before it was changed and each with a guard shown failing on origin/main's code (run there through test-side adapters to the old signatures, or with the one fix switched off) and passing after. **1. A closed basin's rain.** The balance's catchment rain was accumulated once, before any basin closed, and basins were solved in cell-index order, so a lake below a closed basin was fed the rain its neighbour had already evaporated. Basins are solved upstream first and a closed basin's catchment is taken out below its old spill; the inflow is also summed over every cell where a basin's water leaves it, where the maximum under-fed a basin draining across a level rim. `WaterReceivedTest`: two chained basins, a desert playa above a steppe basin, the lower one's inflow 76,800 mm-cells against 76,800 reaching it; without the subtraction 83,328, over by exactly the playa's 6,528. Lakes on 7/42/1234/99 at 512 and 969495 at 2048: 19/24/4/16/32 to 19/24/4/17/32, closed 6/2/1/4/5 to 5/1/0/5/2, playa cells 8/34/10/23/902 to 11/34/15/7/1,133, share of land 1.028/1.887/0.129/1.966/2.681% to 1.010/1.893/0.148/1.994/2.727%. **2. Discharge past 1,200 mm.** The drawn network and the realm stage's riverine threshold read `Runoff.annualWeightMm` instead of the clamped 0..1 rainfall plus a floor; the 60 mm floor is restated as UNEP's hyper-arid line (aridity index 0.05) under a 1,200 mm potential evaporation, the figure it always stood for, so the erosion and the channel criterion that read it do not move. In dry country the old weight added the floor to the rain (a 100 mm cell weighed 160 against a 1,200 mm cell's 1,260) and the new one floors it (100 against 1,200). Two identical islands at 1,500 and 3,000 mm deliver a ratio of 2.0000, 1.0000 before. Drawn kilometres in catchments over 1,200 mm on the five worlds 0.0/1.5/0.8/4.6/2.6% to 0.6/2.5/1.1/6.7/3.4%, under 400 mm 69.6/57.8/75.4/62.9/65.8% to 68.4/56.7/75.0/59.0/64.2%, courses drawn 58/71/56/67/201 to 58/74/55/67/209, median drawn mouth width ratio 0.432/0.348/0.340/0.398/0.319 to 0.460/0.362/0.321/0.436/0.312. **3. Catchments labelled receivers first**, down `FlowRouting.drainageOrder` reversed, not by filled height (0.0 to 0.8% of land cells had a receiver later in that order): a 925-cell tree on a flat, 925 units before, 1 after. **4. A closed basin whole:** each endorheic lake and each connected playa is one sink; a 25-cell lake in a 225-cell basin was 25 units of water and 125 of basin, is 1. **5. The size rule in area:** units are cut at confluences, smallest tributaries kept first, to `maxBasinShare` (or `maxRegionShare`) of the land's area in km², and no merge may pass it; an arid comb of 3.1 times the share was one unit, its largest piece is now 0.78 of the share; the largest realm unit on 7/42/1234 went from 4.2/3.8/3.0 times the share to within it. **6. E-T10, the realm cap.** Not a one-unit realm: on seed 7 the largest realm held 24.2% when the cap had run, in 12 units, and 33.6% on the finished map, because the enclave pass gave it its neighbours' stranded pieces. The pass now passes over a host a piece would take past the cap, and the cap pass sets an unsplittable realm aside instead of stopping. Armed; the largest realm on 7/42/1234/99 and 969495 at 2048 is 21.4/23.8/19.9/20.6/21.0% (33.6/29.2/24.7/23.1/29.9%). **7. Tied biomes** in the realm stage's shares, heartland and settled shares break by ordinal, as the culture stage's did; `RealmTiesTest` generates seed 42 at 512 in two child processes with the enum's identity hashes drawn apart, and the realms' digest differed before and agrees now. **8. Merges over land only**, into the neighbour sharing the longest border on the ground; realm expansion keeps its strait crossings. A small island two cells off a large one: landmasses 2 to 1 before, 2 to 2 after; seed 7 had 14 units on two landmasses, now none. **9. Coastal capitals:** measured and left. With the relief mean over land only instead of land and sea, the coastal capitals on the five worlds were unchanged at 15/18, 7/14, 8/16, 7/15 and went from 3/14 to 4/14; the coastal share with the realms re-laid is 40 of 77 against 36 of 77 before. **Borders at 2048**, seed 718106, straight runs lying exactly along a grid line: realm borders along a row or column 98 of eight steps or more, 10 of 50 km or more, 1 of 100 km or more, longest 123 km, to 94, 8, 0 and 67 km (the run at cell (981,505) follows the ice sheet's ruled edge); on the diagonals 10 of 50 km or more, longest 147 km, to 6 and 102 km; peoples' borders along rows and columns 21 of 50 km or more, longest 179 km, to 15 and 179 km at the same cells, which are ice and biome edges. No guard detector failed on either layer before or after. **Recorded.** Two known failures moved by the lakes and re-recorded, failing as before: the notch's pooled drowned basin (0.2609% against 0.2505% to 0.2591% against 0.2489%) and C I4's glacial lake ratio (1.08 to 1.02). The 512 census lists the realm-border rectangle clause as too small to measure on 7, 42 and 99, where no realm is a small ring now, and measures seed 1234's open-water arcs, clean. Render records re-taken. The 2048 audit tier (`:cartography:audit`) was not run. |
| Chunk 6 review round | 2026-09-25 | chunk/6-water-realms | **Four points a second reading of chunk 6 found, each verified on the code, fixed, and guarded by a case shown failing with the fix switched off.** **Basins with several exits.** Ordering closed basins by where their last exit falls in the drainage is not topological once a basin has two exits: its early exit can feed a lower basin its late one comes after, and the subtraction then walked a routing a closed basin below had already re-pointed. The basins are now a graph, an edge from each to the basin its exits' water reaches next on the routing the fill left, solved in Kahn's order, and the subtraction walks that routing. `WaterReceivedTest` draws the case by hand, with the precondition asserted on the drainage ranks: the lower basin is given 450 mm-cells against 450 reaching it, and 700 under the exit-rank order. The same case asserts the upper basin's two exits, a path leaving it and coming back counted once, and its water reaching the lower basin before any basin closed; the terrain case asserts the last, and its basins turn out to have three exits each. **A closed lake past the size limit.** Each water cell kept its own slopes up to the limit and the sink then kept every water cell with them; a two-cell lake with nine cells draining to its second cell made an eleven-cell unit under a ten-cell limit. The water is now one node, never cut, and the land draining to any cell of it is cut at the limit like any other tributary; the case gives units of 2 and 9 cells, and 11 with `BasinPartition` as it stood. **Stranded pieces.** With the cap respected, the enclave pass left a piece no neighbour could take where it was. Such a piece now becomes a realm of its own with its capital on its best ground when it is the size of the smallest realm, and a smaller one goes to the neighbour with most of its edge; a schism's breakaway takes any run of the rest it would cut off. A hand-made case, a 36-cell piece inside a realm the cap will not let take it, ends as realm 4 with no piece stranded and no realm over the cap; with the fix off the piece is left stranded. Counted over 7/42/1234/99 at 512 and 718106 at 1024 (`RealmPieces`), stranded pieces were 0/0/0/0/1 on origin/main, 0 at the first build and are 0; realms held inside one land neighbour 5/4/5/4/5 on origin/main (4 of them landlocked), 4/4/5/5/8 at the first build (2 landlocked) and 2/5/5/6/7 now (2 landlocked). The four small realms inside the large one on 718106's eastern island at 1024 are realms seeded on that island and grown round, not pieces the cap left, and are still there. The standard-seed clause asserts no stranded piece and no more than origin/main's three landlocked realms inside one neighbour, and passes on origin/main too; the hand-made case is what fails on the defect. Largest realm on 7/42/1234/99 and 969495 at 2048: 21.4/26.6/19.9/24.8/21.0%. **Realm borders at 2048**, 718106: row or column runs of 50 km or more 12 (10 on origin/main, 8 at the first build), of 100 km or more 0, longest 76 km; no guard detector failed. Rivers and lakes on the five measured worlds did not move in this round; the realms on 7, 42 and 99 did. |
| Chunk 6 third round | 2026-09-25 | chunk/6-water-realms | **Two gaps a reading of the review round found, each guarded by a case shown failing with the fix switched off.** **Small pieces adding up past the cap.** A piece smaller than the smallest realm could go to a neighbour whatever that neighbour already held over the cap, so two twenty-cell pieces took a realm at 760 cells of a 768-cell cap to 800, 32 over, against a stated bound of under the smallest realm (24 cells there). The allowance is now counted over the neighbour's whole excess: a small piece goes to the neighbour with most of its edge only while that neighbour stays under the cap plus the smallest realm, and any piece no neighbour can take within that becomes a realm of its own. The pass therefore leaves no stranded piece and no realm a smallest realm or more over the cap, and makes a realm under the smallest size only where a small piece has nowhere else to go. `RealmSpreadTest`'s case ends with the host 12 over and the second piece a realm; with the allowance per piece it ends 32 over. **Basins feeding each other.** A forest of cells allows two closed basins to feed each other, each by a different exit, and the basins' graph then has a cycle that the topological walk appended in index order, the first solved on rain the other keeps. Basins are now grouped by Tarjan's strongly connected components and the groups walked upstream first; a group of more than one is iterated to a fixed point, each member's inflow measured with every closed basin keeping its water, and since a closure only takes water away the closed set only grows and settles within as many passes as the group has members. `WaterReceivedTest`'s four-exit case gives each basin 450 mm-cells against 450 reaching it; appended in index order, the first was given 550. On seeds 7/42/1234/99 at 512 and 718106 and 969495 at 2048 no group of basins feeds each other (21/27/6/19/30/41 closed basins, 15/13/6/14/27/35 of them with more than one exit), so no world moved on this account. |
| Site 6 The opening and download cards that open | 2026-09-25 | chunk/site-6-opening | Built from a design study the author approved as it stood; our own CSS and script, no dependency. **The title card**: a first visit opens on CARTOGENESIS in spaced white capitals over the whole map, with *Build a world from the ground up* under it; the letters light left to right, 115 ms apart and 0.72 s each, white to a pale-gold flash to the page's brass, and stay lit. The band fades in once it has arrived and decoded, or after 1.5 s whatever happens, the card stands 2.5 s from then, and over 1.3 s the map comes down into the opening, the card fades and the panel rises; a scroll, a tap or a key sends it at once, in 0.28 s. Once a visit (session storage, read and written inside a `try`; a browser that cannot remember never plays it), never under reduced motion, never for a reader who arrived at a place on the page, never without scripts. The head's script decides before the first paint: a headless Chrome saw the page's own script finish at 30 to 37 ms and the first paint at 72 to 120 ms at every width, so nothing is swapped after it is drawn; if the page's script does not finish, the head takes its classes off again at DOMContentLoaded and the page is the no-script one. Timed from navigation with the pictures served locally, the band showed at 83 to 87 ms and the opening was settled at 3,892 to 3,897 ms; on a slow connection the card waits at most 1.5 s, so it is settled by 5.3 s. The card's words sit on a scrim of their own, an ellipse of the ink flat at 74% across the middle 55 hundredths of its radius and feathered beyond; every letter's corners were measured inside the flat part (the worst at 0.832 of it) at 375, 768, 1280 and 1920 wide. The study's scrim, 62% at its centre and less where the name's ends sat, puts the lit brass at 2.14:1 over the band's white ice, which it holds (the band's brightest pixel is 255, 255, 255); 74% puts it at 3.28:1 against the large-text 3.0 (the name is at least 22 pixels in the semibold). The line under the name has no italic face bundled and is slanted by the browser, as it was in the study. **The opening**: the drifting world full-bleed across the top and an ink panel under it holding the eyebrow, the `h1`, the lede, the two buttons (brass and ink as the study had them, where the page's primary was oxblood) and a round cue; as the page scrolls, linked to it and never taking it over, the panel rises over the map like a curtain and the map pulls in to a 2:1 plate under the heading, at most 960 wide, the next section rising into the space beneath. Built three ways: in the page's flow, settled, which is what a reader without scripts, a reader who asked for less motion and a screen too short all get; pinned (`unfold`), the stage laid out as the opening at rest, map above panel, and moved by transforms alone (the map translated and scaled, the band inside it counter-scaled across so it keeps its shape and the map's box clips it, the panel translated); and for the card (`splash`), the map laid over the whole stage. Every size is read off the stage, whose height is `100svh`, so a phone's toolbar showing or hiding changes nothing the opening is measured by; not tried on a phone, only in an emulated one. A screen under 500 pixels tall is never pinned, which the head decides; a taller one whose panel would leave the map under a quarter of the screen, or the plate under 240 pixels wide, or where the band laid twice could not cover the open map, is settled in the flow by the script, and a resize or a change of browser zoom measures again. Space short, the plate narrows at 2:1. The drift is the style sheet's own animation of a track of two copies of the band, once round in 240 s, paused off screen and by a round pause control on the map's corner; the scroll is listened to, passively, only while the opening is on screen, and asks for one frame at most. **The band at full size**: `SiteImagery.WORLD_BAND`, the author's window's 800 rows the whole way round the world at the sheet's own pixels, 4096 by 800, 288,294 bytes at quality 60, and halved to 2048 by 400, 90,848 bytes. Measured at quality 40, 50, 60, 72 and 80: 218,320, 253,142, 288,294, 339,250 and 426,212 full, 65,578 to 133,568 half; 60 because, looked at enlarged one and a half times (the band is drawn at up to the screen's height, 1.35 times its rows at 1920 by 1080), it showed no loss 72 did not, where 40 blocked the shaded slopes. A `<picture>` sends the half band to a screen under 700 wide at under 1.2 device pixels to the CSS pixel (the plate there is at most 326 pixels tall, which 400 rows cover to 1.2) and the full band to everything else. Seams 23.2 against a roughest inner pair of 53.6, and 25.0 against 49.6; first stretch 3.3 and 6.7 from the link preview. The hero picture left the page and stays as the link preview. The old `hero-strip.webp` is gone. **The tray** replaces the top bar: the wordmark and the two buttons, along the top on a computer and the foot on a phone (where the wordmark is dropped for the buttons' width), in once the opening's buttons have gone off the top, out while the download section is on screen or while the step frame, the style slider, the roadmap, the report band or the footer is under it; everything it watches, it watches with an IntersectionObserver over the tray's own band, so nothing reads the scroll; hidden, it is `visibility: hidden` and inert. **How a world is made**: the six cards unchanged, the frame that plays them moved under them, beside it only the step's number and name. **Read the land removed**, with `SiteLandmarks.kt`, the pins, `pins.html`, the saved `world.cgw` and its guard, and `:desktop:siteTest`'s heap back to the 512 MB it had before. **Download cards** replace the ledger rows: Windows on brass, Linux on the lit oxblood, the browser on the sunk ink with a hairline; compact, an icon, a round +, the name and a line; chosen, a card opens across the row and the others move beneath it, by measuring each card's place before and after and sliding it between them in 420 ms, its contents scaled the other way at twelve steps so the words keep their shape. Escape or the × closes, and puts the keyboard back on the card's head if it was inside; the reader's system gets the For your system tag and nothing opens itself; with scripts off every card is open, full width, its head saying `aria-expanded="true"`. Every file is a pill that links to `/releases/latest` and names its file in `data-asset`; after the load the script asks GitHub's API once, keeps the answer or the failure for the visit for a quarter of an hour, gives up after 5 s, and turns each pill whose own file the release carries (by exact name, the tag's version put in, on this project's release page only) into a link to it with its size. The Windows card's graphics line says what the code does: exported pictures are drawn on the card where there is one, and erosion, the ocean and the ice sheet when acceleration is switched on (it starts off). The phone note that stood under the hero's buttons is a line of the Browser card. **Loads**, measured in a headless Chrome at 375 and 1280 wide, at one and two device pixels to the CSS pixel, on a first visit and a returning one: the page, all five faces (the whole page is laid out at once, so the three the head does not preload are fetched at load too, which Site 5a's guard left out), the six step pictures, which are lazy but inside the fetch-ahead distance, and one band: 1,373,328 bytes on a narrow screen at one device pixel and 1,570,774 on every other, against 1,432,079 for Site 5a's page counted the same way. Nothing a style sheet or a script asks for was missed: the release lookup comes after the load. Largest paint: 68 to 128 ms on a returning visit (the band); on a first visit the title's type paints first and the largest paint is recorded when the card leaves and the band is laid out again, 3,912 to 3,916 ms, because the band fades in from nothing under the card and Chrome does not count a picture first painted at no opacity. Layout shift 0 and no sideways overflow at 375, 768, 1280 and 1920, at rest, down the page, with the cards opened by the pointer and with scripts off; with motion reduced 0 at three widths and 0.000003 at 1920. **Guards**, each shown failing on a planted defect. `SiteSourcesTest`: a Browser card claiming worlds up to 4096 (the claim guard now reads "worlds up to N" and every clause of the Browser card not naming the desktop; the old pattern let it through); a frame asked for outside the opening (the scroll guard, which replaces Site 5a's ban on scroll listeners: no default prevented, no listener able to, no snapping, one passive scroll listener, inside the opening and taken off again, the page scrolling for the reader only when the cue is pressed, every frame the opening's); the struck letters' animation outside `.splash` (the title card's guard, and the motion guard, which now allows `key-struck` colour and glow and nothing else, and visibility beside opacity and transform for the tray); a card's head saying `aria-expanded="false"` in the markup (the no-script guard); a pill written straight to one release's file (the pill guard: every pill falls back to `/releases/latest`, the pills and `site/downloads.txt` name the same files, no version or size is written); the tray hidden only by moving it (the keyboard guard). `SitePaletteContrastTest`: the study's 62% scrim (2.14:1) and a Windows dim line at 55% (3.07:1); the test now reads each card's ground and inks out of the card's own rule and measures five pairs on each, the command well only on the card that has one, which caught the rule's first draft measuring the well on the Windows card at 3.98:1, and the page's two colours of its own, white and the flash, are named beside the window's palette. `SiteAssemblyTest`: a full band whose last 512 columns were another stretch (seam 214.9 against 113.8), a half band starting 200 columns east (41.1 from the preview), a picture asked for at load that the stated load does not count, and a lookup taking any release file for any pill (every-file-matches-its-own-pill failed); the lookup's eleven cases run the page's own code in Node. `:desktop:siteTest` 22 tests, `SiteSourcesTest` 16 and `SitePaletteContrastTest` 8, all passing clean. |
| Seed link A link that opens a world | 2026-09-25 | chunk/seed-link | **The browser application opens the world its address names, and File copies a link to the world on screen.** The seed stays in the query, so `/app/?seed=718106` written by hand opens that seed at the size and settings a fresh window starts with; everything else follows `#`, which never reaches the server: `v=1`, the size, and every keyed setting that differs from its default, as `name=value` pairs in the panel's order, the style and view by their constants' names. `https://cartogenesis.com/app/?seed=718106#v=1&size=512` is a default world; every keyed setting changed at 2048 is 302 characters. Nineteen knobs are keyed; two are left out by name, where the work runs (the machine's) and the river density (the reader's preference, which a link opened must not overwrite), and a test fails if a knob is neither. Reading takes only the seed, the size and the graphics preference from the window and builds the rest from the generator's defaults through the panel's own knobs. A link in another format is refused whole with a line saying so; a malformed seed, an unknown name or a value outside its control's range is set aside in one line and the rest applies; a size above the ceiling is brought down with the ceiling's own reason. A window opened at a link is armed and generates without Generate, and the set-aside line survives the generation it is about. Floats: the JVM writes 1.337e-6 as `1.337E-6`, Kotlin/Wasm as `0.000001337`; both read back through a double, and a decimal whose double lies exactly on a float midpoint, where that route and the JVM's direct parse can part, is written as its bits instead; 24,000 dial values round-tripped bit-exactly on both platforms, none needing bits. The copy is File's, after Export (Ctrl+L on the desktop only): in the menu on the desktop, in a browser and folded into the phone's button, without a row of the panel. The desktop copies the published address, the browser its own page's; the line says when the world was made on the graphics device (the same to the eye), came from a save with settings no link carries, or has labels and edits that stay behind. Guards, each shown failing on a mutation: a link that drops tilt fails the round trip and the whole-world fingerprint at 128 (`ReachableState`, climate, erosion, sea and ocean branches moved); reading any version fails the refusal; ignoring the query seed fails seed-alone and six others; clamping instead of setting aside fails the set-aside case; six-digit floats without the read-back check fail the float walk (0.5764044 read as 0.5764); the published address in the browser and an unread address fail both browser cases; another base on the desktop and a cleared line fail the desktop's two; an unarmed gate fails the window case. Tests: `:ui:jvmTest` 160, `:ui:wasmJsTest` 158, `:web:wasmJsTest` 41, and 26 in `:desktop:test` across the link, settings-effect and chrome cases, all passing. |
| Site 5b The second set of living figures, and two fixes to the opening | 2026-09-25 | chunk/site-5b | Our own CSS, script and WebGL, no dependency. **The opening at wide screens**: reproduced at 1920, 2000, 2560 and 3440 wide in a headless Chrome. The thin wide band mid-way is the intended moment, the panel risen over the top of a map still far wider than the plate it is heading for; the faint empty rectangle was a fault: the plate's hairline frame stood at the plate's settled place from 60% of the settling on, so where the map had not arrived it showed as a rectangle hanging below the map (at 2000 by 1100, 60% down the runway, the map ended at 885 and the frame at 960) and as two vertical lines across it (3440, 75%). The frame is now four edges drawn round the map wherever it is, each stretched along its own length only, so the line stays a pixel; the shots at all four widths and 0, 50, 60, 80 and 100% show no rectangle and no lines. **The panel just barely translucent**: the ink at 97%. Chosen by `SitePaletteContrastTest`, which measures every word and edge on the panel over the band's brightest pixel (pure white ice) through the panel and holds the page to the least whole percent at which none falls below the WCAG level it reaches on the solid ink: the brass eyebrow is AAA there (7.63:1), 7.13 at 97% and 6.98 at 96, so 97. At the AA floor alone the answer was 83%, which a screenshot showed as dark glass, not "just barely"; 97 leaves the map a ghost passing behind. **The data frame** ("The data behind the map"): one 800 by 600 frame at 1:1 over the cards' window and more of it eastward, (2048, 512), the relief at the bottom and the layers faded in and out over it; the cards became its switches, because each already names and explains its layer, and a frame over four pictures of the same ground would double the section's weight and its reading: with a script each card drops its picture (so the four card pictures, 155,872 bytes, are no longer fetched) and its heading's words move into a button with `aria-pressed` that covers the card, the number square ringed while its layer is on; Rivers is a button of its own over the map. Temperature starts on. The layers are the application's own views taken apart by a new `SiteImagery.Cut`: the relief is the winds view's ground without its arrows (the land shaded and tinted by height, the sea flat); temperature whole; rainfall's land and the currents' sea, each clear where its view draws only a placeholder colour; the winds' arrows alone; the rivers and lakes. The engine draws a map in two passes, a colour per cell and then marks at their own width, so the marks (arrows, river courses) come away as a transparent layer for nothing, the same overlay drawn over a clear raster. Borders cannot: the engine blends them into the cells, three-quarters of the way from ground to ink. They were recovered by laying them over two grounds and solving for the ink and its share (41, 32, 24 at 0.750, from 8,424 of the world's 8,539 border cells), but the frame's land is one realm edge to edge, so a border overlay would draw nothing there and was left out. **The lens** ("From continent to valley", a new section after How a world is made): the whole world at a quarter, 1024 by 512 (45,606 bytes, lazy), and a round lens showing the full 4096 by 2048 render (507,604 bytes at quality 60) at one of its pixels to the CSS pixel, by the pointer, by a held finger (280 ms, a sideways slide moves it and an upward one scrolls, `touch-action: pan-y`) and by the arrow keys once the map is focused; until the full picture decodes the lens shows the quarter picture enlarged. The full picture is asked for the first time the lens is shown and never before; a plain link in the caption opens it. The arrow keys are the one default the page prevents, only while the lens has the focus and only for the four arrows; `SiteSourcesTest`'s scroll guard now holds exactly that. **The tilting relief**, in the same section: the eastern island's western end, 640 by 400 at (2720, 470), its heights (`relief-heights.png`, 321 by 201 points one every two sheet pixels, 64,521, under a 16-bit index, sixteen bits a point in quarter metres across red and green, 63,399 bytes; the highest 3,892 m) as a mesh textured with the patch in Natural (34,012 bytes). Heights twenty times their scale: the patch is 1,875 km across at 2.93 km a pixel, so the range at its true scale would stand a five-hundredth of its width, at twenty about a twenty-fourth. Every point carries a normal from its neighbours' heights and the light, from the upper left in the camera's frame, is worked out per pixel from the blended normal, so no triangle shows at any tilt (looked at enlarged at both extremes). Straight down it is the still picture; tilted up to 15 degrees toward the pointer, the view closes in just far enough that the patch's corners stay outside the frame. Frames are asked for only while it is on screen and still settling; nothing is fetched or drawn under reduced motion; any failure (no WebGL, a shader, the heights) and a lost context leave the still. Decoded in the browser the heights matched the file exactly (the same maximum and sum). No window of that size on the island's range avoids everything: this one is clear of the four marks in docs/TODO.md but holds a scarp running nearly straight down a column and a straight-edged delta flat, now listed there. **Every seed is a world**: seeds 7, 42, 1066, 2024 and 31337 (of eight looked at), each generated as the application makes a world opened with only a seed, the defaults at 512, a few seconds each where the author's world at 2048 takes most of a minute; each 512 by 256, half its sheet, 89,594 bytes for the five, lazy; each captioned with its seed and linked with the application's own link, `/app/?seed=N#v=1&size=512&style=natural`. A row: five across from 900 wide, three from 600, two on a phone. Whole worlds carry their ice caps and dry belts. **Weight**, measured as Site 6 did (375 and 1280 wide, one and two device pixels, first and returning visits): at load 1,412,508 bytes transferred on a narrow screen at one device pixel and 1,609,954 on every other, against 1,376,026 and 1,573,472 before, 36,482 more, the page alone (136,906 bytes on disk where it was 100,272); none of the new pictures is fetched at load, and the load guard, restated, sums 1,409,962 and 1,607,408. Largest paint unchanged at 88 to 180 ms except a first visit at 375 wide and two device pixels, 1,744 ms (124 before), measured twice. Scrolling to the foot touching nothing adds 429,863 where it added 256,175. Using the lens adds 507,800; each layer switched on for the first time adds its picture (rainfall 54,656, currents 7,750, winds 16,504, rivers 15,092). Layout shift 0 and no sideways overflow at 375, 768, 1280, 1920, 2560 and 3440, at rest, in use, with motion reduced and with scripts off. **Guards**, each shown failing on a planted defect: `SiteSourcesTest` (an overlay switch without `aria-pressed`; the full picture asked for as the page loads; the relief canvas not hidden in the markup; a reel link with `tabindex="-1"`; the lens preventing every key; the data layers left fading under reduced motion; the relief asking for frames off screen; the relief lit by each triangle's own face); `SitePaletteContrastTest` (the panel at 83%, and at 96%); `SiteAssemblyTest` (a rivers layer that is the whole reading, which also failed the blank check at 100% cover; the full picture asked for as the page loads, which also failed the load guard; the relief's points spaced three where the heights are two; a reel link to a world of another size). `:desktop:siteTest` 26 tests, `SiteSourcesTest` 20 and `SitePaletteContrastTest` 9, passing. |
| Fix big-link A large link asks first | 2026-09-25 | chunk/big-link-prompt | **A link naming a size above the one the window starts at, `Platform.defaultResolution` (512 in a browser, 1024 on the desktop), makes nothing until the reader answers one question.** Before, a 2048 link opened on a phone began about a minute and a half of paused page on arrival, and about four minutes in a desktop browser. Now the window is not armed; a dialog, "A large world", says "This link makes a 2048 world, larger than the 512 this window starts at", then the measured time for that size on the nearest kind of machine, worded as a measurement made elsewhere ("In a browser on a phone that took about a minute and a half when it was measured; this one may be quicker or slower"), or "has not been measured" where there is none, and in a browser that the page may pause. Two buttons name their sizes, "Make it at 2048" and "Open at 512"; the smaller holds the focus, so Enter takes it and Tab reaches the other, and there is no third way out, since closing on no world would leave a blank window that does not say why. The default answer reads the link again at the default size (`WorldLinks.readAtSize`) rather than scaling the link's world down, so every other pair reaches the world by the one path it always does, and drops the line saying a size above the ceiling was brought down, since the reader chose the size. A link at or below the default, or naming no size, opens as before. The kind of machine is the front end (`Platform.hostName`) and, in a browser, the arrangement: compact is taken to be a phone. The times are `LargeLinks.MEASURED`, five rows, each with its source: desktop 2048 60.5 s (`StageProfileTest`, 2026-09-15) and 4096 173.8 s (`ExportAuditTest`, 2026-09-12, when 2048 took 39.8 s, so likely longer now); a desktop browser 2048 about four minutes (Edge 153, 2026-09-25); a phone 1024 18-23 s and 2048 92.7 s (a 2026 Qualcomm handset, WebGPU on). A browser on a desktop at 1024 has no measurement and says so. Guards, each shown failing on a mutation: never asking fails both asking window cases ("the window made the link's world without asking"); the default answer keeping the link's config fails the Enter case (`(718106, 512)` for `(718106, 128)`); asking at the default (`>=`) fails the no-question window case and the common asks case; reading at the default while ignoring the size fails the default-size and the brought-down-line cases. Tests: `:ui:jvmTest` 165, `:ui:wasmJsTest` 163, `:web:wasmJsTest` 41, and 9 in `:desktop:test` across the link, seed-field, import and save-identity cases, all passing. |
| Chunk 5a The ice obeys the switch, and the browser's ice kernel compiles | 2026-09-26 | chunk/5a-ice-switch | **The ice sheet leaves the processor only when the reader has turned graphics acceleration on, and the browser's ice kernel compiles for the first time.** *The switch (Audit III C3).* Erosion and the ocean read `config.erosion.acceleration` where they call their devices; the ice called `accelerator?.sheet` whenever it was handed one, and the application, the desktop platform, the exports through `ExportSubjects` and the browser all hand one over whatever the setting says. So on a machine with a device a default world's sheet was drawn on the card with the switch off, and one seed made different worlds on different machines. `GlaciationStage.apply` now reads the switch beside the call, which covers every caller at once. The reuse chain was checked by running it, not by reading the guards: the switch lives in the erosion section, erosion is recomputed on any change to it and the sea stage (where the ice is) follows the new erosion object, and `IceAcceleratorSwitchTest` shows it both ways (off to on asks the device; on to off asks nothing and gives back the processor's elevation bit for bit). The guard is common, so it runs on the JVM and on Wasm: a counting fake device answering 1,111 m over the sheet, on seed 7 at 128, the smallest of the standard seeds 7, 42, 99 and 1234 to grow a sheet there (170, 235, 338 and 248 sheet cells respectively). On the ungated stage both cases failed on the JVM with one call at CPU; with the gate, none at CPU, at least one at GPU, and the sentinel in every sheet cell and in the world's elevation. The engine's KDoc, `Acceleration`'s, `Platform.iceAccelerator`'s, `DesktopPlatform`'s comment, `docs/INSTALL.md` (which said the processor "produces the same world") and the README now say what the switch runs. *The WGSL (C8, I2).* The ice kernel named a local `from`, a reserved word (WGSL §16.2), so the module never compiled, `getCompilationInfo`'s error made the kernel return null in silence and every browser generation on a machine with WebGPU fell back to the processor after copying four grids across. It is `marginCell` now. The ice's and erosion's WGSL moved out of their JavaScript functions into Kotlin values (`ICE_SHEET_WGSL`, `EROSION_RATES_WGSL`, `EROSION_TRANSFER_WGSL`, listed in `WGSL_MODULES`), handed to the kernels as arguments; the ocean's stays where it is for the chunk rewriting it. `WgslReservedWordsTest` reads every module against the specification's list (146 words, checked against the W3C draft of 21 September 2026): it failed on the kernel as it stood, naming `[from]`, and passes after the rename; its control finds `from` put back and `target` planted in erosion's kernel, and a third case shows comments and numeric suffixes are not taken for names. `WgslCompilesTest` asks a device's compiler and prints executed or skipped: in this machine's headless Chrome 153 under Karma it skips (`navigator.gpu` present, no adapter); CI's headless Chrome has not been observed, and the line in the XML says which after a run. In the desktop Chrome 153 on the same machine the compiler answers `1:45 'from' is a reserved keyword` for a one-line module with `let from` and compiles it renamed. A refused ice module is now reported in the console with the compiler's messages, once, and the device is marked so later calls decline before any grid is copied; that path is read, not seen, since the renamed module compiled. *Parity.* `IceSheetParity` is the synthetic sheet `GpuIceSheetTest` built inline, moved to common code so the browser's `?selftest` measures its device on the same arrays (`kotlin.random`'s generator is the same on every platform). On this machine's NVIDIA card, `GpuIceSheetTest` executed, not skipped: 41,275 sheet cells, worst thickness 0.0020 m apart, 2.78e-7 of the thickest 7,033 m, 5 receivers differ (0.0121%), inside its bars of 1e-6 and 1e-3. The browser's self-test in Chrome 153 on the same card: worst 0.001953125 m, 2.7772833e-7 of 7,032.5 m, 5 receivers of 41,275. *F7, F8.* `IceSheetParityTest`, named in three KDocs, never existed; they name `GpuIceSheetTest` and the fixture. The GLSL's "`precise` throughout" was not: the stored thickness and `surfaceAt`'s sum were outside it, and GLSL's qualifier reaches back only within one function, so both are now formed in `precise` locals. WGSL has no such qualifier and lets an implementation reassociate and fuse (§15.7.5), which `WebGpuIceSheet`'s KDoc now says in place of any claim. Tests, from the XML: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 285, `:worldgen:wasmJsTest` 28, `:web:wasmJsTest` 45, `GpuIceSheetTest` in `:desktop:test` 1 executed, all passing; the switch guard was also shown failing on Wasm on the ungated stage. |
| Site 5c The landing page's review fixes, and every window looked at on the implicit erosion's terrain | 2026-09-26 | chunk/site-5c | Our own CSS, script and markup, no dependency. **Every picture window, on the new terrain** (merged main at edd3746 first). The reel was looked at on fifteen worlds (1, 7, 12, 42, 99, 123, 777, 1066, 2024, 2026, 5000, 31337, 65536, 90210, 424242) and 31337 replaced by 777; seven were left for an ice wall down a column, a slab of ice with straight edges, a fan of rays or a ruled dry belt, and the five kept still show small glacier flats of their own at 512. **The hero's band**: 4096 by 800 at 105,912 bytes (373,698 before) and halved 34,438 (113,314); seams 1.4 and 1.9 against a roughest inner pair of 46.2 and 49.9; the drift unchanged. **The download cards**: all three on the sunk ink with a hairline, and only the reader's own system marked, a brass ring and a Your system tag in ink on brass; no card wears the oxblood, which the application keeps for errors. The tag's text went from For your system to Your system and it wraps under the name (the name line is a wrapping flex row), because on a phone a card's words have about 113 pixels and the old tag, held to the name's line with no break before it, ran 33 pixels past a 360 screen and 18 past a 390 (measured in Chrome, as the review reported). **The data frame's layers as checkboxes**: one native checkbox a layer (temperature, ocean currents, winds, rainfall and rivers), each with its own label and described by its card's own line (aria-describedby), in a fieldset beside the frame on a wide screen and under it on a narrow one; drawn in the page's colours (appearance none, a bone rim, brass fill and an ink tick when ticked, the brass focus ring); Tab reaches each and Space ticks it natively; the frame starts as the ticks in the markup say (temperature), autocomplete off so a returning page does not bring back another state. The aria-pressed buttons and the card headings turned into buttons are gone; with a script the four cards step aside (the checkboxes carry their words), without one the frame and its checkboxes stay hidden and the cards with their pictures are the page. **Wide screens**: from 1100 wide the relief, the style slider and the data frame each take their words and controls in a column beside them (the relief its caption, the slider its two pickers and caption, the frame its checkboxes and caption), rather than being centred or widened: the pictures stay at their own pixels, the controls sit by what they change, and the words keep a reading measure. **Small polish**: the reel is one world a row on a phone and, from 600, two large over three smaller on six columns, each world drawn at its sheet's own pixels, 1024 by 512 (297,230 bytes for the five, 96,912 before), and re-picked as above; the opening's two buttons stack at one width under 600. **The style slider crossfades**: a picked style is fetched and decoded, then put up by the browser's view transition where it has one (the two sides named, the rest of the page not animated), else by the page's own fade of a copy of the old picture over the new (opacity, 0.4 s); at once for a reader who asks for less motion, or while the frame is off the screen; the keyboard's change goes the same way. **The lens on a phone**: held by a finger, it is placed on the screen rather than in the map's frame (position fixed): above the finger where the screen has room, else below, else beside, then kept wholly on the screen, clear of its edges by 8 pixels and of the tray while the tray is up; mouse and keyboard as they were. A hold is told from a swipe as before, by 280 ms within 10 pixels; only while a finger is down is the frame marked pressed, which alone turns off the callout and selection, and only while the lens is held does a non-passive touch listener prevent the scroll and a menu listener the long-press menu; the frame lets vertical pans and now pinches through. Measured under touch: a swipe over the map scrolls 175 to 181 pixels as anywhere, a held finger moved scrolls none and keeps the lens (the old page scrolled 105 and lost it). **Structured data**: one schema.org SoftwareApplication, since it is one program on three systems, with the browser's address as its UseAction; the lede as description, Windows, Linux and a web browser, DesignApplication, free (an Offer at 0; USD is the one word the page does not say, since an Offer needs a currency), the page, the releases and /app/, the link preview as screenshot, the MIT License by name and address, and the repository as the SoftwareSourceCode it is based on; no rating, review, count or person. **A fix found on the way**: the lens's hold constant was declared with the title card's name, HOLD_MS, in the same function, so since Site 5b the card stood 0.28 s instead of 2.5 (1,593 ms from the map shown to the card gone; 3,811 now). **Weight**, measured as Site 6 did (375 and 1280 wide, one and two device pixels, first and returning visits; GitHub blocked): at load 529,820 bytes narrow at one device pixel and 790,204 on every other screen before, 464,037 and 535,512 after, the band's new rows taking off far more than the page's 13,094 bytes of new markup, style and script put on; the load guard restated to what its list sums to, 461,417 and 532,891, each plus 64 KiB, and the band's ceilings to 128 and 40 KiB. Scrolling to the foot touching nothing adds 648,465 where it added 475,220, the reel's larger pictures. Largest paint 128 to 172 ms on every visit measured; on a first visit that is the band under the title card, which now stands its full time, past the three seconds the measure waits (before, with the card gone at 1.6 s, it was 1,728 to 1,732). Layout shift 0 and sideways overflow 0 at 360, 375, 390, 768, 1280, 1920, 2560 and 3440, at rest, in use, with motion reduced and with scripts off. **Guards**, each shown failing first, on the old page (the real defects) and on planted ones: `SiteAssemblyTest` now drives a headless Chrome of its own over the DevTools protocol (`HeadlessChrome`, the JDK's WebSocket and HTTP server, Chrome found where installed or at CARTOGENESIS_CHROME, failing by name where there is none): nothing past the screen or out of the box that clips it at the eight widths, with every tag shown, each card open and scripts off (failed on the old page: the tag 260 to 393 on a 360 screen and out of its card at 360 to 768); the held lens whole on the screen, off the tray, clear of the finger and found by hit-testing at its centre and rim, for presses at the centre, corners and edges with the map at the top and the foot of the screen at 360, 390 and 768 (failed: the lens at -47 to 103 across and -172 to -22 down); a swipe scrolling and a held finger not (failed: scrolled 175 under the held finger); the structured data parsed as JSON (failed on the old page with none, and on a licence named Apache, an address the page does not link, and a rating). `SiteSourcesTest`: checkboxes each with its own label and the card's words, no aria-pressed, the no-script path hiding them (failed on the old page, and on an aria-pressed planted on one); the lens keeping its press only while held (failed on selection off always); the three defaults the page may prevent and its one non-passive listener (failed on the touch listener preventing whether or not held); the crossfade's instant path, its fallback and both stilled for less motion (failed on the less-motion test removed); no constant declared twice (failed on the old page, HOLD_MS). `SitePaletteContrastTest`: the cards alike and only the reader's marked in brass (failed on Windows brass again), no error colour on the page (failed on the Linux card), and the checkbox's rim, hover, fill, tick and focus against what each sits on (failed on a hairline rim, 1.66:1). `:desktop:siteTest` 30 tests, `:desktop:test` 145 (six skipped, none failing) with `SiteSourcesTest` 23 and `SitePaletteContrastTest` 11, all passing. |

| Schoolroom by elevation alone | 2026-09-26 | chunk/schoolroom-elevation | **The Schoolroom style colors the land by elevation and nothing else, as its landing-page card says.** It had followed the climate at full strength (`climateTint` 1: the drought lifting a cell toward the ramp's earth band, the cold paling it toward the paper, the canopy darkening it) and washed the biome's own color over that at 0.12; both are now 0, and the unread muting is held at the paper, as the other two styles with no wash hold it. A land cell is the ramp at its height; the relief shading, the sea's depth ramp and contours, the coast's ink and the lake colors are unchanged. The ramp's eight stops were already on the declared ruler rather than on any world's range: `relativeElevation` on land is meters above the shoreline over `highestLandMetres`, so the stops stand at 0, 857, 1,714, 2,571, 3,429, 4,286, 5,143 and 6,000 m at the stock scale, and were left alone. **Guard:** `SchoolroomElevationTest` holds every dry land cell of the gallery world (seed 234475 at 512), drawn without relief or coastline, to the ramp's color at its height; holds `MapStyle.ground` to the ramp for every biome and every dryness, cold and canopy of 0, 0.5 and 1; and holds the graphics card's recipe to carrying neither lever nor any climate field. On the style before this change all three failed, 98,250 of 98,250 land cells off the ramp; after it, none are. `ClimateTintTest` exempts Schoolroom from the green-desert clause (98.6% of the 5,556 desert cells now read green, as a physical wall map prints a lowland desert) and holds it with Pen and ink and Colour-blind among the styles the climate cannot reach; the steppe clause drops it by its own filter. **Record:** Schoolroom's 512 render fingerprint moves from 411892921 to -4712975; the other eleven do not move. **Looked at**, seeds 42 and 1 at 1024: the lowlands are the ramp's saturated green everywhere, the uplands step through yellow and orange to rust with the snow white only on the highest cells, and the band edges follow the drainage, dendritic up every valley. The dry belt's edge that the climate tint drew straight along a row across seed 42's western continent is gone with the tint.
