| 4a The ocean's circulation | 2026-09-26 | chunk/4a-ocean-circulation | **The gyres are Stommel's, solved on the ground at a spacing the planet's physics sets, and the sea's temperature is the steady balance of what they carry, what the eddies spread and what the air takes back; the square-grid design's ocean step (its Q3) is done here.** **Why.** Audit III's Run 1 found the old solve a plain Poisson equation with no β: its gyres were symmetric east to west, turned against the wind at 35 to 55 degrees on seeds 7, 42, 59758 and 969495, carried polar water down each basin's eastern side, and were solved on a coarse grid whose water was a majority over fixed blocks of cells. **The circulation** (`OceanCirculation`, `OceanStage`). `r∇²ψ + βψ_x = curl τ / (ρH)`, ψ a velocity stream function in m²/s (`u = -ψ_y`, `v = ψ_x`), with β `2Ω cos φ / a` from the true latitude on a flat plate carrée, `r` the bottom drag β(30°) × 50 km = 9.91e-7 s⁻¹, `H` 500 m, and the belts' and regional wind's stress quadratic, `C_D` 1.2e-3 at an assumed representative 10 m wind for Large and Pond's law. The radius and the spin come from `WorldScale` alone (`radiusMeters`, `ROTATION_RATE_PER_S`, shared with `PressureWind`), and nothing is counted in cells. The x-diffusion is exponentially fitted, so the stencil stays monotone at `βΔx/2r` up to 1.55; a cell-centered multigrid V(2,2) of red-black Gauss-Seidel with the poles as walls converges in eight cycles to a residual of 1e-3. The solve grid is `4πΩ/r` rows, 925 rounded to 960 by 1,920, square on the ground and two cells to the narrowest Stommel layer, the same for every map size; a solve cell is water only where every map cell under it is, so a strip of land one map cell wide is land there at every size. **The heat** (`OceanHeat`). `u·∇T - K∇²T + (T - T_lat)/τ = 0` on the same grid, Scharfetter-Gummel fluxes, face velocities from ψ's corners so the discrete flow is divergence-free, nothing through a coast or a pole, solved by BiCGSTAB with the V-cycle and a per-water-body mean correction as its preconditioner, fourteen iterations. `K` is 2,500 m²/s at 45 degrees and goes as `1/|sin φ|` from `K = V R_d` (Zhurbas and Oh's drifters), capped at their 10,000 along the equator, reached at 10.2 degrees; τ is the slab's 2.0e8 / 25 = 8e6 s, 93 days, a relaxation against fixed air. The path integral, the fine-grid and fixed-fraction forms measured in Phase 1 and the `heatGrid` setting are gone. The faint cell-scale staircase at the cold tongue's edge was the path integral's stop at the solve grid's nearest land cell, and went with it. **The anomaly** is measured from the energy balance's profile plus the water's mean departure from it over a Gaussian band of latitude, σ = √(Kτ), 140 km at 45 degrees: the one-row mean it replaces stepped 0.1 C a row against the water's 0.04 where fifty coastal cells left a row on 969495 at 2048, a straight line across every ocean. The rows no longer average exactly to zero: 0.08 to 0.13 C RMS, at most 0.29 to 0.59, and the whole ocean -0.009 to -0.012 C by area; `OceanCurrentTest` holds the heat those stand for at 0.07 to 1.05% of the balance's transport across 30, 45 and 60 degrees, under its own 2% miss against Trenberth and Caron, and the whole ocean under the 0.05 C the balance sets its mean to. **The device** (`OceanAccelerator`; `GpuOcean`, GLSL with `precise`; `WebGpuOcean`, its WGSL a Kotlin constant free of reserved words) relaxes both problems and agrees with the processor to the bit at aspects 1.0 and 0.5 (`GpuOceanTest`), but is slower (5.0 to 6.7 s against 1.7 to 2.8), recorded in `TODO.md`. The save format is 15. **Guards, each shown failing first**: the discrete basin against Stommel's analytic one, 1.7e-5 and 1.0e-4 of the largest ψ at aspects 1.0 and 0.5 (5.06 with β removed); the interior at Stommel's -0.01379 m/s and the western return at his 42,770 m²/s (with β removed, -2.75e-4 m/s); isotropy on the ground, a localized source spreading 794 km both ways at aspect 1.0 and 749 at 0.5, where the old equal weights on 2:1 cells give 917 against 610; twice the radius doubles the Stommel layer (15.19 to 30.37 km) and holds the Sverdrup interior (x1.0000), where a radius left fixed moves the layer x1.003; the heat carried alike east, west, north and south (decay 844 to 848 km against 847), where the old truncating carry split east from west by 10 C; no heat across a strip of land one cell wide (under 2e-5 C; 5.88 C when land conducts); the strip kept land on the solve grid at 128, 512 and 2048 (960 rows open when the mask is read at cell centers); the heat solve on a difficult fixture in four iterations, one iteration stopping at 0.074; a cancelled generation never falls back to the processor (the device asked 264 times when it did); the ground identical with the currents and without (`OceanLeavesTheGroundTest`); the gyres' senses on the four standard worlds and 969495, with the trades' band held on the belts' own wind because Sverdrup's `u = (x_E - x) ∂(F/β)/∂y` is westward from 0 to 15 degrees under them (the curl reversed fails it); the anomaly's reference smooth across a coast ending on a row (0.05 C a row; 0.70 C with a one-row mean); the double count above (6.4% on seed 7 when the band averages the temperature itself); and on 969495 at 2048 the open ocean's coldest west coast at 20.0 S, inside Earth's 15 to 35, and its warmest water poleward of 45 against a basin's eastern shore. **What moved.** Seed 42 at 512: the anomaly's 5th to 95th percentile from -2.37..3.45 C to -1.31..1.09, 1st and 99th -5.29 and +1.65, 1.09% of the water beyond 5 C, coldest -13.8 C at the confluence, where a boundary current crosses twenty degrees of latitude in 15 to 40 days, inside τ; and it no longer depends on the map's size, -5.39 and +1.68 at 1024, where before the span halved from 512 to 1024 (-1.69 and +2.19). The coldest subtropical west coast (`MoistureBudgetTest`) moved from 41.7, 34.6, 30.1 and 41.7 degrees to 26.9, 40.3 S, 34.3 and 41.7 on seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99; the marine inversion still moves those coasts' rain by -0.2 to +0.4% (-0.1% pooled, +0.1% before), which chunk 4b takes with the upwelling and the coast's reach. The cold-season sea ice reaches 43.1, 41.7 and 44.8 degrees; `SeaIceTest`'s bar, 45 under the Okhotsk's own 44, is re-derived to the Bohai Sea's 37 (Yan et al. 2024). Re-recorded: D I-9's windward coasts 3576 and 4042 mm to 3560 and 4035; the recycling ratio 0.296 to 0.298; seed 7's water 0.8455% to 0.8475%. `CurrentFeedsRainTest`'s seed is chosen by geography, not by its own floors, which makes it seed 7, whose cold coast is six cells, recorded. The 512 census arms every clause of the anomaly running along rows; the 2048 census, re-taken for the first time since Fix 2, carries Fix 3's, Fix 3b's and chunk 6's ground with this climate, and five layers' new violations there were given findings. The render records, the gzip fixture and the interoperability saves are re-taken. **Cost** on seed 42, whole stage: 2.8 s at 512 and 2.7 s at 2048 on all threads, 3.3 and 3.8 s on one thread at 512 and 1024 (0.45 and 1.24 before), 10.5 and 12.0 s in Wasm (0.88 and 2.83 before); `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`. |
| 4a review round | 2026-09-26 | chunk/4a-ocean-circulation | **A second reader's review, each claim checked against the code before anything changed; what the 4a row above says of `K`, of the anomaly's row means and of the 2048 census is superseded here.** **The heat at the coast.** Confirmed: a face corner that touched land was the mean of its four cells with land counted as zero, so it was not zero, and a face shut at the coast carried a velocity the heat never saw; the open faces' flux then did not sum to zero over a coastal cell, and the advective form made or lost heat there. A temperature uniform everywhere cannot show it, since the advective form carries a constant as a constant, so the guard (`OceanHeatTest`, a closed irregular basin with an island, a current whose ψ is nowhere zero beside the coast and a temperature that varies across it) holds the carrying terms summed over the basin to rounding and the basin's budget with the air to the solve's tolerance: -0.0051 of their size and 5.8% of the exchange with the averaged corners; 5e-11 and 4e-8 at aspect 1.0 and 2e-10 and 7e-7 at 0.5 with the fix, and a uniform temperature back to within 6e-6 C with the corners either way. The fix: a corner touching land takes the coast's ψ, zero, which the circulation holds on every land body, so each shut face has both corners on the coast and no flow through it. Every land body at zero means no circulation around an island; Godfrey's island rule, which would give each island a constant of its own, is not in the model. **The ground, against the base.** 19 digests of the terrain, the plates, the erosion and the sea (`ReachableState.digestsByBranch`) on seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99 at 512 and 969495 at 2048, built as the app builds them, identical between 3a66025 and this head, 94 of 94; no test pinned an elevation checksum before (`PipelineTest` compares two runs of one tree). `OceanLeavesTheGroundTest` now pins seed 42's, and fails with the currents let into the erosion's climate. **A solve that fails.** Production used both solves' answers whatever they reached, and a NaN residual compares false with any tolerance. `OceanCirculation.requireSolved` now asks each for a finite residual under its tolerance and finite values, and throws `OceanSolveFailure` naming the solve: on the processor the stage fails, because the processor is the reference and a stopped solve's field looks like an ocean and is not one, and a world saved with it would carry the wrong sea for its seed; relaxed on a device, the ocean is solved again on the processor, as for a device that declines. Guards (`OceanAcceleratorTest`): relaxation that makes NaN fails the stage by name; a device that answers NaN leaves the reference answer to the bit; a stopped solve fails and a finished one passes; all three fail with the check disabled. **The residual guard.** `OceanCurrentTest` now asks every figure to be a number (a NaN whole-ocean mean fails it, and passed before), and states its bars as chosen tolerances, not derived bounds: 2% of the balance's transport, small beside the balance's own misses against Trenberth and Caron (13, 2 and 3% at 30, 45 and 60 degrees), and a whole-ocean mean under 0.05 C, the closeness to which the balance is asked to set its global mean. They read 0.03 to 1.27% and -0.012 to -0.015 C. **`K` near the equator.** The 10,000 m²/s cap was a limiter, and not an observed maximum either. `K = V R_d` with `R_d = min(c/|f|, sqrt(c/2β))`, the equatorial radius within reach of the equator (Chelton et al. 1998); one wave speed `c` for the whole column, 2.64 m/s, from Chelton's near-equatorial 240 km and Earth's β, and `V` 0.098 m/s from Zhurbas and Oh's 2,500 m²/s at 45 degrees. No cap: the equator's `K` is 23,500 m²/s on Earth, inside the drifters' 2-3 × 10⁴ in the eastern equatorial Pacific, and 12,800 at this generator's radius, with β; the two radii meet at 4.3 and 7.9 degrees. Guard: `K` at 45 degrees on both radii, finite everywhere, falling poleward of the meeting, Chelton's 240 km read back and the square-root scaling with the radius; the cap put back reads 102 km. The anomaly's band σ at the equator goes from 283 to 320 km. **The census at 2048, attributed** by running it on 3a66025: the new findings' marks that predate 4a, the same on the base to the last digit, are 42's drawn coast's square corners (0.314 per 1,000 cell widths), 1234's and 99's ice-sheet ground arcs (124.9 and 125.3 degrees), 99's terrain crease (12.08) and 969495's lake arc (233.5). Moved by 4a's climate, base against head: the drawn ice's straight runs, 42 clean against 199.0 cell widths, 969495 clean against 215.0, 1234 179.3 against 188.6, 718106 273.0 against 189.0, 99's row-bearing preference clean against 1.647 (`ICE_EDGE_ALONG_A_ROW`), and 59758's cleared, 1.555 against clean; 59758's peoples' borders clean against two arcs of about 123 degrees (`PEOPLES_BORDER_ARC`, new) and its realm borders clean against one of 127.5 (`REALM_BORDER_ARC`); the isotherms' preference moves in the third decimal. Recorded as known failures and in `TODO.md`, the ice's for the ice chunks. **What the round moved**, re-recorded: the recycling ratio 0.298 to 0.299; the rain sample's cold coast 6 to 9 cells, the coupling there -2.12% and +0.85% on the warm; the sea ice's edge 42.7, 41.3 and 43.4 degrees; seed 42 at 512, the anomaly's 1st and 99th percentiles -5.48 and +1.72 C, 1.18% of the water beyond 5 C, coldest -14.5 C, the same geometry as before, a boundary current crossing twenty degrees inside τ; 969495 at 2048, -5.72 and +2.29, its open ocean's coldest west coast 2.97 C at 20.0 S; the rows' mean anomaly 0.09 to 0.14 C RMS, at most 0.32 to 0.64; the warm coasts' lead 7.6, 10.2 and 8.3%, 8.7% pooled; the marine inversion -0.2 to +0.2%, nothing pooled. The render records, the gzip fixture and the interoperability saves are re-taken. `CurrentFeedsRainTest`'s floors are counted in cells, recorded in `TODO.md` for the square grid. |
| Water colors Rivers and lakes as one water, and the dark channel | 2026-09-27 | chunk/water-colors | **The dark channel on the opening band** (seed 1 at 2048, the Natural style) was not a river: it is a channel of sea one cell wide that `SeaLevelStage.drainDrownedBasins` cuts from an inland arm of the sea to the coast along the southern foot of the east-west range, and the coast was inked along both of its banks, the raster on the landward cells and the traced line centred on each boundary, so nothing of the water was left and it drew as a near-black line darker than every river. Neither the relief shading (rivers are stroked over the finished raster) nor a lake color was involved. It is now drawn as what it is: `NarrowSea` calls a sea cell open only where it belongs to a block of sea wider than one coast pen both ways on the true-shape sheet (`MapRasterizer.coastPenPixels`: 1 by 2 cells at 512, 2 by 3 at 2048), and the rest is a bank to the coast, so the raster, the traced line and the shader all draw the coast round the open water and across a channel's mouth, and the channel is the ocean ramp's color at its depth, the width of a river. The same rule turns the drowned-valley inlets up every sunken coast from inked tufts into water. The graphics card reads it from the one land byte (0 open sea, 1 land, 2 narrow sea), having no binding to spare; 15 views by 12 styles still differ from the processor at a 99.9th percentile of 0 and a worst channel of 1, in 0.0007% of pixels. **Rivers and lakes are one water in every style**: each style's lake shallows take its river color, and a lake 500 m deep is that color shaded to 0.70, the step Atlas drew between its two lake colors before (0.60, 0.73 and 0.78 by channel); `lakeDeep` is derived rather than declared. Before, the lake stood 3.5 to 75.5 CIEDE2000 from the river in eleven styles, and in three (Verdant, Natural, Colour-blind) it was the sea's own color exactly. Rivers changed in Vellum (#6E5B3C to #7A6644), Ink wash (#44505A to #5E6B75) and Nautical (#3E6E8C to #5E8FAD), each lightened so a lake of it is not a blot; the other rivers are as they were, and Pen and ink is untouched (one ink, its lakes ruled in it). On the gallery world at 512, against the style's own rendered land at its tenth percentile, rivers and lakes read 31.1 (Atlas), 12.2 (Vellum), 12.7 (Ink wash), 29.7 (Nautical), 44.7 (Midnight), 44.3 (Schoolroom), 23.3 (Verdant), 13.5 (Scroll), 5.5 (Mars), 27.9 (Natural) and 42.0 (Colour-blind), and lakes stand 8.2 to 53.4 from the style's shallowest sea, against a bar of 5, where two colors part at a glance. **The lake is drawn over the rivers**: the overlay carries the lakes' open water as runs of cells and the front end clips the strokes out of them. A course already ended at the shore, but at 2048 a wide stroke beside a lake reached two pixels into it (71 pixels on seed 1). What looked like a river across a lake on the styles slider was a river along a lake's one-cell arms, which carry its line by design (`LakeResult.openWater`): dark ink over pale water until the two were one color. **Guards**: `WaterColorTest` (one water in every style, and the contrasts above) failed on the palette before for eleven styles and four contrasts (Vellum's lake on land 2.7; Verdant's, Natural's and Colour-blind's lakes 0.0 from the sea). `WaterDrawnAsWaterTest`: every narrow sea cell away from a mouth keeps some of its water, 27 of 27 inked over at 512 and 2,295 of 2,298 in the band's window before, none after; no river pixel inside a lake's open water beyond one pixel of its shore, 0 of 5,301 at 512 before and after, and on seed 1 at 2048 two pixels deep before the clip and none after (the 2048 case in the audit tier). `GeneralisationTest` holds the raster and the traced coast to agree round the banks. **Records**, re-taken on the tree merged with 4a: every style's 512 render moves; the geometry guard's inked-coast figures move as east- and south-facing shores are drawn whole (at 512 from 3.2-3.8 to 3.9-4.7, at 2048 from 4a's 2.8-3.1 to 3.4-4.4). Of 4a's attributions on the drawn coast at 2048, three clear: 1234's square notch at (468, 1181), 42's corner rate of 0.3136 per thousand cell widths (both under the square-notch finding, which is kept attributed in case one returns) and 99's arc at (152, 1037). 99's drawn coast now shows the land's own straight edge down a column, 111 steps at (879, 212), recorded under the straight-coast finding. The drawn coast's isotropy at 2048 drops back under its coverage floor on six of the seven worlds (216 to 419 blocks of chords against the 482 it needs), as it was before 4a's census, and is listed as too small to measure. The 512 and 2048 censuses are green on the merged tree. **Looked at**, all twelve styles, on the band and on the styles slider's window, before and after: the band's channel is a thin line of shelf water, the slider's lakes are the rivers' own water with their dendritic arms reading as one river system, and the one-cell inlets are pale water rather than black tufts. |
| 4b-1 The sea's wind and its upwelling | 2026-09-28 | chunk/4b1-sea-wind | **The sea is forced by the belts' annual wind with its meridional leg, and cooled where its Ekman transport raises water from beneath; the subtropical highs were built as a solved pressure, measured, taken off and made the atmosphere's own chunk.** **The pressure, solved and set aside.** `PressureResponse` (commit b50119c, with `PressureResponseTest`) solved the steady, linear, damped single-layer response of the lower troposphere to the air column's temperature, `αΦ - ∇·(c²a∇Φ) + c²(∂b/∂y ∂Φ/∂x - ∂b/∂x ∂Φ/∂y) = αΦ_eq`, `a = ε/(ε²+f²)`, `b = f/(ε²+f²)`, land's and sea's drags (5.8 and 3.2 hours) and nothing dropped, as an advection-diffusion problem on the ocean's multigrid: `c = NH/π` = 31.8 m/s, `α` the surface exchange over the air below 500 hPa, 2.44 days. Verified: a forced strip's reaches at 30 degrees 1,579 and 859 km against the analytic 1,580 and 851; a cooled disc's centroid 562 km west against the drift over α of 566, and 284 against 285 at twice the radius, at aspects 1.0 and 0.5. On the standard seeds it gave 3 to 8 hPa a half-year, a quarter to a third of its forcing, and an annual mean near zero, since land and sea have nearly the same annual temperature; the curl's zero line bent less than under main's regional wind. By the maintainer's decision the ocean keeps main's regional wind and the highs go to the atmosphere chunk (docs/TODO.md, "Build the atmosphere, so the subtropical highs are real", which now owns the gyres' fronts). **The belts** (`SurfaceBelts`): one copy of the edges and one annual vector, the zonal cosine and a meridional leg `ClimateConfig.meridionalWindShare` = 0.15 of it on the ground, which the march spends as the same float 0.3 rows a cell on an N by N grid; migrated by the tilt and averaged, the profile was smoothed twice, gave the westerlies' stress two humps and its curl three zeros, sent the westerlies' band west on seeds 7 and 42 and stalled the circulation's solve, so it is not migrated. **Upwelling.** Ekman's damped transport `M = τ(r - if)/(ρ(r² + f²))`, r = (2 days)⁻¹ (Zebiak and Cane 1987), diverges on faces with none through a coast and renews the 50 m mixed layer at `w⁺/h`: 0.3 to 2.4 m² s⁻¹ a meter of the subtropics' eastern shores. On the equator the analytic damped divergence under a uniform 0.083 N/m² is 15.9 m/day (`OceanUpwellingTest`'s fixture, where the grid gives 15.88); under the belts' own annual stress the grid's two rows beside the equator rise at 15.4 m/day against that 15.9, and sank at 13.1 until the trades' leg was made continuous there (below). The risen water is a stated closure: off the equator, the winter mixed layer where the isopycnal 100 m down (Weeks, Losch and Tziperman 2023's 41 to 182 m) outcrops by Luyten, Pedlosky and Stommel's eastern-boundary geometry, `sin φ_o = sin φ / (1 - D/H)`, H 500 m, no further than the gyre's edge at 45; on the equator, within twice the deformation radius `sqrt(c/2β)` (131 km here), the trades tilt a thermocline, `h² = H² + (2/ρg')(I - I₀)`, with `I₀` set so each basin's warm layer keeps its volume, its mean `h` along the run H = 150 m (Zebiak and Cane), the surfaced end clipped inside that search, `g' = c²/H` from 4a's 2.64 m/s, and the water beneath it, drawn through a 25 m half-thickness, is the subtropical cells' (McCreary and Lu 1994): the isopycnal at its 200 m base beneath 15 degrees, outcropping at 25.5. Density is temperature's alone. **Nothing rises from below the floor**: the source depth is at most the deepest floor within the shelf's width, 75 km (`SeaConfig.shelfWidthKm`), falling off beyond it with the same length, since over a shelf the upwelled water climbs from the shelf's edge along the bottom (Lentz and Chapman 2004), in the outcrop mapping and the equatorial blend alike; the distance is measured through the map's water alone (`FastMarchingDistance`), since that bottom flow comes through connected water. The share of the rising water from beneath the mixed layer, `s`, rises linearly from none where that depth lies within the 50 m mixed layer to all at the full source depth, and only that share renews the layer, at `s w⁺/h` toward the risen water: the rest is the column's own water and changes nothing. A 40 m basin with no deeper water now keeps its coldest cell 2.25 C above its rows' coldest own water at 30 N and 2.46 at 70, where the closure without the floor put it 3.02 C below, and a 1,000 m basin at 30 N still cools 3.02 C below it. The cell's own floor alone was tried first, as the fix was specified, and it ended coastal upwelling on every coast, the shelf being 30 m deep at the shore: seed 7's cold coast went from 375 km to 23, and the coldest eastern-boundary water on seeds 42 and 1234 moved to the equatorial band's edge. Seed 99's sea at 84.6 N, 75 m deep on its mean and 210 m at its deepest, read an anomaly of -5.53 C against -0.87 without the upwelling while its risen water was the ice's own winter surface; with the water bounded by the freezing point and renewed only in its open months (a quarter of its year), it reads -1.05, and the polar seas poleward of 60 on the four standard seeds are 0.08 to 0.22 C colder with the upwelling than without. `OceanConfig.upwelling` is the switch; the save format is 16 with `meridionalWindShare`, the gzip fixture and both interoperability saves regenerated. **The circulation's residual** is taken in double precision from the single-precision values: summed in single precision it stood at 1.05e-3 of the largest balance on seeds 42 and 99 at 512 and 42 at 1024 under the migrated belts, above the tolerance; now 12, 102 and 12 cycles. **The second reading's five corrections**, each in this chunk's own code and each with a guard in `OceanUpwellingTest` shown failing on 62627c9's ocean first. (1) *The column's own water is neutral.* The layer was renewed at `w⁺/h` toward the latitude's annual water blended with the risen water by `s`, so where `s` was zero the rise still relaxed a warm current's anomaly toward the zonal water; it is now `s w⁺/h` toward the risen water alone. Guard: a 40 m basin with a gyre carrying a 9.1 C warm anomaly and 5,902 rising cells, upwelling on against off: 3.07 C apart before, 0.0000 after. (2) *Liquid water, and only its open months.* The risen water was the energy balance's coldest month at its outcrop, which where the sea freezes is the ice's surface, two meters' capacity, down to -25.4 C; it is now bounded by the freezing point of sea water, -1.8 C, a physical bound. And the renewal acts only on the liquid layer, open to the air for the share `o` of the year the balance's water is above freezing (the balance now records that share and the water's mean over those steps, `T_open`, per band, from its own last year's 360 steps, the climate stage's own freezing test). A stated closure: in its open months the layer relaxes to `T_open` at `1/τ` and is renewed at `e = s w⁺/h`, so the year's mean moves by `o eτ/(1 + eτ)(T_risen - T_open)` with the ice months left as they were; the annual equation's renewal is taken at `R = o e` toward `T_annual + (T_risen - T_open)(1 + oeτ)/(1 + eτ)`, whose steady state is exactly that shift, so a sea frozen all year has no term, one never frozen is renewed at `e` toward the risen water, and a year-round anomaly is damped only while the sea is open. Weighting the rate by `o` toward the risen water itself was rejected: measured against the annual mean, whose ice months sit below freezing, it warms an ice-covered sea toward -1.8. Guards: the coldest risen water over a world with water at every latitude, -25.41 C at 89.9 degrees before and -1.800 after; a basin 1,000 m deep from 55 to 85 N, 141 of its rows frozen for part of the year, spanned -22.80 to 2.61 C before against the -17.41 to 5.18 its open months allow, and -13.65 to 2.61 after. (3) *The trades' leg turns through zero at the equator.* The Hadley leg was `∓0.15 |u|` with `|u|` greatest on the equator, so `τ_y` reversed between the two rows beside it and its down-wind transport `τ_y/(ρr)` converged there; it is now the year's mean direction under the ITCZ's migration by the model's own tilt, `-(2/π) asin(φ/T)` inside `±T` (`SurfaceBelts.hadleyLegNorth`), continuous at `±T`, the energy balance's declination being a sinusoid of the year. The 30- and 60-degree edges and the poles were confirmed to vanish with `u` already; the march's slant does not read the leg and is unchanged (`MeridionalWindTest`). A world with no migration keeps the reversal (docs/TODO.md). Guard: the rows beside the equator under the belts' annual stress, -13.10 m/day against the analytic +16.07 before, +15.36 against 15.89 after, off by 3.33% against a bar of 3.86%: the leg's own convergence where it turns, `(2/π) s r/(βT)`, 2.17%, the damping's `f²` across the first cell, 1.19%, and half a percent. (4) *The shelf reaches through water.* The distance to deep water was jump-flooded through land; it is fast-marched through the map's water (`FastMarchingDistance`, the eikonal equation's upwind solution, exact for a straight front at any bearing, cells joined only through their sides), in kilometers on the ground. Guard: a 60 m sea behind a 23 km isthmus from 1,000 m water drew on 100 m before and 60 after; opened, it draws on 100. It costs 62 to 117 ms, 3.0 to 4.1% of the stage, at 512, 1024 and 2048: one pass over the map, on the processor only. (5) *The tilt keeps the warm layer's volume.* Subtracting the stress integral's mean kept the basin's mean `h²`, not its mean `h`, and the clip broke even that; the level is now found by halving so the run's mean `h` is `H`, the clip inside the search. Guard, the tilt test extended: a 6,250 km basin's mean depth 148.46 m before and 150.0000 after, and 150.0000 with 41 columns surfaced under three times the stress. Measured on the tongue by undoing each correction in turn on the four standard seeds at 512: the volume-keeping tilt alone moves the equator's thirds, deepening the thermocline so less is drawn from beneath, and undoing it gives 62627c9's 2.19, 2.28 and 1.98 C back; the leg's continuity moves them by 0.02 C at most, the band's total divergence being the same and only its rows' shares of it moving. **Recorded as they stand and not changed here:** the march's slant is `share × cellWidth/cellHeight`, which keeps an N by N grid's 0.3 exactly and changes a rectangular grid's on purpose, for the square-grid chunk, every pinned digest being N by N; the rainfall blur's width-derived radius is 4b-2's (O5); and the tongue bar's tie to `τL` stays approximate. **Guards, each shown failing first:** `OceanUpwellingTest`, a coast's rise equals the offshore transport to 0.14 to 1.25% at 0 and 30 degrees to the grid, both aspects, both hemispheres and two radii (zero with the transport let through the coast), a poleward wind raises nothing, the equator's damped divergence to 0.3%, the trades' tilt to 1e-4 (no tilt with no stress), the outcrop mapping, the risen water never warmer than the open months' water it replaces (the warmest month is), a patch's steady mix (the latitude's own without the term); `ColdWaterPlacementTest`, set from Earth before the worlds were read: the coldest subtropical eastern-boundary water within the four systems' 10.15 to 42.31 degrees (Abrahams, Schlegel and Smit 2021), all eight hemispheres: -3.35 C at 36.4 N and -4.24 at 29.4 S on seed 7, -2.39 at 26.5 N and -2.68 at 21.3 S on 42, -3.24 at 33.6 N and -2.01 at 18.1 S on 1234, -2.06 at 29.7 N and -1.78 at 17.1 S on 99 (after the corrections; before them -3.43, -4.31, -3.20, -1.99 and -2.05 where these differ), against 3875e7a's -1.56 at 36.4 N on 42 and -1.21 at 29.7 N on 99; its search was clarified after the worlds were read to start outside twice the equatorial deformation radius, 7.9 degrees, once the equator's tongue met a shore at 5.1 N on seed 42. And an equatorial basin colder in its east than its west by `(2/3) × 4 C × τL / (τ_P L_P)`, the Pacific's 4 C (Karnauskas and others 2009) and 0.040 N/m² over 17,800 km (Wyrtki and Meyers as McPhaden and Taft 1988 quote it), corrected after the worlds were read from a length-only scaling that left the stress out of the tilt. **Recorded:** that bar fails on seeds 7, 42 and 99, 1.77, 2.01 and 1.72 C against 2.84, 3.21 and 3.38 (2.19, 2.28 and 1.98 before the tilt kept the warm layer's volume; 1.17, 1.31 and 1.14 before the deep water was taken from the thermocline's base), and passes on 1234 at 0.73 against 0.20, so no seed newly passes and none is newly armed: the one-layer closure's water beneath the thermocline is warmer than Earth's, and the regional wind's equatorial gales, 1.3 to 1.8 N/m² in some basins, inflate `τL` (TODO.md). **Armed:** `CurrentFeedsRainTest`'s cold coast, 246 km of seed 7's west coast 0.8 C under its latitude against a floor of 117.2 km (258 before the corrections), its floors now kilometers (the ten and five cells at 512). **Re-recorded, the climate moved by the sea the march draws from:** D I-9's windward coasts 3560 and 4035 to 3548 and 4042 mm, and to 3547 and 4039 after the corrections, which moved the sea near the coasts a little; the equatorial bar's signature, for the tilt, as above; the recycling ratio held at 0.299, both cases, having read 0.300 before the floor was read; seed 7's water share 0.8475% to 0.8445%; the 512 census, seed 7's isotherms from two straight facets to one of 188.0 cell widths at (254,60), seed 99's drawn ice armed off its row, seeds 1234's and 42's realm borders now measured for rectangles and seed 99's anomaly no longer; all twelve styles' 512 records together, and ten of them again once the floor was read and again after the corrections, Schoolroom's and Clear's not moving with it; the 512 census did not move with the corrections. The eastern-boundary audit clause (`OceanCurrentAuditTest`) takes the same band and search; 969495 at 2048 reads -4.24 C at 25.1 N and -3.35 at 28.3 S, measured before the corrections; that audit was not run again after them. **The ground holds:** `OceanLeavesTheGroundTest`, both cases. **Cost** on seed 42: the ocean stage 1.0 to 1.9 s on two threads at 512 and 1.7 to 1.9 at 2048, 1.9 to 2.9 s on one thread at 512 and 2.1 to 2.8 at 1024, 4.8 s in Wasm at 512, 3.0 to 3.5 s on the device, which agrees to the bit, the heat stencil with upwelling included; after the corrections the device's whole stage agrees with the processor's to the bit at 512 and 2048 (3.4 to 3.8 s against 1.2 to 1.9), and `GpuOceanTest`'s five tests executed on the card, its six batch cases at a worst of 0.0; docs/PERFORMANCE.md. **Renders**, before (upwelling off) and after, seeds 7 and 42 at 512 and 1024 in the Currents, Winds and Temperature views and 969495 at 2048 in the anomaly: the after ocean carries cold fringes down the subtropics' eastern shores and a cold patch on each equatorial basin's eastern end, the warm streaks of the open subtropics fade, the winds are the march's and unchanged; with the floor read, seed 99 and 42 at 1024 again, the inland seas without deep water lose the dark blue the first closure gave them; and after the corrections, seeds 42 and 99 at 1024 once more, the upwelling picture with its color legend: the rising band on each equator is continuous where a sinking line one row wide ran down its middle, and the anomaly is otherwise as it was. Tests: 316 in `:worldgen:jvmTest`, 42 in `:worldgen:wasmJsTest`, 150 in `:desktop:test` (6 skipped), 125 in `:cartography:jvmTest`, 45 in `:web:wasmJsTest`, none failing, before the floor was read; after it, the ocean, placement and moved-record classes of `:worldgen:jvmTest` (38 tests) and `:cartography:jvmTest`'s census and style records, none failing; after the corrections, fourteen classes of `:worldgen:jvmTest` (upwelling, placement, currents, ground, rain, the moved records, planet size, sea ice, energy balance, incremental reuse and meridional wind; 58 tests), `GpuOceanTest` in `:desktop:test` (5, executed on the card) and `:cartography:jvmTest`'s census and style records (6), none failing once the records were re-taken. **On main, merged,** the full tiers once: 318 in `:worldgen:jvmTest`, 42 in `:worldgen:wasmJsTest`, 153 in `:desktop:test` (6 skipped), 30 in `:desktop:siteTest`, 125 in `:cartography:jvmTest`, 47 in `:cartography:wasmJsTest`, 168 in `:ui:jvmTest`, 163 in `:ui:wasmJsTest`, 45 in `:web:wasmJsTest`, none failing; `OceanCurrentAuditTest` at 2048 passes, 969495's coldest west-coast water unchanged by the corrections at -4.24 C at 25.1 N; and the 2048 geometry census, re-taken where the upwelling's climate moved it and nowhere else: the drawn ice's straight edge on 42 (199.0 to 217.0 cell widths), 1234 (188.6 to 199.5), 718106 (two runs to one of 183.0) and 969495 (two to one of 176.4), and 99's drawn ice preferring the row, 1.647 to 1.736; the isotherms' straight facets on 1234 and 969495 one more each, on 59758 one fewer; 59758's peoples' borders' two arcs shifted a cell; 59758's realm-border arc gone, so that clause is armed; and the sea temperature anomaly on 42 and 99 and the realm borders on 969495 no longer hold a ring large enough to measure for rectangles. The ice edges and isotherms are the zonal climate's known failures, which the upwelling moves where it changes the sea by an ice edge. |
| Q1 The ground's figures, stated on the ground | 2026-09-28 | chunk/q1-ground-figures | **The first chunk of the switch to square cells (2N by N): `WorldGenConfig.forRows`, and two guards that a world of square cells is today's world on the ground. No bit of any world moves. Every restatement of a figure counted in cells moves some world, so all of them go to Q2.** **Why none are restated here.** The fingerprint's 128 by 128 world is built directly, as `WorldGenConfig(42, 128, 128)`, and a world built directly at any grid but 512 by 512 keeps the 512 grid's counts on its own cells. At 128 the tectonics' 26 cells are 2,437 km where they are 609 at 512, and the coast's reach, `OceanConfig.coastalReachCells`, is 937 km where it is 234. Stated in kilometers at the 512 values, the tectonic widths keep every world made through `atResolution`, which is every world the application makes, because `km / cellWidthKm` is exact at power-of-two widths. They move the 128 by 128 fingerprint world and every world a test builds directly at another grid. The other counts, the coast's reach, the routing's smooth field, the landmarks' volcanic reach, the realms' imperial and city-state areas, the shortest trunk, also move every world made at another size, the application's included. The glaciation's two window ceilings move the worlds made 4,096 wide. Q2 moves every test world onto square cells anyway, so it carries both. Its proof is that after the change, the 128 by 128 world built directly equals the old `atResolution(128, 128)` world bit for bit. `TODO.md` has the entry. **`forRows(seed, rows)`** is `WorldGenConfig(seed, 512, 512).atResolution(2 * rows, rows)`, from `CALIBRATED_GRID_CELLS`, the grid the counts were set on. `atResolution` scales the tectonic widths by the width's ratio, so on 1,024 by 512 a belt has twice the cells of 11.7 km that it had of 23.4. **What the two promise after Q1.** `atResolution` carries the tectonics' widths and nothing else, and `ResolutionScalingTest` is unchanged. `forRows` gives the 512 world's tectonics on the ground at any row count. The remaining counts reach half as far east-west on square cells as on today's grid of the same rows, and the (a) operators, which count a row as a column, reach as far north-south as east-west. Q2 owes the restatement in kilometers, and with it a `ResolutionScalingTest` whose tectonics carry nothing. Q3's ocean was done in 4a. Q4 owes the default grid and the saves, and Q5 the ladder by rows. **`GridShapeTest`.** The same seed at 512 by 512 and at `forRows(512)`, in twelve bands of fifteen degrees, on the four standard seeds. Each tolerance is the largest difference 512 by 512 and 1,024 by 1,024 show over the four seeds and twelve bands, rounded up at the second figure: a band's desert share 0.0469 (seed 7, -30 to -45 degrees), its land's mean rain 26.4% where it is over 100 mm (the same band), its mean temperature 0.38 C (seed 42, 15 to 0), and its ice share 0.0158 (seed 42, 75 to 60). The square grid's largest differences: desert 0.0355, rain 17.0%, temperature 0.25 C, and ice 0.0225, which runs as a known failure (seed 1234 at -75 to -90 degrees +0.017; seed 99 at 75 to 60 and at -60 to -75 +0.022). **It cannot fail on the tree before**, since Q1 moves no bit and 4b-1 already spends the belts' slant as a slope on the ground. Its control is that slant counted as 0.3 rows a cell again, which is what the tree before 4b-1 did on square cells. On seed 1234 it moves a band's desert by 0.085 and its rain by up to 59%, and it fails both clauses. **What is left for the guard to catch.** Over the whole map, the deserts are 3.42, 1.71, 6.70 and 2.66% of seeds 7, 42, 1234 and 99 at 512 by 512. They are 3.06, 1.37, 5.76 and 1.85% on square cells, 3.83, 1.59, 6.02 and 2.34% at 1,024 by 1,024, and 2.15, 0.90, 4.68 and 1.21% under the control. So 4b-1 closed most of the design's 37 to 46%. Square cells still carry 10 to 30% fewer deserts, where a finer grid of today's shape moves them -12 to +12%, though every band stays inside its spread. The coast's reach, a count Q2 restates, and the rain blur, which reaches as far north-south as east-west on square cells, are the suspects. They are not isolated. **`TectonicGroundTest`.** The share of the map whose rock is still rising, and the 90th percentile of those cells' distance from their boundary in km, at 512 by 512 against `forRows(512)`. The tolerances are the largest relative spreads 512 by 512 and 1,024 by 1,024 show, 1.72% and 2.73%, both on seed 99, rounded up. Square cells: 0.2074 and 416 km against 0.2081 and 414 (seed 7), 0.3350 and 393 against 0.3394 and 403 (42), 0.2061 and 399 against 0.2059 and 401 (1234), and 0.2264 and 388 against 0.2297 and 398 (99). The control is 1,024 by 512 built directly: 0.1950 and 222 km on seed 42, and both clauses fail. **Nothing re-recorded**: `WorldFingerprintTest`'s 512 digests and `CrossPlatformFingerprintTest`'s 128 fingerprint, on the JVM and in Wasm, are the tree before's to the bit. |
| Q2 The ground's figures in km, and the tests on square cells | 2026-09-28 | chunk/q2-square-tests | **The second chunk of the switch to square cells: every length the generator counted in cells that is a length on the ground is now stated in kilometers, and every test's world is built by rows. What stays in cells is a limit on a numerical step or the least the grid can draw, each named below with the grid it binds on.** **The tectonics (its own commit).** The fifteen `TectonicsConfig` widths are `…Km` at the 512 by 512 grid's values (the boundary's falloff 609.375, the Andean belt 328.125, the arc's offset 304.6875 and width 117.1875, the collision 609.375, the island arc's offset 187.5 and width 164.0625, the rift 164.0625, its shoulders' offset 257.8125 and width 164.0625, an epoch's drift 1,054.6875, the belts' age blur 70.3125, a hotspot chain 2,578.125, its spacing 351.5625 and a plume 117.1875 km), read as cells by `config.cellsFor` in `PlateStage.BeltCellWidths`. The age blur's radius is in kilometers but its spread is not: `PlateStage.roundWithAge` keeps the two box passes' spread, `sqrt(2 r (r + 1) / 3)` cell widths for a radius of `r` cell widths, which is not proportional to `r`, so the same 70.3125 km radius spreads 0.94 of itself on 23.4 km cells (r = 3 at one epoch), 0.88 on the 11.7 km cells of 512 rows (r = 6) and 0.85 on the 5.9 km cells of 1,024 rows (r = 12), tending to `sqrt(2/3)` of it on a fine grid. It is the one tectonic length left that moves with the grid; restating it moves every world at every grid, so it waits for a chunk that changes the tectonics (`TODO.md`). `atResolution` now moves the width and the height and nothing else, `CALIBRATED_GRID_CELLS` is gone, and `forRows(seed, rows)` is `WorldGenConfig(seed, 2 rows, rows)`. `ResolutionScalingTest` holds that: no tectonic setting carries `Cells`, and a round trip through another grid returns the config. It fails on the tree before, whose `atResolution` scaled the widths. **The proof, of the tectonic commit alone, on one seed at two grids.** After that commit, seed 42 built directly as `WorldGenConfig(42, 128, 128)` equals the tree before's `WorldGenConfig(42, 512, 512).atResolution(128, 128)` on all 49 branches of the world's digest outside the config (the tree before's own direct 128 by 128 world differed from its rescaled one on 46), and seed 42 at 512 by 512 is unchanged on all 49. No other seed or grid was compared. Save format 17. **The bare counts (the second commit).** The coast's reach `OceanConfig.coastalReachKm` 234.375 km, `FlowRouting.SMOOTH_FIELD_PERIOD_KM` 187.5, `LandmarkStage.VOLCANIC_REACH_KM` 140.625, the atlas's imperial and city-state realms 1,647,949 and 411,987 km², `BasinPartition.MIN_TRUNK_KM` 187.5, and the glaciation's two window ceilings 1,500 and 562.5 km: each the count times the 512 by 512 grid's cell, so with the distributaries' fall held at its old expression seed 42 at 512 by 512 kept all 49 branches, while 128 built directly moved 34. **The four disputed, by the rule that a limit bounding a numerical step belongs to the grid and a length on the ground does not.** `DeltaFan.GROOVE_MIN_REACH_CELLS` stays 4 cells: it is how many cells a fan needs for its grooves to be told apart, a property of the grid that draws them; in kilometers it would withhold the grooves from a fine grid that can draw them and hand them to a coarse one that merges them. `MoistureBudget.MAX_CONVERGENCE_PER_CELL` stays a quarter per cell of travel: it bounds one step of the march where the linearized update is taken. It binds on 0.32 to 0.69% of cells at 512 by 512 and 0.14 to 0.40% on square cells (seeds 7 and 42, final climate). **Three resolution floors in `PlateStage` stay in cells**, each the least the grid can draw and not a length on the ground, now said in their comments: the rift's flat floor is at least one cell wide, which binds where a cell is wider than the default floor's 164.0625 x 0.55 = 90.2 km, at 64 rows of square cells and fewer and on the 128 by 128 fingerprint grid; a half-graben segment is at least two cells and an accommodation zone one, which at the defaults (0.040 and 0.016 of the map's width) bind only under 50 and 63 cells across, narrower than any grid the program makes. `PlateStage.CENTRELINE_STEP_CELL_WIDTHS` stays four cell widths: what the stretch averages away is the staircase the cells make, whose step is a cell, while the bends it must follow are the boundary warp's, far longer than four cells on any grid. The distributaries' fall becomes a gradient, `HydraulicErosion.DISTRIBUTARY_FALL_PER_KM` = 0.15 per 23.4375 km, times the step's length on the ground (a diagonal step falls 1.41 times an axial one), floored at `MIN_DISTRIBUTARY_FALL`, 1e-7 of the field: a fall is a slope times a length, so it is the ground's. The fall is the same on the ground at every grid above that floor and not below it. At the defaults an axial step falls 9.6e-6 of the field per kilometer of the step (the pond depth's 0.0015 of the field times 0.15 per 23.4375 km), so the floor binds only where a step is under 10.4 m, on no grid the program makes. That moves seed 42 at 512 by 512 on 25 branches (the eroded height, the sea, the ocean, the climate, the rivers, the realms, the labels). Save format 18. The Courant coefficient's prose is restated as 0.24 sqrt(cells) on a grid as many cells tall as wide and 0.34 on square cells, and `highestLandMetres` keeps 6,000 m, restated as the world's ruler, a mean over ground set on the 512 by 512 grid's 23 km cells (Tibet's interior averages 5,023 m): the square grid's 11.7 km cells could resolve higher ground, but every stage from the belts' ceiling to the lapse rate is calibrated through it, and re-deriving it would move every altitude on every map for a statement about the highest cells alone. **The tests by rows.** Every worldgen test's world is `forRows` of the rows its grid had: 512 by 512 became 1,024 by 512 and 1,024 by 1,024 became 2,048 by 1,024. Synthetic operator grids stay, and the isotropy tests (`GaussianBlurTest`, `ThermalAspectTest`, `HeightIntegrationTest`, `CratonReachTest`, `RoutingGroundTest`) run at an aspect of 1.0 beside 0.5. `RoutingGroundTest` at 1.0: 28.07% taken down a column against the rule's 27.94, 28.07 along a row against 27.94, and 43.87 on a diagonal against 44.13, 1.47 standard errors apart. **Instruments restated in kilometers**: the Earth-likeness coastline boxes (`COASTLINE_BOX_KM`), `GroundTextureTest`'s flank window 93.75 km, `TectonicGroundTest`'s offset 398.4375 km, `IsostasyTest`'s flexure bins 187.5 km, `TectonicHistoryTest`'s inland reach 1,218.75 km, and `CratonReachTest`'s 398.4375 km. The flexure bins and the inland reach, counted in cells, failed their controls on square cells, which is how they were found. `RuledLines`' offset and bar, its seven-step run, and `OutlineRuns`' bar stay in cells: they ask whether water or a shore lies along the grid's own bearings to within its staircase, whose step is a cell. **`TectonicGroundTest`** now compares 512 by 512 with `forRows(512)`, both on the ground (the figures are Q1's to the digit); its control is the square world with every tectonic length halved, and both clauses fail on it. **`CombGuardTest`** runs at `forRows(512)` and is two-sided: the larger axis's comb may be at most 1.5 times the smaller's (`AXIS_FACTOR`), and each axis at most the 0.021 km per 1,000 km² the router makes on isotropic ground over the same land (`COMB_FLOOR_KM_PER_1000_KM2`). The axis bar is taken from the control's parallel sustained reaches, 1.00 and 1.30 times as long on one axis as the other, and not from the ridge-qualified comb the clause reads, whose control figures (0.0201 against 0.0068 on seed 7, 0.0111 against 0.0137 on seed 42) are a handful of reaches and too few for a ratio; where an axis is that small the clause falls back to the floor. The isotropic control is a case of its own: the guard passes it on both seeds. Written as a case, it failed first at a floor of 0.02, which had rounded the control's 0.0201 down; the floor is now the control's most rounded up at the second figure. The axes: 0.160 and 0.146 on seed 7, 0.197 and 0.181 on seed 42 (1.10 and 1.09), where 512 by 512 carries 0.412 and 0.061, and 0.524 and 0.080; its control is the 512 by 512 world, which fails the two-sided clause. The floor clause runs as a known failure, the symmetric comb, seven to ten times the router's own, with a bearing census (`BearingCensus`) as its second instrument: the steep channel cells run along the two axes 2.2 to 2.6 times as often as the fall line does, and along the diagonal 1.4 to 1.6 times (seed 7: E-W 6.8 against 3.1%, diagonal 8.2 against 5.1, N-S 6.4 against 2.9; seed 42: 6.2 and 2.7, 8.1 and 5.6, 6.2 and 2.4). What the runs support: the comb is the hydraulic rounds' (handed the same network on the ground before the rounds, the census reads 0.000 to 0.001), and over the rounds the steep cells' steps turn from the diagonal to the two axes alike (col/row/diagonal 27.6/30.6/41.7% before, 34.0/33.8/32.2 after on seed 7; 29.7/28.3/42.0 and 32.9/34.1/33.0 on seed 42); the figure is the census's as much as the ground's (a sustained reach of 5 steps, 58.6 km, reads 1.6 to 1.8 times what the 6 steps of 60 km read; 46.9 km 0.44 to 0.47, 82 km 0.09 to 0.10; partners at 11 to 40 km 0.12, at 23 to 59 km 0.19 to 0.20; a ridge threshold of 50 m 0.17 to 0.18 and of 150 m 0.13), and the isotropic control over the same land reads 0.0068 to 0.0201. No cause beyond that is named. **Re-recorded, each for the grid it now reads**: the rainfall calibration (D I-9), the rain-dissection pin (B-I2), the equatorial cold tongue, the erosion's two implicit-update clauses, C I4's lakes, Q1's polar ice (seed 99 at 75 to 60 degrees now +0.021, with the coast's reach at 234 km on square cells where it was 117), Greenland's thickness, the foreland, the jump flood's two, the outlet notch's two, the estuary ceiling, and the old belts (now within 1,218.75 km, seed 42 at 0.0036). **Armed, now passing**: `ScaleFreeTest`'s channel heads (the network grows 1.32, 1.33, 1.33 and 1.31 from 512 rows to 1,024 against the bar of 1.35, where it grew 1.43 to 1.54 on the old grid), `GroundIsotropyTest`'s coast (0.962, 1.008, 1.060 and 1.055, pooled 1.021), `MoistureBudgetTest`'s two recycling clauses (pooled 30.3%), `StraightRunTest`'s ruled bars, `LittoralCoastTest`'s dimension (excess 0.221 against a bar of 0.245), `GlaciationTest`'s comb (the ice adds 1.64, 1.17 and 0.73% of the world's water), `ScaleFree`'s maritime reach at 50 km (x0.97, 0.88, 0.85, 0.83); at 150 km it stays a note (x0.82 to 0.88). **New known failures on square cells**, none isolated, `TODO.md` has each: seed 42's coldest northern eastern-boundary water in the equatorial tongue (-2.22 C at 7.9 degrees), the cold half's wind onto the subtropical coasts (+0.31 m/s pooled), the lowstand's estuary gain (1.47 pooled under 1.5), and no audited ice dome filling a third of its disc. The ice's flow clause, gated on a dome that fills a third of its 500 km disc, therefore read nothing on square cells; it now also reads seed 20 (`IceSheetTest.DOME_SEED`), scanned from seeds 1 to 25 at 512 rows, where two domes qualify, seed 5 at 34.1% of its disc and seed 20 at 44.2%, the larger and the only one clear of the gate. Its sheet flows outward on 90.1% of its cells at a mean 49.3 degrees off radial against the clause's 67% and 67.5, so the clause is armed there (seeds 59758 and 7 had recorded it failing on the 512 by 512 grid), and the same sheet's flow turned a quarter reads 50.1% at 93.2 and fails it, which is now a case. About 35 s of the tier. Also re-measured: `RiftSegmentationTest` moved to seed 34 (seed 43 lost its bridge on square cells; 12 seeds scanned) with a bar of 0.2 on the variation of width, `ClimateFedErosionTest` pools its "some cell" clause across seeds at the old Courant figure, `OceanLeavesTheGroundTest`'s digests are re-taken at 512 rows, and `ColdWaterPlacementTest`, `PressureWindTest`, `SeaLevelHistoryTest` and `IceSheetTest` carry the four above. **The tier's cost.** `:worldgen:jvmTest` on Q1's tree (84f723a): 25 min 14 s from the first class to the last, 71 min 55 s in classes, 322 tests, 246 shared-world generations taking 51 min of them. On Q2's, 51 min 56 s on the first run (before the re-records, 30 of 323 failing) and 71 min 01 s on the second (none failing), with 139 min 35 s and 142 min 43 s in classes: the classes' own times agree, so the difference in wall time is the machine's. The second run's 247 generations took 104 min: the same worlds, each of twice the cells, and 53 of the 71 minutes the classes added; the rest is the measuring over twice the cells. The standard worlds stay at 512 rows, as the design has it, and the detail guards' 1,024-row worlds keep the 5.9 km cell they were written for. **Held at 256 rows**, 512 by 256 with the 512 by 512 grid's cell width, where the figure does not depend on the count: `IsostasyTest`'s two controls (where the sea-level cut lands is a matter of shares of area, and a world with one crust has one mode), `IncrementalReuseTest`'s equality case (already at 256), `LayerCaptureTest` (whether a sink moves a bit; 37 s to 11, every path it asserts still taken) and `ErosionSkipTest`'s equality (the skip's argument is tile by tile; 10 s to 1). **Not moved, and why**: a guard on the four standard worlds borrows worlds the other classes generate anyway, so moving it would generate more, not less; the classes that build variants (`GlaciationTest`, `OutletIncisionTest`, `LakeWaterBalanceTest`, `SnowBalanceTest`, `PressureWindTest`, `MoistureBudgetTest`) measure the physics on the standard cell, and those figures move with it (the channel network 1.31 to 1.33 times denser a grid finer, the drainage's standing water 0.83); `GenerationStopTest`'s bound and `IncrementalReuseTest`'s cost ratio need rounds long enough to time; `RealmTiesTest` needs tied biome counts, which are counts of cells; `ReceiverClampTest` counts pits cell by cell. **The shared worlds' retention** stays at 700 MB (600 before Q2): sixteen worlds of 512 rows, the count 600 MB kept of the old grid, would take 1.25 GB, and at 1.25 GB one worker ran out of its 3.5 GB heap in `LakeWaterBalanceTest` and failed its 17 tests after it. What 700 MB costs is `PressureWindTest` making its four variants three times over, eight generations and about 160 s; `TODO.md` has the eviction order that causes it. **Records moved.** `:cartography`'s twelve render records (`RecordedRenders`), all twelve together, because the gallery world, seed 234475 at 512 by 512, moved under them with the distributaries' gradient; nothing else in `:cartography` (125 tests), and nothing in `:desktop` (150, 6 skipped) or `:ui` (168). The save fixtures (`GzipFixture`, the desktop's `folder-written.cgw` and the browser's `DesktopWrittenSave`) are regenerated at each format, 17 and then 18, with no converter. `CrossPlatformFingerprintTest` and `WorldFingerprintTest` build their worlds by rows and record nothing; the 128-row fingerprint is `elevation=2444014908821582024`, land 12,518, rivers 1,333, realms 15, on the JVM and in Wasm alike. |
| Q2's 2048 census | 2026-09-28 | chunk/q2-census-2048 | **The geometry census at 2048 on Q2's merge failed on 67 items, six of them violations with no record; each of the six was traced to Q2's smooth field and is a class already found, so the census is re-recorded and nothing in the generator changes.** **How.** The audit's own worlds (`WorldGenConfig(seed, 512, 512).atResolution(2048, 2048)`) were built with each of Q2's changes put back in turn by a switch in an uncommitted probe: the coast's reach (10 cells, 58.6 km, where it is now 234 km), the smooth field's period (8 cells, 47 km, where it is now 187.5 km), the shortest trunk, the glaciation's two ceilings and the distributaries' fall. The tectonic commit was checked on its own: seeds 7 and 59758 at 2048 carry the same digest on all 49 branches outside the config before it and after it, since a width in kilometers at the 512 grid's value reads as the same number of cells at 2048 as the old count scaled by four. With all five put back (the landmarks' volcanic reach and the realms' government areas were left, since no layer the census reads draws them), seeds 42, 1234, 59758 and 969495 fail nothing, and seed 7 fails nothing with the smooth field alone put back. **The six**, each cleared by putting back the 47 km smooth field and by nothing else unless said: (1) seed 7's drawn coast turns 7 square corners over 12,383 cell widths, 0.565 per thousand against a bar of 0.005. Four of them stood at the same cells before Q2 (656,1174; 1089,934; 1546,1602; 327,2005), 0.323 per thousand, too few for the count's lower bound to clear the bar; the smooth field's period added three (1537,1434; 1524,1583; 236,1894). Each is the drawn coast running 6 to 17 cells along a row or a column and turning square at an inlet, two at the mouths of the far south's straight drowned gullies, and none lies on the field's 32-cell lattice. Recorded under `COAST_SQUARE_NOTCH`, the class seed 42's 0.314 and seed 1234's notch were until the narrow sea was drawn. (2) seed 42's ice carving follows an arc, 123 degrees of a circle 11.9 cells in radius at (235,1254), and (6) seed 969495's ice surface one of 123 degrees at (925,1328): `ICE_ARCS`, which 1234's and 99's ice carried before and no longer does. (3) seed 1234's drawn coast follows two arcs, 157 degrees about (1546,1287) and 131 about (1816,684). The coast's arcs were mapped to `DELTA_LOBES_ROUND`, but no delta built either (no sea-lobe cell within 12 cells of either centre): the arc is the land's own level line at the sea, the class of `TERRAIN_ARC`, which 1234's contours carried before Q2. So the coast's arcs now have their own finding, `COAST_ARC`, with that written in it. (4) seed 59758's ice surface has a level line running 69.5 cell widths (407 km) at 110.7 degrees at (1381,503), along the sheet's straight margin by a rift valley: `ICE_EDGE_ALONG_A_ROW`, whose straight edges carry into the surface; this one is not along a row, and the finding says so. (5) seed 59758's realm border runs 64 steps north-south down the axis of a rift lake at (1689,1776), against a bar of 63.9: `REALM_BORDERS_STRAIGHT`, which 42's 65 steps were until this census; it also goes with the old distributary fall. None of the six is a figure of Q2's own making a shape: the period is a length the flat routing's jitter bends courses over, now the 512 grid's at every grid, and what moved is where the courses, the coast and the ice ended up. **The rest, re-recorded.** With the smooth field put back the only other items left are the isotherms' straight runs on 42, 1234 and 969495, which are the coast's reach moving the climate, and one of 59758's peoples' arcs; seeds 99 and 718106 were not probed, and their items are the same kinds. The 17 clauses the moved ground passes are armed, since each named violation is gone: 42's realm border, 1234's terrain contour and ice arcs, 99's river course, terrain contour, ice arcs and peoples' corner, 59758's ice facets and ice corner, and 969495's lake arcs, drawn ice, realm arc and biome corner. The 14 violations found at another place in the same class have their new signatures: the isobaths' arcs on 7, 42 and 969495's facets, the isotherms on 42, 1234 and 969495, the river corners on 1234 and 59758, the ice surface's arcs on 1234, 99 and 59758, 718106's drawn ice, and 59758's peoples' arcs. The 26 clauses that gained a figure to measure come off the insufficient list (7's realm rectangle, 42's and 969495's valley basins, 99's ice carving isotropy and scour basins, 718106's scour basins), and the 4 that lost theirs go on it (42's scour basins' facets and creases, whose longest line is 368 km where they need 370 and 374, and 99's and 718106's realm rectangles, no ring small enough). |
| Q2b The tier's worlds at half size | 2026-09-28 | chunk/q2b-half-size-tier | **The generator's tests build their worlds at 256 rows wherever the figure a guard asserts does not depend on the grid's detail, and keep 512 rows where it does. No bit of any world moves.** **`SharedWorlds`** now names the four standard seeds (`STANDARD_SEEDS`) and two grids: `COARSE_ROWS`, 256 rows, 512 by 256 square cells of 23.4 km, the 512 by 512 grid's cell width, for a share of area, a band's mean, a budget, an equality between two routes or a physical figure; and `DETAIL_ROWS`, 512, for texture, outlines, straight runs, channel heads, network statistics, lakes, comparisons between grids and bars set on that grid. Every class says in its KDoc or at its constant which it reads and why. A world of 256 rows holds about 20 MB of arrays, so the 700 MB the lender keeps holds both standard sets, some 390 MB, with room beside them; the retention is unchanged. Each report line now names its worker's process, which is how the regenerations below were counted. **Moved to 256 rows, whole**: `AbsoluteRainfallTest`, `CatchmentUnitsTest`'s standard worlds, `ColdBiomeShareTest`, `ContinentalityTest`, `CultureHearthLandmassTest`, `CultureRealmTest`, `CurrentFeedsRainTest`, `ErosionUnitsTest`, `GeographyAuditTest`, `ImplicitIncisionTest`'s production case, `IncrementalReuseTest`'s cost case (its equality case was already there), `MeridionalWindTest`, `OceanCurrentTest`, `RealmIdRangeTest`'s seven worlds (the author's at 1,024 rows stays), `RealmSpreadTest`, `ReceiverClampTest`, `SeaIceTest`, `SeasonsTest`, `SnowBalanceTest`, `UnitsTest`'s ruler and `VegetationDensityTest`; and `BoundaryPairTest`'s belts, measured on the cell width their widths were set in cells on (the hotspot cone's roundness stays at 512 and 1,024 rows, as written). **In part**: `ContinentalShelfTest`'s land-mask equality (the shelf's 75 km is 3.2 cells at 256 rows, so its band stays at 512), `DepositionTest`'s mass budget (a delta is a few cells, so the mouths stay), `IsostasyTest`'s slider, hypsometry, flexure and ice (the collision rate stays: the setting was derived from the denudation on the 512-row grid's slopes), `MoistureBudgetTest`'s convergence and inversion, and `PressureWindTest`'s interior, ice edge and control. **Measured moving with the cell at 256 rows, and so kept at 512**: the continental recycling ratio, 0.285 against 0.303, under Earth's band where 512 rows sits just inside it; the monsoon, the warm half onshore +0.06 m/s pooled against +0.29, the bar +0.10, with seed 99's largest subtropical continent centered at 29.0 degrees over 1.8 times the area where it is 19.4; and `ColdWaterPlacementTest` whole, whose equatorial basins are runs of water a strait of a cell or two joins or parts (seed 7's stress times length -669 N/m² km against -323) and whose recorded failure, the tongue reaching seed 42's eastern shore, is not there at 256 rows, so the clause would have read fixed. **Stay at 512 or 1,024 rows**, each for its detail: the Earth-likeness suite, `ChannelGapTest`, `ChannelInitiationControlTest`, `ClimateFedErosionTest`, `CombGuardTest`, `DeltaMouthTest`, `DeltaOutlineTest`, `FlatCourseTest`, `GlacialBasinShapeTest`, `GlaciationTest`, `GridShapeTest`, `GroundIsotropyTest`, `GroundTextureTest`, `IceSheetTest`, `JumpFloodDistanceTest`, `LakeWaterBalanceTest`, `LittoralCoastTest`, `OutletIncisionTest`, `RibbonLandTest`, `RiftSegmentationTest`, `RiverCourseTest`, `RiverProfileReportTest`, `ScaleFreeTest`, `SeaLevelHistoryTest`, `StraightRunTest`, `TectonicGroundTest`, `TectonicHistoryTest` (the belts' age blur spreads a different share of its radius on each grid), `ValleyIncisionTest` and `WaterlineBasinTest`; `GenerationStopTest` and `RealmTiesTest` for Q2's reasons; `OceanLeavesTheGroundTest` and `WorldFingerprintTest`, which pin the 512-row world. Synthetic grids are left as they were. **Restated on the ground, so a mask asks for the same coast on either grid**: the monsoon masks' reaches in `AbsoluteRainfallTest` and `MeridionalWindTest`, 30 and 8 rows of 11.7 km, now 351.5625 and 93.75 km; `SeasonsTest`'s inland reach, 12 columns, now 140.625 km, and its Mediterranean floor, 40 cells of 512 rows, now 40 x 11.71875² = 5,493 km² (seed 7's 39 cells at 256 rows are 21,400 km²). None moves a figure at 512 rows. **Re-recorded, for the cell size**: D I-9's calibration, whose figure is the 99.5th percentile of the land's rain, read on cells of four times the area: seed 42's coast from 3,644 to 3,407 mm, inside the bar, and seed 99's from 4,029 to 3,826; and the foreland's moat, the same bin at 1,125 to 1,313 km, 721 m under a belt that rebounds 603 m where it was 480 under 412. No bar was re-derived: no moved class has one taken from a control at 512 rows. `RealmSpreadTest`'s landlocked bar, origin/main's three on the 512 by 512 grid, is kept; 256 rows read two, 512 rows none. **Controls at 256 rows.** Every in-class control ran there and bit: the desert bands without land recovery, the sea ice, the seasons, the marine blend, the vegetation field, the zonal wind, the convergence and the lid, the old normalization, the crust's aim, the one-crust hypsometry, the ice load and the flexure, the shelf's land mask, the one-profile belts and the cones eight times as wide, and the receiver rule, whose absence leaves 5, 48 and 64 channel cells below their receivers on 718106, 42 and 7. Mutations, one a group, each shown failing at 256 rows and reverted: **climate**, the current coupling hardcoded to 1, as `CurrentFeedsRainTest` records it being shown (on and off both 597.35 mm); **ocean**, the gyres' eastward flow turned round (`OceanCurrentTest`: the westerlies' band flows west, -0.05 m/s on seed 7); **ice**, accumulation that ignores the snowfall (`SnowBalanceTest` fails four clauses, ice on 26.43% of the land and 23.0% of the cold dry interior); **erosion budgets**, half of what leaves for the sea missing from the tally (`DepositionTest`, round 1: 284.1 incised against 12.9 laid and 135.6 lost); **reuse**, the rivers' guard without the lakes section (`IncrementalReuseTest`: lakes, waterBalance and runoffFraction differ). The first reuse mutation tried, the ocean's guard without the climate section, passed: the sea stage's guard already takes the climate section while the ice is on, so a climate change remakes the sea and the ocean with it. **`RiverProfileReportTest`** borrowed its five worlds without the check `BorrowsSharedWorlds` declares, so the lender made them for it alone every run, 106 s; it now declares it. **The tier**, `:worldgen:jvmTest` alone on a quiet machine. Before, on Q2's tree (4f6b306): 54 min 30 s from the first class to the last, 161 min 10 s in 85 classes, 325 tests none failing, 252 shared-world generations of 191 distinct worlds taking 7,113 s (the guard's own lender left out). After: 52 min 56 s, 137 min 53 s in the same 85 classes, 325 tests none failing, 266 generations of 205 distinct worlds taking 6,068 s: 1,024 by 512 went from 199 generations and 4,317 s to 133 and 2,833, 512 by 256 from 11 and 67 s to 91 and 620, and 2,048 by 1,024 stayed at 30 of 21 worlds, 2,611 s then and 2,494 now. The moved and part-moved classes took 3,192 s and take 1,955. **The wall did not follow the classes**, and the target of about 25 minutes is not met: Gradle deals the classes to its four workers without regard to cost, and the worker that drew `GlaciationTest`, 21.6 minutes and fourteen worlds of 1,024 rows on its own, ran from the tier's first minute to its last, while another had finished its borrowing classes by the 14th; the classes average 34.5 minutes a worker. Of the 266 generations, 205 are distinct worlds, 42 a world another worker had made, 15 a world the same worker had made and dropped, 525 s, mostly where 1,024-row worlds push the standard worlds out, and 4 were `RiverProfileReportTest`'s own, above. `docs/TODO.md` has the balance and the eviction, with the candidates: `GlaciationTest`'s three cases as three classes, `IncrementalReuseTest`'s equality at 128 rows, and a store the workers share. The `RiverProfileReportTest` change came after the tier's run and was run alone. `:worldgen:wasmJsTest` was not run: no common test moved. `:cartography`'s and `:desktop`'s tests, which compile `SharedWorlds`, were compiled and not run. **Then, for the balance.** `GlaciationTest`'s three cases are three classes, each borrowing its worlds as before: `GlaciationTest` keeps the lakes (six worlds of 1,024 rows), `GlaciationLatticeTest` takes flat frozen country (two of 1,024 and four of 512) and `GlaciationCombTest` the mountain flanks (six of 1,024), with `glaciatedZone` top-level for the first two. Every figure they print is the tier's above to the character (22 lines), and run side by side they take 9.4, 4.9 and 8.6 minutes where the one class took 21.6. `IncrementalReuseTest`'s equality and identity cases run at 128 rows: seed 99 there still carries the lakes, rivers, realms and landmarks the case checks for, and the case now asserts that each of its 39 settings moves the world, which each does from the stage its section owns downward (the rivers' own setting 3 branches, the landmarks' 1, the cultures' 2, the tectonics' 46); the rivers' guard without the lakes section still fails it, on lakes, waterBalance and runoffFraction. The equality case takes 253 s, the class 290 s where it took 476. The cost case stays at 256 rows, where a generation is long enough to time. The tier was not run again. |

| Q5 The ladder by rows | 2026-09-28 | chunk/q5-ladder-rows | **The applications name every size by its rows and make a grid twice as many cells across: the chip "1024" is a world 2048 by 1024 with square cells 5.9 km on a side, and its picture the 2048 by 1024 sheet the chip drew before.** **The one path.** `Knobs.atResolution(config, size)` is `config.atResolution(COLUMNS_PER_ROW * size, size)`, and `Knobs.sizeOf` reads a size back off a grid as its rows; the chips, the lit chip, the header's line ("1024 rows" where it said "1024 px"), `Settings.startingConfig`, `whyOutOfReach` at generation, the library listing, the bug report's title (its field keeps the grid) and links all go through them. `ExportSubjects` asks `Knobs.makesAtSize`, so an export at the size on screen draws the world on screen and is never made again (with the N by N comparison every export regenerated), and the export note, the progress line and its sheet are said from the grid the size makes. The cartouche and a data export's sidecar give the grid ("2048 × 1024"); the interface names the size. **Links**: format 2 writes `size` as rows; a link of format 1 is read, not refused, its size read as rows and every pair applied, and opens with a line saying its world has square cells and differs from the one it was copied from. No fingerprint is checked when a link opens (the design's question 9 supposed one; none exists at run time), so the line is what says so, and `WorldLinkWorldTest` shows the two worlds' fingerprints differ. No converter. **The export row gains 1024** (1024, 2048, 4096, 8192): with the browser stopped at 1024 rows and the row starting at 2048, a browser had no size it could export at, and `Exports.clamp` would have sent it 2048; 1024 is also the desktop's starting size, where the export is the world on screen. The default export size is 1024 for the same reason (at 2048 every fresh browser would have been told its default was brought down). The settings keep their key: a stored number names a chip, and a chip names the same sheet as before. **Measured**, seed 42, Ryzen 7 5700X, a 12 GB heap (three quarters of a 16 GB machine, the packaged app's `MaxRAMPercentage=75` there), one size a JVM, the app's work in its order (generate, the processor raster and the pane's sheet, a PNG export at the world's own size, a save), the world, raster and screen image held throughout; peak live is the largest heap a collection left, resident the process's peak working set. 1024 rows: 50.3 s on the processor, 46.7 s with acceleration on (the incision, most of the work, has no card path), peak live 1.1 and 1.4 GB, resident 3.7 and 3.9 GB, drawn in 0.6 s, exported in 1.4 s, saved in 7.2 s (137 MB). 2048 rows on the processor: 210.8 s, peak live 3.0 GB, resident 4.8 GB, drawn 1.8 s, exported 4.6 s, saved 27.7 s (555 MB). 4096 rows with acceleration on: generated in 994.6 s, drawn in 19.1 s, exported in 29.8 s and saved in 109.2 s (2.25 GB), all under the 12 GB heap, but with 10.3 GB live after a collection and 14.0 GB resident at its fullest, leaving a 16 GB machine about 2 GB for the system, the graphics driver and everything else. In a tab of the production build (Chrome 152, acceleration off, the JavaScript heap sampled every 250 ms): 512 rows generated and drawn in 36.2 and 39.7 s, heap at most 0.9 GB; 1024 rows in 171.6 s, heap at most 1.7 GB. **Ceilings.** Desktop 2048 rows; 4096 rows is drawn disabled with its reason, as 8192 was, since it finished only with 2 GB of a 16 GB machine to spare and is offered only if it fits with room. Browser 1024 rows, on the tab's measured 1.7 GB; 2048 rows is four times the cells and at the tab's heap a cell wants about 6.8 GB, past what a Wasm heap addresses. A browser opens saves of at most 1024 rows. Defaults unchanged by name: the desktop app 1024 (50 s on the processor, under the 90 s the brief allowed), a desktop tab 512, a phone 512. **The phone** cannot be timed here: `LargeLinks.MEASURED` states its 1024 as about four minutes, estimated from the tab, and the compact line under the chips says "expected"; `docs/TODO.md` asks for a phone's figure. **Guards, each shown failing on the ladder as many cells tall as wide** (`Knobs.atResolution` as `(size, size)` and `sizeOf` as the width, 14 of 92 cases red): `PanelKnobsTest`'s new clause that every size on the ladder and on the export row, through the chips, an export, the settings and a link, draws a cell to a pixel (red: "the panel's chip at 512 makes a 512 by 512 grid, drawn 2 by 1 pixels a cell"); the header by rows; `SettingsTest`, `LargeLinksTest`, `WorldLinksTest` (format 1 read as rows, the ceiling by rows), `SheetCameraTest` (the 512 world is 1024 by 512 cells, a cell a pixel), `BugReportTest`, `WorldLinkWorldTest`, `SavesAndExportsTest`. With the two paths alone put back (the export's "on screen" by width and height, the link's size by width), 5 of 28 red: the export at the size on screen regenerated, the link wrote `size=1024` for a 512 world and a 2048 world's link asked for 4096. Restated as well: `ChromeGalleryTest`, `PhoneAtlasTest`, `WorldLinkDesktopTest` (the cartouche's rows), `SaveIdentityTest` (two "1024" nodes now), `SettingsEffectTest`, `ExportSmokeTest` (the ceiling 2048), `BrowserCeilingTest`, `SiteSourcesTest`, and the site's sizes and reel links (format 2) only where Q5 moved them. Not run and still N by N: the audit tier's `ExportAuditTest` and `SaveResolutionAuditTest`. **The header.** With the desktop's 4096 disabled and its reason printed under the size chips, the header in the 1440 by 900 window `ChromeGalleryTest` photographs took the whole panel column and every section went below the fold (the test's wide panel showed none of the sections' knobs).
