The British spellings it and `SiteSourcesTest` look for are shared in `AmericanEnglish`, with a few added (artefact, plough, mould, sulphur, judgement). **The documents.** README: sizes by rows and square cells, the ceilings (2048, 4096 with the heap, 1024 in a browser), the times, the true shape a cell to a pixel, `atResolution` as a change of grid only (it claimed a rescaling that no longer exists), links of format 2 and format 1 read as rows, the 4096 save's 4.9 GB, and its British spellings converted. `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`: the desktop's cost by size from Q5 with the 4096 row; the exports re-measured by `ExportAuditTest`, 2048 rows 211.4 s of generation against a second or so of encoding (PNG 10.2 MB, heightmap 8.8 MB); the raster at 2048 rows, 0.30 s on the card and 1.12 s on the processor (0.57 single lamp); the stage profile re-measured at 512, 1,024 and 2,048 rows, 12.9, 43.3 and 195.2 s with erosion 63.8% at 2048 and the sea level 23.0%; why 8192 is not offered (134 million cells, about 41 GB live at 4096's rate); the tab's figures from Q5. The bug form's placeholder is "2048 × 1024". `docs/TODO.md`: three entries closed on every grid the application makes (the six operators that count a row as a column, the glaciation's two distances measured with the cell's width, the drowned valleys' catchment area), each still true of a test's grid of cells twice as wide as tall; the site's marks and the band re-read on square cells. **The audit tier by rows**: `ExportAuditTest` at 1024 and 2048 rows, the sizes every desktop offers (4096 rows holds more than the tier's 10 GB heap and was measured in Q5); `SaveResolutionAuditTest` saves and opens a real 2048-row world (555 MB on disk in 27.5 s, opened in 6.4 s) and a synthetic 4096-row one (8192 by 4096, 4,898,948,958 bytes of payload, 65 MB on disk in 32.6 s, opened in 15.2 s), letting the saved world go and checking what opens against SHA-256 digests of its arrays, so one world is held at a time. Run once with the band's water case (the band's world moved): 4 tests, all passing. **Tests, from the XML**: `:desktop:test` 155 with 6 skipped (36 classes; the two benchmarks), `:desktop:siteTest` 30, `:ui:jvmTest` 171 (20 classes), `:ui:wasmJsTest` 165 (17), `:web:wasmJsTest` 45 (9), `:cartography:jvmTest` 129 (20), and in the audit tier `ExportAuditTest` 1, `SaveResolutionAuditTest` 2 and the band's water case 1, and `StageProfileTest` 1 for the profile; all passing. `PanelKnobsTest`'s browser clause, which pinned the old sentence for 4096, was restated to the sentence naming the memory. |
| R1 Record mode and the pipeline map | 2026-09-30 | chunk/record-mode | **The pinned figures re-record themselves on request, and the pipeline's stages are mapped for whoever changes one.** `-Precord` sets `cartogenesis.record=true`: a stale pin passes and writes its replacement to `build/pin-records/<task>/` (JSON Lines), and `:worldgen:applyPinRecords` (with `-Preview` to look first) writes them into the source in place, printing file:line, old, new, kind and the measuring test, and refuses any pin it cannot place uniquely. Covered: the geometry census's `known(...)` and `insufficient(...)` lines (re-taken only where the tolerance no longer matches; a clean or unmeasurable entry is deleted; a violation with no named finding still fails), the text signatures of `KnownFailures` in `:worldgen`, `:cartography` and `:desktop` (single, joined and same-file constants; a clause that no longer fails still fails with a notice to arm it by hand), and `RecordedRenders`' style hashes. Not covered: `:ui`'s multiplatform twin; seed choices and bars derived from controls are never rewritten, being decisions. Guards: `PinRecordsTest` and `GeometryRecordTest`, each a stale pin that fails normally, records, rewrites a copy and then holds. `docs/PIPELINE.md` maps each stage's inputs, outputs and downstream readers, with the loops (the provisional climates inside erosion and sea level, isostasy solved three times), to be read before any change. |
| T1 The test tier's world cache and its everyday and deep tiers | 2026-10-01 | chunk/test-tiers | **Worlds kept on disk between workers and runs, and the per-merge tier split into an everyday tier of standard worlds and a deep tier run by pipeline stage.** No generator code and no pinned figure moved; every test reads the worlds it read before. **The cache.** `WorldDiskCache` (shared test support) sits beneath `SharedWorlds`' lender: a world it does not hold is read from `build/world-cache` if any worker of this run or an earlier one made it, and stored there when made. A file's key is the seed, the grid, a SHA-256 of the full settings (every default written out) and a hash of the generator's compiled classes (`:worldgen`'s class directory or its jar, which hash alike) with the versions of Kotlin, the coroutines and serialization libraries and the JVM, so any change to the generator misses every world stored before it. `WorldFile` writes the world's object graph in binary, as `ReachableState` walks it: primitive arrays in bulk as raw bits, objects by class and field, an object met twice as a reference to the first, a Kotlin `object` as its instance; `:cartography`'s save codec was not usable from `:worldgen`. A world is written to a temporary name and renamed into place; a lock per world (a monitor in the JVM, a file lock between JVMs) makes two workers that want it at once generate it once, the second waiting and reading (measured: 7 to 25 s of waiting for a 512-row world against 21 to 39 s of generating one). Each file carries the fresh world's digests by branch and every read is checked against them, failing the borrower if they differ; a file that cannot be read is deleted and the world made again. 20 GB cap (`-PworldCacheGigabytes`), least recently used variant first and standard worlds last; `-PworldCache=off`; `./gradlew clearWorldCache`. Guard: `WorldDiskCacheTest`, a standard world (42 at 256 rows) generated fresh and read back digest by digest with as many containers, a bit flipped on disk refused, a file cut short regenerated, two workers generating once, a generator change missing, and eviction in both orders. **The tiers.** Everyday: default settings at 512 rows or fewer, one grid per seed. Deep: a class's own variant for an on/off control, the same seed on several grids, or 1,024 rows or more. Classified by reading every class and checked against the baseline run's `SHARED WORLD` lines, which found one more (`CombGuardTest`'s half-height control on the old square grid). Where a class mixes the two, its control methods are listed by name and its other cases stay everyday; nothing was split into new classes. `:worldgen`: 26 deep classes and 19 classes with deep methods (plates 3 + 3, erosion 4 + 5, sea 6 + 2, ocean 0 + 1, climate 6 + 5, rivers 4 + 1, realms 1, engine 2 + 2); `:cartography`: drawing 1 + 2; `:desktop`: erosion 1, ocean 1, rivers 1, drawing 5 + 3. The audit tier is unchanged. `deepTest` in each module, with the per-merge budget; `-Pstages=climate,ocean` runs those stages and every stage they reach by `docs/PIPELINE.md`, the loops included (climate and ocean reach back to erosion and the ice), with `engine` and `drawing` always; `docs/PIPELINE.md` says which stages each source area calls for. **Measured** on a 4-thread, 15 GB machine with `-PtestWorkers=2`, from the timing report: `:worldgen:jvmTest` on main 91m 43s, 327 tests, 245 generations of 205 distinct worlds, 7,067 s of generation (30 at 1,024 rows, 2,946 s). With the cache and the split: everyday 215 tests (the cache's 8 new among them) in 9m 30s cold, 13 generations of 13 distinct standard worlds, 294 s, and 6m 21s warm, no generation and 22 reads in 7 s; deep 120 tests in 67m 09s after an everyday run, 192 generations of 192 distinct worlds, 5,320 s, and 20m 54s warm, no generation and 260 reads in 121 s. Both tiers in one run: 76m 31s cold, 27m 04s warm. What a warm everyday run still spends is the classes that run stages or the engine themselves (`LittoralCoastTest` 1m 57s, `RealmTiesTest` 1m 31s). The cache after both tiers: 16 GB, the everyday tier's worlds 0.75 GB of it. `:cartography`: on main 130 tests in 10m 42s; now `jvmTest` 127 in 8m 00s and `deepTest` 3 in 2m 40s. `:desktop`: `test` 129 with 7 skipped in 22m 23s, `deepTest` 26 with 13 skipped (the card and the benchmarks) in 14m 59s; `SeedFieldTest`'s typed-seed case timed out at its one minute here and on main alike. Not run here (no card, no browser): `:ui:wasmJsTest`, `:desktop:siteTest`, the card's cases. |
| L1 A rift is one rift at every grid, and a lake's pockets balance apart | 2026-09-29 | chunk/l1-lake-area | **Two causes of lake area following the cell count, found by L1's diagnosis and fixed; no claim of resolution independence is made for lakes until L2 (the post-cut outlet) lands.** **A: the rifts.** `PlateStage.segmentRifts` seeded each run's half-grabens from the run's lowest cell index, a different number at every grid, so a rift was a different chain at each: seed 42's trough held its plate floor below the shoreline over 11.8%, 2.2% and 4.5% of it at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows, and a closed basin of 2,455,444 km2 of catchment and a lake of 281,662 km2 at 512 rows alone. Each half-graben is now named by the pair's two plate ids, its stretch of rift (runs a third plate splits within the trough's half-width, `riftWidthKm`, are joined into one course) and its own place along it, and placed in kilometers along the course from an anchor the grid does not move: where the course crosses its seeds' bisector midpoint, read through the plate warp (`PlateWarp`, the one `assignPlates` bends the partition by), or the end nearer it. The centerline's stretch is `CENTERLINE_STEP_KM`, 93.75 km, where it was four cells: counted in cells the same rift measured 1.6% shorter at 256 rows than at 1,024 (4,283 against 4,353 km on seed 42's longest), a far join half a graben out; at 93.75 km, 0.3 to 1.1%. Polarity alternates by the half-graben's own ordinal from a parity drawn per course, so it does not depend on how many a grid cuts. **Earth's lengths, the maintainer's decision**: `riftSegmentMinKm` 60 and `riftSegmentMaxKm` 160 (Tanganyika's units 80-160 km and Malawi's polarity intervals 60-100, Rosendahl and others 1986-87; the western branch's ~100 km border-fault segments, Ebinger 1989; later Malawi work reads 100-200, recorded in the KDoc), and `riftAccommodationKm` 25, a zone 50 km across, "tens of kilometers" taken so the taper spans a cell at 256 rows; they were 0.04 and 0.10 of the map's width (480-1,200 km) and 0.016 (a 192 km half-length, 384 across). Set by the brittle crust's thickness (Scholz and Contreras 1998), so not scaled by the world's width. Save format 20 for the three renamed settings. **The guard**, `RiftIdentityTest`, shown failing on the cell-index key: no rift agreed on a single compared cell, ten troughs flooded a share of their corridor past the bar (0.369 against 0.560 on seed 7's 5-13, 0.017 against 0.192 on its 7-13), and every half-graben was 500-2,000 km. After: every rift of seeds 7, 42, 1234, 99, 718106 and 59758 agrees on 98.5% or more of its compared cells at 256 and 512 rows against 1,024 (bar 95%, policy), the same half-graben names over the stretch both grids reach, breaks at most 22 km apart; every trough's floor below the shoreline within the half-cell bar (0.089 and 0.054, derived); and every whole half-graben, read between its end cells' centers, walked 33-179 km and straight 33-168 km on the ground at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows and on a 20,000 km world, inside 60-160 km with two cells of slack for each ruler (33 km is one diagonal step of a 256-row grid's 23.4 km cell: a 60 km half-graben drawn in three cells). **The other grid-dependent draws**, checked and left: the crusts' choice by accumulated cells and raster adjacency differs at 256 rows on 2 of seeds 1-60 (512 and 1,024 agree on all), none of the five measured; the routing's per-cell sub-grid draw and the delta lobes' per-cell wobble are per cell by design and cannot be re-keyed, and re-drawing either at one grid moves the five seeds' lake area by up to a quarter (sub-grid -11/+4/-25/+19/-22%, wobble +23/-10/+1/-2/-7%) and the largest lake by up to half or double: the chaos floor any lake bar sits on. All three in `docs/TODO.md`. **B: fill, spill and merge.** `RiverStage` flooded a closed basin's lowest k cells at one level wherever they lay, so separate hollows below the basin's internal saddles were one lake (seed 42's lake 1 at 512 rows: eight pieces). `LakePockets` builds the basin's depression hierarchy once, by one sweep of its cells from the lowest ground up with a union-find, and lays each pocket's region out as one contiguous range so one running sum answers every pocket; no pocket copies its hypsometry and nothing floods the grid. Each pocket balances its own catchment's runoff against its own water's evaporation; a pocket full to its saddle passes only its surplus to the pocket across it (the saddle's lowest neighbor's leaf), and two become one surface only when both stand at the saddle, the supply of the pair then less what one passed the other (Bonneville's floor: Great Salt, Utah and Sevier lakes, Utah Lake spilling down the Jordan). Supplies are added leaf by leaf in index order, so every rounding is one answer on every platform. A level is where a lake filling from empty first stops paying, found on the running minimum rather than by bisection, whose precondition, a gain that falls as the area grows, fails where the high ground round a hollow is frozen: on a hand-laid hollow with a hot floor, frozen middle slopes and a hot rim the bisection lands at 34 cells and the lake stops at 3 (`LakePocketsTest`, with the spill, the merge and ties). A pocket's catchment is read where water enters the basin, less the basin's own water carried back by a path that leaves across a level rim and returns, which first counted up to 5.69 times a basin's rain (filling seed 42's rift basin at 1,024 rows to the brim) and, left out whole, lost tributaries (0.68 of one of seed 7's basins); now exact to a ten-thousandth (`LakeBodyTest`'s rain clause). Its body clause, shown failing on main (seed 42's lakes 1 and 8 in eight and five pieces, one lake in two pieces on each of 7, 1234 and 99), holds one piece per lake and no two levels touching. **GPU rule**: a sweep in ground order with a union-find and a graph solve over pockets, sequential per basin as the hypsometry sort it replaces was; no per-cell part, so no device path. **Cost**, the river stage on seed 42 with the balance against without it (fill to the brim, no pockets): 0.7 s against 0.6-0.7 s at 1,024 rows, beside a 52 s generation; 10.5-11.3 s against 9.1-9.2 s at 4,096 rows, 33.6 million cells, beside a 1,178.5 s generation, 7.8-8.6 GB in use (under a 14 GB worker set for the measurement; the audit worker's 8 GB ran out generating it). The pockets and the balance cost 1.3 to 2.2 s there, under 0.2% of the generation. **Lake area by grid** (km2 at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows and on the 512 by 512 grid; before in brackets): 42 310,913 / 330,963 / 464,172 / 321,625 (225,220 / 646,820 / 334,328 / 331,238); 7 340,027 / 229,202 / 173,653 / 213,135 (279,602 / 272,049 / 218,079 / 241,699); 99 445,496 / 280,014 / 410,751 / 310,913 (493,286 / 255,569 / 153,362 / 410,614); 718106 134,583 / 178,116 / 199,677 / 162,048 (160,400 / 200,912 / 192,364 / 165,619); 59758 244,446 / 330,276 / 343,117 / 409,515 (280,701 / 305,145 / 386,581 / 396,606). `ScaleFreeTest`'s new clause, per seed on share and largest lake with a provisional 1.35 bar, runs as a known failure: x1.96/x5.38, x1.49/x1.46, x2.10/x1.50, x1.59/x3.48 on 7, 42, 1234, 99 (main: x1.28/x1.67, x2.87/x4.73, x1.79/x2.83, x3.21/x8.08). What is left: the post-cut outlet (seed 99's 329,556 km2 at 1,024 rows stands on a floor 130 m below the sea); a rift chain below the sea is one enclosed body over the cap at 256 and 512 rows and stays sea, and at 1,024 its sills part it into land basins holding lakes; and the chaos floor. Lake shores' growth across grids is recorded without a bar (outline dimension 1.1 to 1.9 read as 1 + log2 of the growth; no lake-shore dimension sourced independently), under the comb. The Caspian clause (C-I1), pooled over 59758 and 42 at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows, reads 1.23 times the Caspian's share of the land (59758 at 1,024 rows 2.56), a known failure still. **Records moved**: `OceanLeavesTheGroundTest`'s pin (the plates' partition held; height, uplift and age moved); known failures re-recorded on the new worlds (D I-9, B-I2, the cold tongue twice, the comb, C I4, the ground texture, the thin sheets, the foreland, A-I11, the outlet notch twice, the winter wind, Q1's ice); the lowstand's estuary gain armed again (1.58 against its 1.5); `RiftSegmentationTest` re-picked by a scan widened to seeds 1-40 (seed 35: 4/3/0.43 against 1/0/0.13) and its sea-bodies clause armed again at three; `MeridionalWindTest`'s monsoon seed 29 to 9 (1.09% against 2.35%); `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s dry basin 13 to 16 (2,602 cells, 5% wet) and its lake clause stated for several pockets; four clauses the new worlds tipped recorded as known failures with the cause stated or said to be unisolated: `OutletIncisionTest`'s control, which holds an over-large lake on one seed of six (718106's 0.978% of its land) where it asks two; seed 7's channel network growing 1.38 from 512 to 1,024 rows (bar 1.35), the graded coast's gain 1.27 (floor 1.3), and `GridShapeTest`'s desert in seed 42's trough band, -0.050 against 0.047, where the control spread re-measured on the new worlds is 0.025; `ImplicitIncisionTest` counts a cut under one float step of the height as too close to judge, as its half-drop clause already did. GEOGRAPHY's rift and lake figures, TODO's lake-area entry and three new entries. In `:cartography`: the geometry census at 512 rows re-recorded from its own printout (new violations: a crease on 42's coast at (623,208), 7.51 against 6.14, under the coast's straight-run finding; round arcs on 42's and 1234's isobaths, 99's contours and its ice surface, under their findings; cleared: 7's isobath crease and its ice's square corners, whose clauses asked to be armed; coverage gained and lost on the troughs and basins); the 2,048-column census, the audit tier's, was not re-run. The render pins re-taken, all twelve moving together; ordinary ground re-derived 0.8901 to 0.8897, its definition, at which the sky's cone pins none of its bearings where the old figure pinned nine; the ink gain's and the river floor's known failures re-recorded. In `:desktop`: `OutletResolutionTest` on rows at 256, 512 and 1,024, since a 2,048-row world ran the per-merge worker out of heap; the heightmap's open-sea clause armed again, seed 42's cell above its shoreline gone with the ground. Format 20's gzip fixture and two interoperability saves regenerated. **Renders** (512 and 1,024 rows, before and after): seed 42's rift, before, is three large closed lakes at 512 rows, one with gully fingers, and two small ones and a sea arm at 1,024, a different rift at each grid; after, the same chain of basins about 110 km long at both, East Africa's shape, as inland seas at 512 rows and at 1,024 as land basins holding a chain of compact closed lakes and playas, with the sills crossing the trough as straight east-west rungs (docs/TODO.md). Seed 718106's rift is marine: after, the isthmus between its northeastern and southern lands is gone at both grids, a strait where there was a land bridge, which the site's pictures will show. Its open dendritic lake is unchanged, and still stands up the comb's gullies. **Review round.** *The sills were rungs.* A corridor cell read its place along the rift off its nearest boundary cell, so every cell of the strip that cell owns shared one place and a join ran along the strip's edge: a grid row across a north-south trough; where the rift's cells step a column, a boundary cell rows away owned cells far out on the shoulder and the place jumped by as many rows. Each course now carries a centerline (its cells' mean place in each 93.75 km stretch, a direction at each vertex from its neighbors), and a corridor cell's place is where a line square to it, turning evenly from vertex to vertex, passes through the cell (`RiftCenterline`). Each join is a relay ramp (Rosendahl 1987; Morley and others 1990): on the flank of the half-graben before it the join lies further along, on the other flank back, swinging through half the two faults' overlap (20-50 km, drawn) over a ramp of half to one and a half of the floor's half-width, bowed a quarter of the overlap halfway across, and wandering up to the accommodation zone's half-length on a 200 km noise; the lean has half the room short of the neighbors' joins (their half-gap less 12.5 km) and the wander the other half, held by a tanh so no half-graben is squeezed to nothing. `RiftSillGeometryTest` reads the plate floor's 100 m level lines over the trough's continental flanks within 50 km of a join, on seed 42's north-south rift and its two east-west ones, through every census detector at a place family of 2,000: shown failing on the rungs (straight runs along the east-west bearing 1.53 times the others', crease 9.98 against the natural 4.37); after, the bearing count 1.19 and every grid-bearing detector clean; the east-west rifts read clean before and after, too few joins in their trough for the bearing count. The crease still reads 9.38 and is recorded with its magnitude: the trough is 328 km across against half-grabens 60-160 km long, where Earth's rift valleys are 40-70 km across, so a saddle's flanks run on as long level lines (docs/TODO.md). *Tried and taken back out*: a taper over half each half-graben's length (the throw's profile, Walsh and Watterson 1987) and a flexural fade of the half-grabens' differences beyond the trough (exp(-2 pi d / L)) smoothed the trough and removed the shoulders' whiskers, and moved the climate: the ocean stopped solving on two of `MeridionalWindTest`'s worlds (residual 0.00107 and 0.00116 against 0.001) and its monsoon coast fell to 1.24%; keeping half the trough's depth through a relay softened the crease to 5.86 and spread seed 42's largest lake to x4.13 across grids. *The keys* named a stretch by its rank among the pair's courses, which is topology: cutting seed 42's rift 2-7 into three stretches with an 80 km one in the middle, and then taking it away, moved 444 of the outer stretches' 609 cells to another half-graben; the draws are now keyed by the pair and the half-graben's place in the pair's one sequence on the bisector coordinate, and none moves. At 256 rows the anchor itself sits up to 54 km off (seed 59758's 1-13 and 3-10; every rift within 17 km at 512 rows), which the identity share passes by leaving each stretch's end half-grabens out as its text allows (docs/TODO.md). *The rim return* walked on through a closed basin that keeps its water: on a hand-laid chain of a closed basin feeding a tributary back into its upper neighbor, the neighbor read 950 mm against 1,050 reaching it; the walk now stops at such a basin (`WaterReceivedTest`), and `LakeBodyTest`'s rain clause compares an endorheic basin with the rain reaching its sinks. *The lake-area record* carries its magnitudes, a twentieth of tolerance, so a worsening fails as another violation; re-taken on the ramps at x1.36/x2.30, x1.56/x2.58, x1.80/x3.44 and x4.53/x13.52 on 7, 42, 1234 and 99: seed 99's largest lake is 254,883 km2 at 256 rows and 18,848 at 1,024, worse than the tree before L1, not isolated. `MeridionalWindTest` asserts its zonal control on seed 9 (1.09% against 2.35%); `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s dry basin moves to seed 10 (1,609 cells at 33 mm, 14% wet; seed 16's was 437 cells); seed 7's channel network is recorded at 1.35. Re-taken: the ground pin, the 512-row census (new: 42's isobaths a 672 km facet, 99's contours a crease of 8.98 at (875,2); moved: 42's ice edge along a row 155 steps where it was 90, 1234's isobath facets, both inked coasts' facing; cleared and armed: 42's coast crease, 42's and 1234's isobath arcs, 99's contour arc and ice-surface arc, 1234's ice edge), the render pins; the relief's exaggeration re-derived from 38.0 to 37.25 by its own rule (37.50 pins seven of the cone's bearings), 2.2% under the 512 by 512 grid's contrast, and with it `ClearStyleTest`'s realm pairs (3-7 clears) and `RiverSelectionTest`'s idle floor (seed 42 exercises it now) re-recorded. *A lake level with its brim*: seed 10's dry basin drew an endorheic lake whose surface stood at its saddle (0.08186 against 0.08186), the minimum depth over its last wet cell clipped to the brim; within a minimum depth of the brim the surface now splits the difference. *The whole suite on the new worlds*: known failures re-recorded with their new figures (D I-9's coast 3,755 mm, B-I2's dissection, the cold tongue with seed 7's east now 0.50 of 2.14 C where it was 1.42, the comb, the humid channel ratio 1.05, C I4's lakes, the ice sheets, the foreland, A-I11's jump flood, the outlet notch twice, the winter wind +0.20 m/s, Q1's ice bands now seed 7's and 42's); armed again, their clauses passing: `GridShapeTest`'s trough desert, the graded coast's gain (1.32) and `OutletIncisionTest`'s control (seed 43 now starts over the Caspian too); `CombGuardTest`'s floor re-derived from its control, 0.021 to 0.023, where seed 42's control rows read 0.0229 on the new land; seed 7's warm coasts settle a hair under its cold ones (0.570 against 0.573, 4.1% the other way at L1), recorded as a per-seed floor beside the pooled gap it still passes. Two workers died of an access violation in the collector under the full suite's load, the machine's known fault; their classes passed alone. *Renders after the round*: seed 42's rift at 512 and 1,024 rows is the same chain at both, closed lakes in the north and a sea arm in the south, the lakes' outlines leaning with the relays and no bar across the trough on the eroded ground (on the plate floor the joins still cross the shoulders square to the rift, docs/TODO.md); seed 718106's rift is a strait at both grids; seed 1's opening window, 1,600 by 800 at (3,264, 848) of its 2,048-row sheet, keeps its range, coasts and rivers, and the two lakes at the range's southern foot are one lake now. **GPU rule**: the corridor's place and the relay are part of `stampEpoch`'s processor pass, which has no device path; not added here. **The valleys at Earth's width, the maintainer's decision.** `riftWidthKm` 27.5 (a valley 55 km across, the middle of the East African valleys' 40-70 km: Tanganyika about 50, Malawi 40-75; Rosendahl 1987, Ebinger 1989), `riftShoulderOffsetKm` 45 and `riftShoulderWidthKm` 60 (the flanks crest close behind the escarpment and fall over a plate's flexural half-wavelength, Weissel and Karner 1989), where they were 164.0625, 257.8125 and 164.0625, the 512 grid's 7, 11 and 7 cell widths. In km and not scaled by the planet: the valley is 2.3, 4.7 and 9.4 cells across at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows on the 12,000 km world, 1.4, 2.8 and 5.6 on a 20,000 km one. At 256 rows it is the rift's cells and one either side, its flat floor held at one cell (27.5 x 0.55 = 15.1 km, under a 23.4 km cell); guards on the trough's shape read 512 rows and finer. What reads the width: the stamp's floor, walls and shoulders and the relay ramps (whose lean now crosses a 30 km floor), the joining of a split rift's runs (within the valley's half-width), and the guards' corridors (`RiftIdentityTest`, `RiftSegmentationTest`, the sill guard, which now reads the shoulders' reach, 105 km); the margin blur, the craton reach and the flexure do not read it. *Re-measured*: the sill guard's crease reads 3.17 against the natural 4.37 on seed 42's north-south rift with no depth kept through the relays, and is armed; so would the rungs, 2.56, since a join square to a valley 4.7 cells across is too short a line for the census, so the guard no longer separates the two and stands as a check on the valley and its shoulders. The shoulders still switch at each join square to the rift, over their 105 km and faintly on the plate floor. The lake census (km2 at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows and 512 by 512): 42 448,242 / 422,287 / 363,510 / 401,550; 7 450,439 / 356,094 / 270,744 / 317,780; 99 235,107 / 285,233 / 294,914 / 167,816; 718106 152,161 / 141,312 / 191,471 / 171,936; 59758 249,939 / 260,651 / 310,467 / 367,767. `ScaleFreeTest`'s spreads x1.66/x2.47, x1.23/x1.31, x2.21/x2.56, x1.26/x1.38 on 7, 42, 1234, 99, re-taken. *Seed 99's spread isolated* with the probe's toggles, its largest lake at 256, 512 and 1,024 rows: in the 328 km trough 254,883 / 96,130 / 18,848 km2; with the post-cut outlet off 319,153 / 329,453 / 332,027; with enclosed water drawn as sea 54,932 / 20,874 / 18,848. So it was a drowned trough basin held as land by the enclosed-water rule, which the outlet pass drained at 1,024 rows and not at 256: L2's pass count on L1's wide trough, far past the chaos floor's quarter. At Earth's width the basin is not made: 65,369 / 85,419 / 90,157 (outlet off 488,342 / 274,246 / 291,069, recorded for L2; enclosed as sea 65,369 / 35,980 / 71,033). *Through-channels, the maintainer's decision*: whether a rift holds closed basins or drains along its axis is an outcome, as on Earth (Tanganyika, Malawi, Turkana and the Dead Sea closed; the Rhine, the Rio Grande and the Jordan through), and nothing shapes the relays or the depth to prevent either. `RiftSegmentationTest`'s sea-body and land-bridge clauses asked a flooded rift's segments to stay apart and are printed now, not asserted; its width clause cannot tell the plain trough from the segmented at this width (seed 35: 4 bodies, 5 bridges, 0.52 against 2, 0 and 0.41) and is printed too. The KDoc of `riftDepth` and `segmentRifts` restated. *Rift prevalence at 512 rows*, reported and not asserted (continental rifts, both plates continental): total axis 3,024, 4,397, 6,640, 2,341, 4,189 and 1,460 km on seeds 7, 42, 1234, 99, 718106 and 59758, 110, 160, 242, 85, 152 and 53 km per million km2 of land (27.5 Mkm2 each), mean 134; 1, 1, 2, 1, 3 and 1 systems; drowned 0, 6, 40, 4, 34 and 100% of the axis; of the dry axis cells, drained to closed basins 100, 65, 0, 56, 26 and 0%, draining 160 km or more down the valley to the sea 0, 21, 43, 35 and 33% (seed 59758 has no dry axis). Earth's active continental rifts: East African eastern branch 2,200 km and western 2,100, Baikal 2,000, Rio Grande 1,000, the European Cenozoic system about 1,100 (the Upper Rhine graben 300 of it), Shanxi 1,200 and the West Antarctic system 3,000, 12,600 km over 149 Mkm2 of land, 85 km per million km2 (64 without the West Antarctic system, under ice and largely below sea level); the Red Sea, the Gulf of Aden and the Gulf of California are left out as young oceans floored by new oceanic crust, which the generator draws as ocean ridges, and the Basin and Range as a diffuse province rather than a valley. The generator's rifts run 0.6 to 2.8 times Earth's, 1.6 on the mean (docs/TODO.md). *Re-taken for the width*: the ground pin, the 512-row census, `ScaleFreeTest`'s records (the channel network's recorded seed is 1234 now, at 1.35), C I4's glacial lakes; `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s dry basin to seed 27 (1,723 cells at 40 mm, 22% wet; seed 10's kept 62%). Renders in the app's own drawing, the export path: seed 42's rift is a narrow valley holding a chain of small lakes and an axial river at both grids, more of it under water at 512 rows; 718106's rift is a submarine trough along the coast at both; seed 1's opening window keeps its range and coasts, the lakes at its southern foot small again. *The suite on Earth's width*: recorded as known failures, their causes not isolated: `GridShapeTest`'s deserts (seed 99's band at 45 to 30 degrees, -0.067) and polar warmth (seed 99's band at 90 to 75 degrees, +0.49 C), its Q1 ice band now seed 99's at -60 to -75 degrees (+0.024); the graded coast's gain, 0.341 against 0.266 (1.28 under the 1.3 floor); the ice's comb on seed 42, 2.23% of its standing water over the bar; and four realms landlocked inside one neighbor against three (`RealmSpreadTest`). Armed again: seed 7's warm-coast floor in `OceanCurrentTest`, its coasts back over it. The other known failures re-recorded with their new figures; C-I1 now reads 1.34 times the Caspian's share pooled (59758 at 1,024 rows 2.68). *The relief re-derived by its own rule*: the exaggeration 37.25 to 37.75 (38.00 pins four of the cone's bearings), the lamp's contrast 0.2044 against 0.2078 on the 512 by 512 grid, 1.6% under; ordinary ground 0.8897 to 0.8963 under the declared sky, the haze still matched at 0.10. With them the twelve render pins re-taken, the ink gain's known failure re-recorded (the land slope's seventy-fifth percentile rounds to 0.38 against the widest stroke at 0.39), and the heightmap's open-sea clause recorded again as a known failure, one sea cell of seed 42 at 512 rows standing over its shoreline once more (docs/TODO.md, reopened). *Main's record mode and test tiers merged in*: one record-mode pass (`-Precord`) of the everyday tier and of the deep tier from the plates on (`-Pstages=plates`, which reaches every stage) in `:worldgen`, `:cartography` and `:desktop` wrote no record, and `applyPinRecords -Preview` found none: no known failure's signature, census entry or render hash moved, and no clause asked to be armed. The 512-row census, the sill guard (bearing count 1.03, crease 3.17 against 4.37) and `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s dry seed 27 (1,723 cells at 40 mm of rain against 576 mm of evaporation, 22% of it wet at balance) hold on the final tree. **Tests**, counted from the JUnit XML on the final tree: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 232 tests and `:worldgen:deepTest` 120, 0 failed (23 known failures reported); `:cartography:jvmTest` 128 and `:cartography:deepTest` 3, 0 failed (7 known failures); `:desktop:test` 130 and `:desktop:deepTest` 26, 0 failed, 6 skipped (the card's benchmarks; 3 known failures). `:worldgen:wasmJsTest` 42, 0 failed, on the worlds of Earth's width before the merge; not run on the final tree. |
| G1 One grid on the desktop; the browser version cut and its app frozen | 2026-10-04 | chunk/g1-single-grid | **For the development budget the desktop makes every world on one grid, 1,024 rows (2,048 by 1,024 cells, 5.9 km a side), the browser version is no longer developed, and the browser app on the site is a stored copy marked as an older preview.** No generator code moved and no pinned figure moved: the site's figures, rendered from the engine, came out byte for byte the ones the tag's build rendered. **The browser build, removed.** `:web` (its sources, its browser tests and Karma settings), the `wasmJs` targets of `:worldgen`, `:cartography` and `:ui` with their source sets, the dead `worldgen/src/jsMain`, `kotlin-js-store`, `ui/karma.config.d`, the WGSL paths (all in `:web`), the parity tests (`CrossPlatformFingerprintTest` and CI's fingerprint comparison, the browser half of `FolderInteropTest` with `RegenerateInteropFixtures`) and `WebDeploymentContractTest`; the Node.js, Binaryen and Yarn repositories and `kotlin.incremental.wasm`. Kept: the accelerator seams, every `expect` with the JVM's `actual`, the `Platform` members a browser implements, `commonMain` where it was; docs/WEB_VERSION.md is the archive, the tag `web-frozen` (8198db27) the last commit that built it, with how to revive it. What the removed browser tiers cost, measured on the tag on this machine with the main code compiled: 4 min 47 s of wall time together — `:worldgen:wasmJsNodeTest` 4 min 36 s (42 tests), `:cartography:wasmJsNodeTest` 59 s (49), `:ui:wasmJsBrowserTest` 46 s (165), `:web:wasmJsBrowserTest` 32 s (45) — and the Wasm production bundle in every site deploy. **The site.** `:web:assembleSite` moved into `:desktop` as `assembleSite`, writing `desktop/build/site`: it unpacks `web-frozen.zip` (the `app/` tree the tag's `:web:assembleSite` wrote, less the shell; SHA-256 `1846834731…4108023`, checked against `frozenWebAppSha256`), lays `site/` over it, renders the figures and stamps the loader. `site.yml` downloads the zip from the release `web-frozen` first, and a missing release fails the deploy rather than taking `/app/` off the site. The tree assembled on the branch holds the same 62 files as the tag's, every one byte-identical but the two pages. The landing page's main call to action is the desktop download; the browser card, the Notes and the loading shell say the preview is older and runs an earlier generator; the web zip left `downloads.txt`, the page, docs/INSTALL.md and the release notes' template. `SiteAssemblyTest` reads `composeTarget` out of the stored application's wasm in place of the contract test, and its Features check holds the page to the one grid. **One grid in the interface.** The panel's and the Settings dialog's grid chips (512 to 4,096, 4,096 offered where the heap held it), the export-size row and its setting, the stored grid preference, the compact window's 512 cap and the large-link question (`LargeLinks`) are gone; a world starts at `Platform.defaultResolution`, and an export is the world on screen at its own grid. `forRows` and `atResolution` stay as APIs; `WorldCeilings` stays as the guard on a save of another grid, which opens as before; no format change. **Cross-grid clauses report** (`CrossGridReport`, `CROSS-GRID holds` or `departs` and the figures): `ScaleFreeTest`'s four (the statistics and ground at 512 and 1,024, the plate seeds at every grid, the standing water at 256, 512 and 1,024 and the channel-head density, the last two known failures until now), `ScaleFreeAuditTest`'s one, `PipelineTest`'s land fraction at 128 and 512 rows, `GridShapeTest`'s four bands (three of them known failures), `TectonicGroundTest`'s belts against 512 by 512, `RiftIdentityTest`'s two (half-grabens and trough share), `GlaciationAuditTest`'s lake country at 512 and 2,048, `RiverSelectionTest`'s and `RiverSelectionAuditTest`'s one pane from several grids, and `ReliefShadingTest`'s contrast against the 512 by 512 grid. Their controls, which fail when a comparison cannot see a difference, stay armed, as do every single-grid guard, `UnitsTest`, `ResolutionScalingTest`, `PenAndInkTest`, `RiverWidthTest`'s pen and the geometry census, which hold arithmetic or one grid. On the branch the deep classes report what they recorded before: standing water and channel density, deserts, polar warmth and ice depart; the rest holds. **Tests, from the XML**: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 226 (65 classes), `:ui:jvmTest` 160 (19), `:desktop:test` 125 (26 classes), `:desktop:siteTest` 30, all passing; targeted, `:worldgen:deepTest` `ScaleFreeTest`, `GridShapeTest`, `TectonicGroundTest` and `PipelineTest`'s deep methods 17, `:cartography:deepTest` `RiverSelectionTest` and `ReliefShadingTest` 2, `:desktop:deepTest` `WorldLinkDesktopTest` 4, `:cartography:jvmTest` `GzipInteroperabilityTest` 1; the audit classes demoted were compiled, not run. |
| K1 Every figure on the ground, so a planet of any size draws the same physics | 2026-10-05 | chunk/k1-figures-on-the-ground | **The noise lattices, the rain's conversion, the map-width divisors, two areas and the river pen are stated on the ground at today's values, bit for bit on the 12,000 km world; the climate's weather noise and the ice sheet's scour are made square on the ground, which moves every world; and guards hold every figure at half and twice the planet's width.** Worked from the Earth-size audit (2026-10-04), each of its claims read against the code first. **Part 1, the restatements.** `GroundLattice` now takes a wavelength in kilometers and reads it as whole cycles round the planet, `round(worldWidthKm / wavelengthKm)`, at least one, so the lattice closes at the antimeridian on any planet (the audit's D3); down the map it advances by a row's own height on the ground, as before, and its KDoc no longer says every map's cells are twice as wide as tall. The wavelengths, each the 12,000 km world's count turned into kilometers: the plate warp 2,000 km (6 cycles), the margins 500 (24), the terrain's base 3,000 (4, `TerrainConfig.baseWavelengthKm`), the belts' range swell 923.1 (13, `rangeVariationWavelengthKm`), width swell 1,714.3 (7), rim jitter 705.9 (17), crest roughness 1,000 (12), arc chain 461.5 (26) and seamount size 1,333.3 (9), the fine relief 125 (96, `detailWavelengthKm`), the relay crest 200, the weather noise 2,400 across and 1,200 down (5 and 5), the sheet's basins 461.5 (26, `sheetBasinWavelengthKm`) with hummocks a third of it, and the routing's smooth field 187.5 km, now 64 lattice cells round the planet with each cell's place worked out in whole numbers, which is the period-in-cells arithmetic to the bit wherever the cells divide the map and closes the seam where they do not. No lattice the audit missed: the only other noises are per-cell hashes, the Fourier transforms' wavenumbers and the hachures' pitch on the sheet. The rain's conversion (D1) reads a fixed `ClimateStage.REFERENCE_CELL_WIDTH_KM` of 23.4375 km, `12,000 / 512`, where it read this planet's own 512 grid. The divisors are kilometers read as the whole cells inside them (`WorldGenConfig.cellsWithin`): the strait reach 300 km, the capital's hinterland 187.5 and relief 125, the rain blur 93.75. `SeaConfig.enclosedSeaMaxShareOfSurface` (0.00073) and `GlaciationConfig.maxLakeShareOfSurface` and `minLakeShareOfSurface` (0.00016 and 0.000016) are the shares of the surface their derivations name, 52,560, 11,520 and 1,152 km² on the 12,000 km world and the Caspian and Superior themselves on Earth's. `RiverPen.FULL_STROKE_KM` is 28.8 km, the 0.24% of the 12,000 km world's width the stroke was held at. Save format 21 for the seven renamed settings; the gzip fixture regenerated, and the browser preview's folder save and `FolderInteropTest` gone, as rule 11 says. **Not restated, and why.** Every share in the audit's rank 10 is a share of a total that differs from seed to seed (the land, its runoff, the largest river, the frozen ground), so no area is today's value and a restatement moves every world, which Part 1 could not. The basin, region and island-realm shares and the landmarks' spacing size the pieces of a count per world, and wait with the counts; `riverBorderShare`, `RIVERINE_FLOW_SHARE`, `DRAWN_RIVER`, `deltaMinCatchment`, `minCatchment` and `fullCatchment` should be discharges or areas and are in `TODO.md`; `trunkCatchment` and `outletCatchment` are shares of a body's own ice and are right as shares. Each says so in its KDoc. **Weak claims corrected:** the README's "every setting is a length on the ground", the `GroundLattice` KDoc, `epochDriftKm`'s plate radius (about 1,300 km on a 14-plate 12,000 km world, not 3,200), `SEED_REFERENCE_CELLS`, `millimetresPerMarchUnit`, `STREAMLINE_CELLS` (141 km of flow line on the 512 by 512 grid, 35 on 1,024 rows: the elongation falls with the grid, `TODO.md`), the rain blur, the glacial lakes' cell counts, `RiverPen`'s `NationsConfig.worldWidthKm`, `riverBorderShare`'s "share of all land" (it is a share of the largest river's flow). **The proof of Part 1**: per-branch digests of eight worlds (seed 42 at 128 and 512 rows and at 512 by 512 and 128 by 128, 969495 at 256 and 1,024 rows, 718106 at 256, 7 at 32) identical to main's on all 49 branches outside the settings; the everyday tier 639 tests, none failed, no pin moved. **Part 2.** *The weather noise* covered as many cycles from pole to pole as round the equator, twice as long east-west as north-south on the ground; it is a ground lattice of 2,400 km. *The scour (D2)*: the basins' and hummocks' lattices likewise, now square. On the standard worlds D2 is under what a basin can show (seven seeds at 512 rows read 0.6 to 1.6 east-west against north-south, either way), so `ScourBearingTest` hands the stage a flat frozen plain, eight seeds at 256 rows, with its largest basin opened: main's basins read 1.56 times as long east-west as north-south against a bar of three standard errors (1.07), the branch's 0.994 (bar 0.914 to 1.094). *Moved*: seed 42's ground pin in its erosion and sea branches (terrain and plates held, the provisional climate feeding the erosion); the twelve render hashes, twice, once for the worlds and once for the relief re-derived by its own rule, 37.75 to 37.50 (37.75 pins four of the cone's bearings; contrast 0.2044 against 0.2077, 1.6% under); the 512-row census (new: 7's drawn coast an arc of 122.8, 7's isotherms along a row, 42's realm borders a facet of 53, 1234's isotherm facet, 99's sheet ground and carving along a row, 48 and 46 steps; cleared: 42's isobath right angle, 42's ice facet and isotherm facets, 99's ice along a row); known failures re-recorded with their figures (the jump flood 6 to 7 cells, seed 99's windward coast 3,737 to 3,708 mm, the cold tongue, the graded coast, the thin sheets, C I4's cold lakes, the Caspian clause with seed 7's largest lake 2.25 to 1.73 Caspians, the notch's fill 81.1% to 102.5% of the control's, B-I2's dissection, the ground texture, the foreland's moat 711 to 684 m, the winter wind +0.22 to +0.16 m/s, the symmetric comb). Armed again, passing: `RealmSpreadTest`'s landlocked realms and `GlaciationCombTest`'s comb on seed 42. Recorded, causes stated: seed 42's flanks one-sided by a hair (1.52 against `CombGuardTest`'s 1.5), the level-sill control (0.1371% against 0.1370%, too close to tell the rules apart), and the ocean's stall: seed 42 at 256 rows under the belts' wind alone stops at 0.00120 against the 0.001 tolerance (the weather noise alone does it; with the scour's change taken out it stayed), which four clauses that read that control world now pass by through `OceanStall` (`TODO.md`; L1 took its change out over the same stall, this one is the brief's). `PipelineTest` holds a cell on a raised flat, routed by the flat's potential to a receiver level with it, to the weaker rule. Renders at 1,024 rows on the 12,000 km world, seeds 42 and 969495, main against the branch: the tints shift a little and the glaciated plateau's scour lakes sit elsewhere; neither shows a grain along the rows. **Part 3, the guards.** `GroundFiguresTest` reads every `GroundLattice(…)`, `GroundLattice.wholeCycles(…)` and `cellsWithin(…)` out of the main sources and holds each lattice's wavelength on the ground within half a cycle's share of the circumference, and each reach within a cell, at 6,000, 12,000 and 24,000 km on three grids; the areas to the planet's area and to the 12,000 km world's square kilometers; the rain's conversion to the same millimeters per kilometer of travel; and the source to four rules — no declaration named for cycles, a frequency, a divisor or a share of the map's width unless computed from kilometers (the multigrid's `MOST_CYCLES` and the coast pen, `TODO.md`, let stand by name), every Perlin call on a lattice's `x` and `y`, every lattice and reach from a figure in kilometers, and no grid dimension divided by anything but two (`IceSheetParity`'s fixture let stand). On main's source the rules name 34 places, every lattice and divisor this chunk restated among them; with the lattices given main's fixed counts and the rain main's reference, the lattice and conversion clauses fail on every lattice and every planet width. `PlanetWidthRainTest` (deep, climate) holds land rain on seeds 42, 969495 and 7 at 256 rows on planets half and twice as wide within the seeds' own spread on the 12,000 km world (1.63 for the mean, 1.39 for the 99.5th percentile): the branch reads 1.51 and 0.94 for the mean, 1.32 and 0.94 for the percentile; main read 2.01 and 1.80 on the planet twice as wide. That a planet half as wide is wetter by half is not isolated (`TODO.md`). `RiverPenGroundTest` holds the stroke at 28.8 km and at 0.0024 of the sheet on the 12,000 km world to the bit. `EarthSizeGeometryReport` (deep, drawing) prints the census on seeds 42 and 969495 at 40,075 km and 1,024 rows, a report and not a gate: main 33 clauses in violation (coast facets 9 and 4, plate-boundary facets 17 and 14, isobath facets 41 and 45, isotherm facets, alignment and isotropy on both, biome edges along rows 5 and 2, ice as drawn); the branch 9 (coast facets none, plate-boundary facets 1, isobath facets 3 and 1, isotherms none, biome edges a right angle on 42, peoples' borders a right angle on 969495, a valley glacier's crease, the coast's corner rate on 42, both inked coasts' facing). **Earth's size at 1,024 rows, against the audit's B sheets**: the straight-edged plate polygons, ruled rifts and constant-width belts are gone; coasts are ragged and continents irregular, belts swell and break, and the tints no longer run in wet green bands along the rows; the zonal aridity bands remain as broad latitudinal stripes. Land rain is 395 and 348 mm on seeds 42 and 969495 (the audit's 1,539 to 1,674 at 512 rows, Earth's 715), the ice-sheet biome 1.7 and 2.7% of the land, and seed 42's largest lake 2.79 Caspians (the audit's 1.09; D6's class). **Tests**, counted from the JUnit XML on the final tree: `:worldgen:jvmTest` 232 and `:worldgen:deepTest` 121, `:cartography:jvmTest` 130 and `:cartography:deepTest` 4, `:desktop:test` 124 and `:desktop:deepTest` 24 (6 skipped, the card's benchmarks), `:ui:jvmTest` 160; none failed. |
| K2 An Earth-sized planet by default, with every geographic figure set from Earth | 2026-10-08 | chunk/k2-earth-default | **The default planet is Earth's, 40,075 km round (2,048 by 1,024 cells of 19.57 km), and the tectonic counts, the drift, the hotspot trails and the catchment areas are set from Earth's figures, each per area or length so a planet of another size keeps them per area; D6's lake is fixed at its cause, the glaciation's octagonal window is a disc, and the ocean stall is gone.** Geography only: realms, peoples, landmarks and the atlas's ranks are untouched and listed in `TODO.md`'s atlas-overhaul entry. **The tectonic figures.** `plateCount` became `meanPlateAreaKm2`, Earth's 510.1 Mkm² over its 15 plates (7 major, about 8 minor), so `plateCount(scale)` is the sphere (`WorldScale.sphereAreaKm2`, width²/π) over it, 15 on Earth's and 2 on the 12,000 km world; the partition does not match Earth's two populations (Bird 2003; Morra et al. 2013): seed 42's plates on the sphere run from 2.77 to 0.28 of the mean, Earth's from 3.0 to about 0.007 (`TODO.md`). `hotspotPlateFraction` became `hotspotsPerMillionKm2`, Courtillot et al.'s 49 (EPSL 205, 2003) over Earth's surface, drawn uniformly on the sphere and stamped only on oceanic plates. `epochDriftKm` is derived: `plateSpeedMmPerYear` 28 (the median RMS speed of plates more than a quarter cratonic, Zahirovic et al. 2015) over `epochLengthYears` 300 Myr, 8,400 km, and a drift past a pole carries the seed over it. The trails: 5,800 km long (Hawaiian-Emperor, Lō'ihi to the trench, 81 Myr at 72 mm a year), volcanoes 40 to 60 km apart, 50 km in the middle (USGS HVO; ten Brink 1991), a base radius of 72.5 km (half Mauna Loa's), each volcano's volume log-uniform over the decade below the largest (Robinson and Eakins 2006) and each plume's flux drawn over Sleep's factor of twenty with his mean (a density falling as the inverse square of the flux; Sleep 1990), radius and height as the cube roots. **The trails are not ruled.** With every plume fixed and one drift a plate, the trails were dashed lines thousands of kilometers long, parallel across each plate (rule 13). Each plate's past drift now turns at the planet's reorganizations, 3,960 km of travel apart (55 Myr: Matthews et al. 2012's 105-100 Ma and Torsvik et al. 2017's 47 Ma), by an angle uniform up to the Hawaiian-Emperor bend's 60 degrees, every trail on a plate bending at the same age as the Pacific's do; and each plume drifts at 0.23 of its plate's speed (Tarduno et al. 2003: more than 40 mm a year from 81 to 47 Ma, about none since, against the plate's 72) on a course that wanders smoothly over 2,450 km of travel (the 34 Myr the Hawaiian plume held one course). `HotspotTrailTest` (everyday) reads every run of volcanoes on its own plate longer than one course on seeds 42, 969495, 7, 1234 and 99 at 512 rows, and calls one ruled when the narrowest strip holding it is under one base radius across: fixed plumes and unturning plates rule 82 of 82, the shipped trails 14 of 80, the bar most (a population is ruled when most of it is; the Ninetyeast Ridge is a straight trail on Earth). The young end a reader sees is still near-straight and, within a plate, near-parallel, as Earth's young Pacific chains are; most chains are now small drowned seamounts. **The catchments.** `deltaMinCatchment` became `deltaMinCatchmentKm2` 70,000 (the Po; Ebro 85,000, Rhône 98,000; Caldwell et al. 2019 read a likelihood, not a threshold); `HydraulicErosion.DRAWN_RIVER` became `DRAWN_RIVER_CATCHMENT_KM2` 14,000 (the Po's over its five delta branches); the glaciers' `minCatchment` became `minCatchmentKm2`, Bahr, Meier and Peckham's length-area scaling (1997) on the Fedchenko at `minTroughLengthKm`, 7,118 km², and `fullCatchmentKm2` 24 times it, the model's own ratio. `trunkCatchment` and `outletCatchment` stay shares of their own field. **D6.** Main at Earth's size kept a lake of 2.79 Caspians on seed 42 (1,034,600 km², endorheic, 2.9 S), because `LakesConfig.runoffFraction` handed every basin a third of its rain, Earth's global share, where Earth's falls with dryness. A lake's catchment now sheds Budyko's share (1974), `1 - sqrt(f tanh(1/f)(1 - e^-f))` with `f` Thornthwaite's potential evaporation over the rain (`LakeWaterBalance.runoffShareOfRain`), accumulated by the river stage; `runoffFraction` is gone. Seed 42's largest is 1.00 Caspians in km², 0.49 of the Caspian's share of land, 969495's 1.34 and 0.65, 7's 0.46 and 0.22; `OutletIncisionTest`'s and `OutletResolutionTest`'s Caspian clauses (C-I1) pass and are armed. What stays over a Caspian in km² sits in closed basins on a dry equator, losing only Thornthwaite's evaporation (`TODO.md`). **The octagon.** `GlaciationConfig.reliefWindowOctagon` became `reliefWindowShape`, a disc on the ground by default (square and octagon kept as controls), one half-width slide per distinct row folded down the column; `ReliefWindowTest` reads one summit's relief radius by bearing: the disc spreads 1.92% and 1.67% (square and half-height cells) against bars of 2.44 and 1.95, the square 36.9 and 80.0, the octagon 12.4 and 73.3. No device path (`TODO.md`). **The ocean stall** K1 recorded no longer happens at Earth's size; `OceanStall` is gone and its four clauses armed. **The default.** `WorldScale.worldWidthKm` is `EARTH_EQUATOR_KM`; the 12,000 km world stays constructible, and `CalibrationPlanet` (shared test support: 12,000 km, fourteen plates) is where a guard measuring an operator or a drawing constant at the cell size it was set on builds its worlds, each saying why: the gallery, the isobaths, the relief shading, the river pen's span, the thermal aspect, the shelf contour, the comb guard, the boundary pairs and the rift sill, and in this chunk's second half the margin's and rift's bearings (`TectonicGroundTest`), the reuse chain (`IncrementalReuseTest`, whose settings move nothing on 157 km cells), the ribbon strips (`RibbonLandTest`, whose strip is still a share of the map's width), the two basin samples of `LakeWaterBalanceTest` and the ground's texture (`GroundTextureTest`, whose 94 km box is under three of the Earth-sized planet's 39 km cells at 512 rows). The hotspot cone guard stamps one cone of the largest radius with the stage's own stamp, since a generated world's highest cone is now a few cells. The zonal march's reference read the rain blur as a 128th of the map, four cells where production's 93.75 km is one at 256 rows; it reads production's figure. README, the site's resolution row and relief scale, and `SiteImagery`'s KDoc follow the default. Save format 22 for the renamed settings; the gzip fixture regenerated. **Pins and bars.** Re-recorded and reviewed: the render hashes, the geometry census, the ground digests and the known failures' signatures. Re-derived: `DataExportTest`'s JPEG drift by its own halfway rule (54 to 63), the scale bar (9.784 km a pixel, a 2,000 km bar), `UnitsTest`'s rounding tolerance, `LakeBodyTest`'s float floor, `GroundFiguresTest`'s reference. | G2 The drawn coast from the shoreline's own level line, inked alike on every side | 2026-10-09 | chunk/g2-drawn-geometry | **Every clause the geometry census makes on a drawn layer, traced to its operator; the drawing's own cleared, the fields' recorded.** Census on main: at 512 rows four clauses, each world's inked coast by facing (7, 42, 1234, 99: the most-drawn facing 2.66, 3.92, 3.60 and 3.75 times the least); at Earth's size eight (`EarthSizeGeometryReport`, 42 and 969495): both inked coasts' facing (3.60, 3.03), 42's terrain contours and ice surface, one arc of 139 degrees about (1153, 1001), the sea temperature anomaly's aligned side on both, an isobath facet and a plate-boundary facet on 969495. No clause stood on the drawn coast's right angles or corner rate at either grid (42's drawn coast at Earth's size held two right angles over 15,658 cell widths, under the rate's bar), and none on the rivers. **The drawing's: the inked coast.** `MapRasterizer.drawCoastline` inked a bank cell whole where the cell east or south of it was open water, so north- and west-facing shores were a quarter to two fifths drawn, and the shader copied it; the overlay (`Shoreline`) ran along the half-cell lattice, every vertex halfway between two cells. **The fix.** `CoastLine` is the shoreline as the ground's own level line: marching squares round the banks (`NarrowSea.banks`), each crossing where the straight line through the two cells' altitudes in meters, each on its own half of the ruler, reaches zero, and halfway only where the mask and the ground disagree (a channel too narrow for a coast, a bank whose floor is under water: 1,622 cells of seed 42 at 512 rows; its 527 cells of land below the waterline touch no sea). The raster inks every cell, land and water, by its distance on the true-shape sheet from that line, full on it and nothing a cell away (`CoastLine.reachPixels`, a smoothstep), so a straight coast takes one cell of ink across it, the old line's weight, centered on the waterline. The overlay traces the same line, smooths it along its own length with a Gaussian of half a cell integrated over the line in eighths of a sigma (`Shoreline.smoothedAlongTheCurve`), which moves no vertex further than `sigma * sqrt(2 / pi)`, 0.40 of a cell, and simplifies it as before. The crossings from land toward open sea spread across the edge (seed 42 at 512 rows: quartiles 0.21, 0.47 and 0.75 of the way to the water cell, mean 0.48), so the line has no bias toward either side. The shader carries the same arithmetic, handed the reach, the two rulers' meters and the marching squares' table rather than copies of them. **Fidelity** (`CoastLineTest`, everyday): against the field's shoreline traced by the census's own tracer on the altitudes, every inked cell's ink is the pen's at its distance to within 7.7e-5 (42) and 7.2e-5 (7) over the 10,965 of 13,597 and 9,399 of 12,045 inked cells where ground and mask agree, the old operator off by 1.0; the drawn coast's vertices stray 0.12 and 0.13 cells at the median and 0.222 and 0.224 at worst against the bound of 0.399, the old trace 0.499; on sloping planes at 24 bearings, on square cells and on cells two pixels wide, the ink is the pen's at the true distance to 5.4e-6 and smoothing moves a straight shore by 4.9e-6 pixels. **Controls:** the old raster operator, reproduced, fails the facing clause on 42 and 7 (north and west 26% and 38% drawn) where the rendering draws all four facings 100%; the old trace fails the fidelity bound. The census's natural shorelines (four fields of its isotropic noise, 132,748 cell widths) drawn by the new operator at the census's pane fit no arc, and a stamped disc of 24 cells drawn the same way is found. `GeneralisationTest`'s vertex clause, a known failure under Audit III F-D2, is armed: every traced vertex's nearest cell is inked (8,479 vertices, none bare). `RasterMarkWidthTest` reads the mark as the pen's, two pixels both ways at its commonest and one at its thinnest, where it was the landward cell's one. **GPU**: `GpuRasterTest` on the card, 15 views by 12 styles, 99.9th percentile drift 0, worst channel 1, 0.0007% of pixels differing; cells two pixels wide, worst 1. **Census after**: at 512 rows two clauses, 7's and 99's drawn coasts an arc each (124 degrees of circles 12.0 and 12.1 cells round), the facing gone on all four; at Earth's size seven, the facing gone on both, 42's drawn coast an arc (124 degrees of 12.1 about (759, 761)) and the six field clauses unchanged; 42's drawn coast at Earth's size now turns no right angle, and its longest straight run is 38.7 cell widths where it was 34.7 (bar 49.8). **The new arcs are the ground's**: the old staircase's quarter-cell scatter kept these round shores under the arc detector's rms bar; with `SeaConfig.littoralDepositionalShare` at zero 7's and 42's are gone (the grading's curvature flow rounds a headland), 99's stays and is not isolated; recorded under the coast's arc finding. **The fields' clauses**, in `TODO.md`: 969495's isobath facet is the 5,000 m level line running straight across plate 13, 145 to 183 cell widths from any boundary; its plate-boundary facet is the partition's; the anomaly's aligned sides lie within three degrees of the equator, varying under 0.05 C along 80 columns and 0.5 C over three rows; 42's arc is the ice sheet's surface wrapping an ice-free hollow. Also listed there: `NarrowSea`'s square window, and Audit III F-C4's level-0 contour, outside this chunk. **Records**: the 512-row census re-recorded (four facings off, two arcs on); the twelve styles' render records re-taken, every style moving with its coast. The 2048-column census (`GeometryGuardAuditTest`, audit tier) was not run: it was already behind the default planet on main (42's facing recorded 3.147 against main's 3.599) and wants an 8 GB heap the machine did not have free; its seven facing entries will clear. **Tests**, counted from the JUnit XML: `:cartography:jvmTest` 135, `:cartography:deepTest` (drawing) 4 and `:desktop:test` 124 on the final tree, and `:ui:jvmTest` 160 on an earlier one, the module being untouched; none failed, none skipped. `:worldgen` is not touched and was not run. |
| H1 Earth's hypsometry on the Earth-sized planet | 2026-10-10 | chunk/h1-hypsometry | **The land is the continental crust above the sea's cut, so its hypsometry is set by where the cut stands on the platform and by the platform's own shape; the cut now drowns Earth's 29% of the crust, the standard column floats at Earth's platform's 374 m rather than at the land's 840, and the platform's closed sags are filled to their spill.** (This label is also the 2026-09-12 row *H1 Tectonic history*; the chunk was planned under it.) **Diagnosis**, seed 42 at 1,024 rows on main, each part's mean over the finished land: the column's own altitude above the shoreline 709 m (the platform at 840 m, the cut 245 m above the isostatic datum at the plate stage and 95 m after erosion), the craton's tilt +42, the belts and their detail +212, the base relief and texture +89 (-255 on the lowest third of the land), erosion -158 (-270 on the lowest third), the sea stage +1; seeds 969495 and 7 read 508 and 579 m of platform, +224 and +211 of belts, cuts 447 and 408 m above the datum. Main's continental crust stood 93% in 500 m-1 km before its relief and its tilt, and 52% in 1-2 km with the tilt; the land was that hump, centred 600 to 700 m above the sea. The cut climbed because `continentalCrustSubmergedShare` was S2's 0.20, measured on the 12,000 km planet's crust at its datum, where the Earth-sized planet's continents, three times as wide against the same margins, drown 12.5% of themselves there (seed 42). The interiors flood in the base relief's sags: of the drowned crust more than 800 km from its edge, 80, 90 and 85% was already under water at the plate stage, its relief 958, 857 and 706 m below the mean, and 10, 5 and 8% of it lies within 600 km of a present plate boundary; the sea stage moves the land by a metre, and erosion, its deltas included, lowers it (the lowstand was not run apart). Measured and not taken: the freeboard alone (500 m: the cut follows it and no band moves), a submerged share of 0.10 (the cut falls below the datum and the plains rise, mean 1,068 m), the relief scaled by 0.45 to 0.8 (the bands come toward Earth's, but no Earth figure asks for it: the platform's spread in Earth's window already reads 532 to 550 m against ETOPO5's 503), the map-scale relief share at 0.05, and the craton's tilt off (lowlands up two points, interiors drowned further: 0.39 within 800 km). **The three changes.** The submerged share is Earth's 0.29 (Cogley 1984: continental crust 41.2% of the surface, land 29.2%). The freeboard is Earth's continental platform, the mean altitude of the six ice-free continents' land under 2 km and shelves to 500 m in NOAA's ETOPO5 by continent in 500 m bands, 374 m, where 840 m was the mean of all land, orogens and Antarctica's ice included, which this generator lays on the column itself; the cut then lands -169 to +133 m from the datum on the five `IsostasyTest` seeds at 256 rows. `TectonicsConfig.basinFill` fills the platform's closed sags with sediment to their spill before the belts are laid on, by a priority flood seeded from the ocean floor (`PlateStage.fillContinentalBasins`): Bohacs's overfilled lake basin (AAPG 1999), whose accommodation is less than its catchment supplies; a sag open to the sea by lower ground, and every rift and moat, is left. **After**, at 1,024 rows on seeds 42, 969495 and 7: land under 200 m 24.2, 25.7 and 23.1% (Earth 25), 200-500 m 26.2, 26.8 and 21.5 (22), 500 m-1 km 25.9, 21.2 and 28.3 (22), 1-2 km 16.6, 10.7 and 14.1 (19); mean land 736, 961 and 982 m; the platform in Earth's window 392 m about the sea with a spread of 507 on seed 42 against ETOPO5's 374 and 503. Pooled over the standard worlds at 512 rows in ETOPO5's bands: under 500 m 0.480 against 0.537 +- 0.097, 500 m-1 km 0.276 against 0.196 +- 0.029, 1-2 km 0.155 against 0.154 +- 0.033, above 2 km 0.090 against 0.113 +- 0.077; mean 831 m against 840 +- 224; water shallower than 500 m 0.283 of the land's area against 0.25 +- 0.057. Before: 0.378, 0.306, 0.243, 0.073; 885 m; 0.164. **Guards**, `HypsometryTest`: the four bands, the mean and the shelf, each against Earth's land total within its continents' spread over the square root of seven; `the guards fail on the platform before H1` (deep, plates) runs the same seeds with the three changes taken back and shows the band and shelf clauses failing there, reading main's 0.378, 0.306, 0.243, 0.073 and 0.164 to the last place. The mean clause passed on main too (885 m: the plains too high and the ranges too low cancel in the mean) and is a bar against regression. The 500 m-1 km band runs as a known failure, cause not isolated (the tilt lifts the interior about 180 m; with it out the band reads 0.228 on seed 42). **Recorded:** the drowned crust lies in the largest continents' open sags, 0.394 within 800 km of the edge at 512 rows against 0.80 (main 0.465): 29% drowned where 20% was, the shallow water as a whole Earth's, its place not (`TODO.md`); `GeographyAuditTest`'s pooled tropical desert band at 0.71 against a bar of 0.54; `LakeBodyTest`'s three-cell lake on seed 42 whose surface stands at its saddle's ground. **Armed**, nine recorded misses H1's ground clears: `CurrentFeedsRainTest`'s cold and warm coasts, `GlaciationCombTest`'s straight lake, `IsostasyTest`'s moat (62 m of 239: a thinner sheet, not a deeper moat) and foreland (750-938 km, 1,140 m under the belt, 10 m of forebulge), `ReceiverClampTest`, `SeaLevelHistoryTest`'s seed 42 and pooled estuaries (1.55x), `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s dry basin, `ClimateFedErosionTest`'s B-I2 pin, and `PenAndInkTest`'s aspect (28.9 degrees). **Re-pinned by their own rules:** `IsostasyTest`'s shoreline residual bar 1,000 to 300 m (residuals -169 to +133 against the control's +424 to +572), its craton tilt read upward from the standard column now that a column 6 km thinner stands under water, `DeltaOutlineTest`'s straight-run bar 1.2% to 0.75% (square fans 0.93%, curved 0.6%), `LakeWaterBalanceTest`'s samples by its own scan (dry seed 1, 1,748 cells at 120 mm keeping 37%; wet seed 10, 3,991 cells at 583 mm, full), seed 42's ground digests, the render hashes, the geometry census and the known failures' signatures. Isostasy's own checks: a cold oceanic column at -6,604 m against Parsons and Sclater's -6,400 (-5,926 before), thermal buoyancy 2,823 m at a ridge and 266 on the oldest floor. **Renders** at 1,024 rows, seeds 42, 969495 and 7, main against the branch (atlas sheet and elevation view, `notes/h1/renders` in the maintainer's checkout, not tracked): the plains are lowland green over most of every continent where they were yellow upland, the coasts are fronted by broad pale shelves, seed 42's south-eastern inland sea is a plain with rivers on it, and the crust drawn for Earth's share adds continents, a new one at seed 42's north-east and a joined one across seed 7's middle; but seed 969495's isthmus is now a shallow sea broken into islands and seed 42's central bay is larger, which is the drowned interior the record above names. **Tests**, counted from the JUnit XML on the final tree: the everyday tier on the final tree, `:worldgen:jvmTest` 237, `:cartography:jvmTest` 130, `:desktop:test` 124 and `:ui:jvmTest` 160, none failed; the deep tier at `-Pstages=plates`, `:worldgen:deepTest` 122 (twelve failing, each dealt with above, then their nine classes rerun at 29 and the last two at 4, none failing), `:desktop:deepTest` 24 (6 skipped, the card's benchmarks) and `:cartography:deepTest` 4, rerun alone after the processor's access violation in its first run, none failed. |
| WC1 One world cache for every checkout, kept small | 2026-10-10 | chunk/wc1-shared-cache | **T1's world cache, which each checkout kept under its own `build/world-cache` and filled to the 20 GB cap, is now one directory for every checkout and worktree on the machine, held to the cap across all of them and rid of stale code by age.** No generator code and no pinned figure moved. Five checkouts had reached 95 GB between them, and each entry stayed until the cap took it, since every code change strands the last code's entries. **Where.** `~/.cartogenesis/world-cache` (beside the application's `~/.cartogenesis/worlds`), unless `-PworldCacheDirectory` names another, or failing that the environment variable `CARTOGENESIS_WORLD_CACHE`; resolved in the test JVM by `WorldDiskCache.sharedDirectory`, so the build passes the directory only when the property is given. `-PworldCache=off` as before. **Sharing.** Entries stay keyed by the hash of the generator's classes, so different code never reads another's worlds and two checkouts of one commit share theirs. A write is a whole temporary file renamed into place, as at T1. A reader now opens the entry first and reads from that handle: Windows refuses to delete a file `FileInputStream` holds open (checked on this machine with the JDK the tests run on, as was the deletion of a locked lock file below), and elsewhere a deletion takes only the name, so no eviction cuts a read short; an entry gone before the open is a miss, with no message and no delete. T1's eviction also removed every other generator's directory that held no world yet: in a shared cache that is another code's generation under way, and since NIO opens files with delete sharing its locked lock file goes too, after which a second writer locks a new file of the same name and generates beside the first. Directories and lock files now go only with their whole generator, by age, and a writer touches its lock file when it takes it. **Kept small.** `-PworldCacheGigabytes`, 20: one generator's both tiers as T1 measured them (16.95 GB) with the standard worlds of four more (4 x 0.75 GB). Past it the least recently used go across every generator, the running generator's standard worlds last; T1 kept every standard world last, which in a shared cache would hold every commit's standard worlds over the running code's variants. Use is the file's modification time, set on every hit before the read, never the file system's access time; the eviction reads each file's time and size once before sorting, since other processes touch them meanwhile. `-PworldCacheStaleDays`, 3: at a cache's first use in a test JVM, every other generator's directory in which no file was touched for that long goes (an empty one by its own time). `clearWorldCache` empties the shared cache and this checkout's old `build/world-cache`, which `clean` never reached; `clearWorldCacheStale` removes what a first use would. Both run `WorldCacheMaintenance` on `:worldgen`'s test classpath, as `applyPinRecords` does, so the directory and the age rule are the code the guard tests; they are `:worldgen`'s tasks, registered from the root script, because Gradle refuses the root project's task resolving another project's classpath. Run through Gradle here: `clearWorldCacheStale` on the shared cache (nothing stale) and `clearWorldCache` with `-PworldCacheDirectory` on a scratch directory, which it emptied while the shared cache kept its files. **Guards** in `WorldDiskCacheTest`, seven new: two checkouts of one code in two JVMs (the second a child process on the test classpath) generating a world once while a third with other code stores and evicts; a reader holding a world open through another code's eviction reading every byte, the world going at the next eviction; a stranded code's standard world going before a variant this code used since; a hit marking the world used; the startup prune and `clearWorldCacheStale`'s, on a stale directory, an empty one, one read two days ago and one holding only a fresh lock; the directory's resolution, and in situ the build's. Against T1's behavior, restored in place (its sweep, its eviction order, no prune, the directory under the checkout), four fail: the two checkouts (the lock file gone), the eviction order, the prune (the stale directory kept and the generating one removed) and the directory; the reader's passes, T1's reader having read from an open handle already, and the hit's, T1 having touched after the read. **Measured** on this 16-thread machine: `WorldDiskCacheTest` 16 tests, none failed. `:worldgen:jvmTest` with the shared cache empty 244 tests in 6m 00s (18m 46s in classes), leaving 16 worlds and 1.00 GB in the cache (5 of 512 by 256 cells at 20.7 MB, 11 of 1,024 by 512 at 81.7 MB); again at once 3m 59s (11m 38s in classes), 42 worlds read from the cache and none generated. Both runs failed only `GroundFiguresTest`'s scan on `Shoreline.kt:179`, a scan of source this chunk did not touch, so it fails on main as well (TODO). |
